Ether at a vault's address cannot be taken out. `launch()` does not lengthen the wait of a version already published. A token that fixes Permit2's allowance at infinity cannot be sold through a pull-2 router. A vault that follows copies the active version as it is, a removed asset or today's cash token included.

## 4. Data model

**New tables** (`packages/db/src/basket-schema.ts`, migration `0006`; `0012` adds the picked chain and a step's cash; `0016` adds a vault's name; `0017` a step's withdrawals; `0018` adds the snapshot worker's two tables; `0020` adds a vault's number). They cache chain state and hold off-chain text. The chains are the source of truth. No table of his changes.

| Table | Key columns |
|---|---|
| `chains` | text id (`solana`, `base`, `robinhood`) with its family, network and EVM chain id; every `chain_id` column is text with a foreign key here. `seedChains(db, configs)` writes the rows from the chain configs at API start and after `pnpm db:migrate`, and refuses a database whose rows name another network: one database per network |
| `users`, `user_wallets` | `privy_id`; `chain_id`, the person's current chain, where new plans are made (gate `CHAIN-SWITCH`), and `chain_picked_at`, the time of the last pick or switch, null when an outside wallet named the chain; `(family, address)` unique, `kind`; written only from a verified identity token. A `users` row is written at the pick, the first time an outside wallet names the chain, or by the goal join, with no chain, for a person who has neither ("The goal join", below); nothing writes `user_wallets` yet |
| `consents` | order id, address, kind, text version, a hash of the legs it covers, time |
| `basket_assets` | id `chain:slug`; `(chain_id, address)` unique; class, underlying, issuer, tier, price source, session, `max_weight_bps`, `blocked_countries` |
| `index_families` | `family_id`, slug, a folded-name key (unique), name, copy, `creator_user_id`, `creator_kind`, `kind` (`index`, `single`), `platform` badge, `params jsonb` |
| `recipes`, `recipe_versions` | family, chain, onchain id; version, components, `meta_hash`, `effective_at`, status, nullable `fee_bps` |
| `baskets`, `proposals` | user, kind (`personal`, `follow`), family; the stored `BasketProposal` keyed by `inputs_hash`, and `from_link` for a plan made from a link (migration 0013, gate `AGENT-LINK`), read by a partial index on `created_at` (0015). A `baskets` row is a person's plan in one vault: the API writes it when the vault is joined to its plan (the goal join, below), with `created_at` the time of the order that opened the vault |
| `vaults` | `(chain_id, address)` unique; owner, plan, recipe, accepted version, auto-follow, targets, balances, `value_usd`, `vault_type`, `observed_at`. Written first when the step that opens the vault is confirmed (`value_usd` null: nobody has valued it yet), then overwritten by every portfolio read. `basket_id`, the plan, is set once by the goal join and never touched by the cache |
| `follows` | user, family: the watchlist and pending prompts |
| `orders`, `legs`, `leg_attempts` | the `Order`, `Leg` and `Attempt` fields; nullable `org_id` and `basket_id` (a buy's vault number, 0015) on orders; a leg's `cash_raw` and its `withdrawals` (0017, gate `WITHDRAW`); attempts carry `message_hash`, `nonce` (the nonce of record once a transaction was reported), his provenance columns, nullable `fee_amount`, unique `(chain_id, tx_id)` |
| `keeper_runs`, `keeper_legs`, `keeper_vaults` | one open run per chain (a partial unique index, which works through any connection pooler); the keeper's own leg log; `synced_version` per vault. The two vault tables name a vault by `(chain_id, vault_address)`, not by a row of `vaults`: the keeper finds vaults on the chain and may not write the API's cache |
| `vault_numbers` | a vault's number among its person's vaults, which a vault with no name is called by (migration `0020`, gate `VAULT-NUMBER`): `(user_id, chain_id, address)` is the key and `(user_id, number)` is unique, with `number` above zero. A row is written once, with the next free number of the person, when the server first holds the vault as theirs (`apps/api/src/portfolio/numbers.ts`), and is never updated or deleted but with its user. It names the vault by chain and address and does not hang off `vaults`, whose row is rewritten on every read |
| `vault_snapshots` | one read of one vault at one time, kept whole and never overwritten (migration `0018`): `(chain_id, address, observed_at)` unique, and an index on `(owner, observed_at)` for a person's history. `block_or_slot` (the chain's height at the start of the pass, null where it could not be read), owner, `basket_id` (the plan the vault was joined to at that read, or null), the plan's number, the followed portfolio's id and the accepted version, auto-follow, `value_usd` cut to cents, the cash, the positions as `view()` gave them (amount, value, weight, target, drift), a pending version, `loss_used_bps`, the chain's `band_bps`, `loss_cap_bps` and `paused` at the read (null where the chain has none: the mock has only a band), `prices` (every price the view stood on, each with its own `source`, `fetchedAt`, `method` and `provenance`), and the row's own `provenance`, `source` and `method`. It names a vault by `(chain_id, address)`, as the keeper's tables do. Only `apps/snapshot` writes it |
| `snapshot_runs` | one pass of the snapshot worker over one chain: `started_at`, `finished_at`, `vaults_read`, `vaults_failed`, `error` (why the pass as a whole failed, with no node address in it; `left open by a pass that never closed it; closed at a later start or pass` for a run that a start or a later pass closed), `provenance`. One open run per chain, by the same partial unique index as `keeper_runs` and whatever the label. The index stops two passes that open at the same moment. A worker that starts closes every open run on its chains, a live worker's included, since a row does not say who opened it. A pass that finds a run that started longer ago than any pass can take (two intervals, and 30 minutes at least; 30 minutes with `--once`) closes it with that reason and goes on; a younger one is left, and the pass is skipped. So: run one worker a database |
| `price_observations` | asset, value, his `source`, `method`, `fetched_at`, `provenance` |
| `idempotency_keys` | as named |

**One pool for the API (API-INTAKE-OPS, Oct 7).** The API process reads through one client (`sharedDb` in `packages/db`), so it holds at most `DB_POOL_MAX` connections. Before, the legacy routes (`read`, `plans`, `risk`, `transactions`, `monitor`) and `/v1` each made their own, six pools in one process, which a session-mode pooler's cap of 15 clients for the project could not hold (`EMAXCONNSESSION` on the hosted API). The seed at start and `pnpm db:migrate` each open one connection and end it. A failed query is logged by its name and code alone on every route (`loggable` in `plugins/loggable.ts`): the driver's error repeats the statement's values, which name the person.

Three Postgres roles, not yet made: migration `0006` creates the tables only. Roles are cluster-wide, need `CREATEROLE` and carry no password in a migration, so they are created at the deploy, before the hosted database is used. `api` can read the keeper tables and not write them. `keeper` writes only the keeper tables and its attempts. `collector` writes only the risk tables. The snapshot worker will need a fourth: it writes `vault_snapshots`, `snapshot_runs` and the `chains` rows `seedChains` keeps, and reads `vaults`, `orders`, `legs` and `user_wallets`. Supabase's Data API is switched off, because it exposes `public` tables to anonymous callers by default (security review, checked against Supabase's docs).

**Private conversation storage (Oct 8 reconciliation; source pending independent review/CI).** Migration `0019_private_conversation_history` follows the unchanged generated snapshot0018 and adds `vault_conversations`, `plan_threads`, `plan_turns` and nullable `proposals.thread_id`. No database has been migrated by this work. Combined-source deployment requires0019 first because ordinary proposal queries also name the column; vault503 fallback alone is not deployment safety for the whole API.

`GET`/`PUT /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}/conversation` resolve fresh authenticated ownership through the same chain reader as the stateless model reply. The row and its advisory lock include the person's id, chain, normalized address, authoritative active network and provenance. GET/PUT return that network; the client must namespace local state and reject missing/mismatched network responses. PUT requires expectedNetwork matching the freshly proved active network before writing: a precondition, never a storage selector. A delayed old-network request returns409 NETWORK_CONFLICT without a DB write. Unknown and other-owned vaults give one private404, and a transferred owner never receives the previous account's words. Transactions serialize empty-row and existing-row writes; expectedRevision mismatch returns409 with the current revision, with no automatic overwrite retry. Driver failures become a fixed unavailable503 without SQL, private parameters or causes. The model UI saves only bounded plain transcript with checkpoint:null. Existing legacy checkpoints remain compatible data, not a confirmed sheet, sourced proposal or financial authority. Reopening display history never restores a financial approval, creates an order or applies targets; fresh replies still use current owner/catalog/risk validation and unchanged quota.

Guided `GET`/`POST /v1/baskets/{id}/thread` records are separate: every distinct candidate of one build shares one owner-only thread, one initial attachment and idempotent build events naming actual proposal IDs. An authorized prior build is resolved once; sibling alternatives never become rebuild ancestry. Private row ownership/from-link exclusions, sheet-verified facts and closed dictionary reply keys stay enforced. Attachment now accepts200 chronological person messages within22000 aggregate person characters, with a512KiB personalize body limit; pages remain50rows and turnPOST remains in the existing parse quota. Append and attach share a thread lock. Missing history schema/query returns retryable503, while financial plan/order work retains fail-soft record handling. Unsupported current guided rendered summaries/progress are excluded from this closed format and full-fidelity guided replay is not claimed. Model display text and model allocation confirmations are never converted into guided facts. A stored sheet still omits PersonalSheet limits/mix under the existing contract; changes to those cannot be inferred from this history. Deleting a proposal cascades its turns, leaving sibling-written events; no deletion route or new retention policy is implemented.

**The goal join, on the server (PORT-1).** A vault is joined to the plan it was opened for: `vaults.basket_id` names a `baskets` row, and that row names the stored plan (`proposals`) or, for a vault opened to follow a shared portfolio, the family. The API makes the join when a buy's `create_vault` leg is confirmed, on the call that sees it, while the order is in its time: the order's read, the report, the build, the cancel and `continue` each make it on every path on which the call settles that leg, also when the route then refuses (`apps/api/src/orders/plan-join.ts`). A build or a cancel can settle the leg and still refuse: by tracking a step that was sent (and then a price has moved past a later step's minimum, or nothing of the step is left to cancel), or by finding the landing of a transaction nobody reported (409 `STEP_LANDED`). The join is made on that same call. To make it the API reads the vault from the chain, writes its `vaults` row if the cache has none, and in one transaction locks that row, makes the person's `users` row where there is none, writes one `baskets` row and sets `basket_id`. The row lock is the guard against two joins at once; there is no unique index. A join that could not be made then (the chain did not answer, or did not show the vault yet) never fails the order's answer and is not tried again once the order is past its time: `GET /v1/portfolio` makes it for any of the person's vaults that has none, from the newest buy of that owner on that chain with a confirmed `create_vault` leg and the vault's number. A buy that adds money to a vault opens none and joins nothing. The join is kept in the database and changes no answer: it is what puts a vault into `vaults` from the moment it is opened, where the snapshot worker finds it, and what each snapshot row carries as `basket_id`. `GET /v1/portfolio` says a vault's plan as API-ADD-MONEY has it (`planId`, the stored plan's id, joined in the request by chain and the vault's number through `listPersonPlans`), and `GET /v1/me/plans` lists the plans by person (API-PLANS). The three read the same orders and need different things of them: the portfolio's `planId` needs only a buy of the person's with the vault's number, while the join needs a confirmed `create_vault` leg. So a step that landed and was never reported leaves a vault with a `planId` and no join. No read tracks a step that was never reported, and the portfolio's catch-up needs the confirmed leg too: the leg stays `built`, and the vault unjoined, until a call settles the step. While the order is in its time, that call is its report, or a build or a cancel of that same step, which finds the landing and answers 409 `STEP_LANDED`; whichever it is makes the join. Past its time (a landing found late usually is), a report or a cancel still settles the step and the portfolio's next read makes the join, and a build answers 410 `ORDER_EXPIRED` and settles nothing. The plan read through the join (`plansOf`, answering `VaultPlan` of `packages/schemas`: `personal` or `follow`, when the order was placed, the stored plan's or the family's id, and for a stored plan its sheet, its card, its verdict and its `observations`, the four together or not at all) is for the portfolio's own routes, from PORT-2 on. Whose goal it answers follows the rule a plan is read back by (`loadReadablePlan`, API-PLANS): the plan was made from a link, or its row names the same person as the `baskets` row; a vault bought from a plan that names another person, or nobody, is answered that it is held and no goal. `plansOf` answers a goal only where the whole stored plan still reads (it has to parse as a `BasketProposal`; `loadReadablePlan` refuses to everybody one that does not), and a holder is answered the goal of a plan made from a link whatever the agent-surface flag says: `AGENT_SURFACE` closes the read by id to anybody, not a holder's own vault. The card's figures travel with the plan's `observations`, each reading with its own `source`, `fetchedAt`, `method` and `provenance`. A join once made stands: it is made for the first sign-in holding the owner's wallet that the server sees (an order names wallets, not a person: `orders.user_id` is not written), and it keeps the time of the first order also where a chain started again and the vault was opened a second time at the same address (the mock, a test network deployed again). The newest order is taken only where no join exists.

Units: weights are integer basis points; token amounts are raw units in `numeric(78,0)`; dollars are display values only.

**Onchain.** Solana: the accounts in 3.7. A vault is about 1.06 KB (0.0060 SOL rent) plus a token account per asset (about 0.0015 SOL); a 12-asset vault costs about 0.027 SOL, refundable. EVM: a proxy per vault holds owner, portfolio id, accepted version, auto-follow, operator, targets, last keeper time per asset and the loss counter. The factory holds asset config, the router allowlist, keeper, guardian, closed days and parameters.

**Reading state, with no events.** EVM: `vaultsOf`, `vaultAt`, `snapshot()`, `indexAt` and `active()`, sent together as JSON-RPC batches that name one block (as built: `packages/chain-evm` makes no Multicall3 call). Solana: `getProgramAccounts` with `memcmp` at offset 8 for the portfolio, and at offsets 40 and 76 for the keeper.

## 5. Vault rules per chain

**Money comes in as cash only** (decided on Oct 2). A deposit is the chain's dollar token and nothing else; the same action then buys each asset in the plan's proportions, as trades the app builds and the owner signs once. The vault refuses a deposit of any other token. A token sent to the vault's address from outside cannot be stopped; it counts for nothing and the owner can withdraw it.

**The owner can always** deposit cash, swap with their own signature and slippage through an allowed router, set targets, switch auto-follow, and withdraw every token in kind to their own wallet. Withdrawal is per token and calls no router, feed, factory or registry. Only a program or beacon upgrade can block it. Owner trades are allowed at any hour; outside the session the app shows a warning.

**The keeper can call one function,** and the vault checks each call. Starting values are not yet calibrated. What a keeper leg's preview states follows its bytes: on EVM `keeperSwap` carries `minOut`, so `preview.minimums` has that one entry and the send is held to it; on Solana `keeper_leg` carries an amount in and no minimum, the program works one out from the reference price (check 4), and the list is empty. On Solana a keeper leg has cash on exactly one side: an asset is never traded for another asset.

| # | Check | Solana | EVM | Parameter, start |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Caller and switch | Signer is `vault.keeper` or Config's default; auto-follow on | Caller is the factory's keeper (no operator of a vault's own is built); auto-follow on | none |
| 2 | Recipe assets only | One mint is the cash mint and the other is in `positions`, with the keeper's switch on in the asset list | One token is the cash token and the other a target of the vault, bought only while listed, with the keeper's switch on in its `AssetConfig` | none |
| 3 | Balance change | Own input and output accounts read before and after; no third vault token account; no delegate, close authority or size change after | Exact approval, zeroed after, Permit2 included; own balance deltas; no other accepted asset fell | none |
| 4 | Value, per trade | `value(received) ≥ value(spent) × (1 − tolerance)` from the asset's Scope entry; cash counts as $1 | Same from the asset's Chainlink feed; cash counts as $1 (EVM-3: the 0.5% peg check first written here is not built) | 75 bps Solana, 125 bps EVM |
| 5 | Toward target | Sell only if over its target, buy only if under. The trade may end anywhere inside the band, on either side of the target, and not outside the band on the far side; it also ends no further from the target than it began, and at most half as far on the other side if it crosses it. Vault value = the cash account at $1 + the traded asset's account × price + Σ `tracked × price` of the other positions | Same, with every target's balance read from the token | band 50 bps |
| 6 | Cooldown | Per non-cash asset | Same | 3,600 s |
| 7 | Weekly loss cap | Lost value added to a counter that decays linearly over 7 days. `loss_accum` is in raw units of the cash mint; what is left of it `now` is `loss_accum × (7 days − (now − loss_ts)) / 7 days`. A leg that loses is refused when that plus its own loss is over the cap, as a share of what the vault was worth before the leg; a leg that loses nothing is not held to it and does not touch the counter. A leg that loses starts the seven days again for all that is left | Same | 100 bps of vault value on Solana: any seven days stay under 2% |
| 8 | Fresh price | Scope entry inside the asset's price range, and at most 120 s old; its 1-hour average at most 3,600 s old; the two within `twap_dev_bps` of each other. For every position the vault holds something of, not only the one traded | The same from the asset's `feed` and `averageFeed`: `answer > 0` inside the range, both `updatedAt` within `maxAge`, the two within `priceDevBps`; for every target the vault holds something of. Base also checks the sequencer feed. What the average is on each network: "The average on each network" below (`POOL-AVERAGE`) | 120 s and 200 bps (placeholder); 26 h for EVM stocks |
| 9 | Market open | Clock inside the session; not a closed day; past `closed_until` | Same, from `block.timestamp`; plus the pause probe and `haltUntil` | Mon to Fri, 14:30 to 20:00 UTC |
| 10 | Multiplier window | Not within 24 h of a change of the mint's multiplier, before or after: the mint's next multiplier differs from the one it has, and its effective time is less than a day away either way. Nor a mint whose issuer gave it a transfer hook program after it was listed (`HookNotAllowed`) | Robinhood Chain: not within 24 h of the token's own `effectiveAt()` **[C 13]**. Base: the guardian sets `haltUntil` from the issuer's schedule | 24 h |
| 11 | Pause | `Config.keeper_paused` | `keeperPaused()` | guardian pauses, admin unpauses |

The session window sits inside the New York session in summer and winter time, so there is no daylight-saving code. Closed days are loaded at deploy from his `fixtures/risk/us-market-holidays.json`, as far ahead as it runs, with half days counted as closed. A fresh-looking feed on a holiday then fails check 9.

**Check 5, exactly** (decided on Oct 2; the rule for SOL-3 and EVM-3, and what the mock's keeper leg and the contract's two band cases hold to, with the band from the mock's config, 50 bps unless set). A weight is an asset's value over everything the vault holds, cash included, with cash counted as $1. A sale needs the asset over its target before the trade, and a purchase needs it under: `NotTowardTarget` otherwise. After the trade the asset may sit anywhere inside the band, on either side of its target; it may not sit outside the band on the far side: `PastTarget`. So a fill a little better than the reference price does not revert. The asset also ends no further from its target than it began, and a leg that crosses the target ends at most half as far on the other side as it started (`PastTarget` too; found in review on Oct 4, tightened to half on Oct 4 in TNET-4; on EVM since EVM-3): inside the band alone, a stolen key could carry an asset from one edge to the other and back each cooldown, every leg toward the target and each paying the tolerance, and with "no further" alone it could still flip an asset that had drifted to exactly as far on the other side. Half as far makes each crossing close the distance. The EVM keeper swap holds the same rule (EVM-3, `_noFurther`); the mock does not model it. A leg that spends nothing is refused (`NothingTraded`), so it cannot use up the asset's cooldown. `planRebalance` still sizes each trade to land on the target or just before it.

**What the cap does and does not bound.** Checks 4 and 7 are measured at the reference price. If the reference is wrong by X, a leg can lose X plus the tolerance while the counter records only the tolerance. Check 5 limits this to assets that are truly off target, and on Solana X is at most the width of the asset's price range. The app's trust notice says "plus any error in the price reference".

**The reference is the weak point** (found on Oct 2, `docs/risk/STATE-RISK.md`: Kamino's own price for two stock tokens read exactly 1.0000 on every refresh from 2025-07-29 to 2026-02-11, and one stock read about a hundredth of its price once in market hours). So on Solana the keeper trades an asset, and values a vault that holds it, only when the admin has switched the asset on in the asset list, after checking by hand that its entry moves, that its time refreshes in market hours, and that it is close to the pool price (`programs/README.md` has the steps). The age of the price, the age of its average and the distance between the two are checked on every leg. An entry that is wrong, steady and followed by its own average passes all three. What bounds it is the asset's price range (decided on Oct 4, after review): a floor and a ceiling the admin writes with the switch, the ceiling at most twice the floor, checked on every leg for the traded asset and for every position valued. Outside the range the leg is refused; no range means no price passes, and the switch cannot go on without one. The admin sets it as a band around a price checked against the pool and moves it by hand, never automatically. Inside the range a wrong entry is still not caught: the switch stands in front of that, and the guardian's pause behind it.

**The average on each network** (gate `POOL-AVERAGE`, proposed on Oct 9; decided in principle by Thom, parameters still to be set from a dry run, open to Rodrigo). The vault reads one contract in the asset's `averageFeed` slot and does not know which kind it is.

- *Solana.* The same source's own one-hour average: the Scope entry's TWAP, held to an hour.
- *Robinhood Chain test network (46630).* A second `TestPriceFeed` that our price writer fills with a copy of mainnet's. It is ours, so it proves the contract's arithmetic and nothing about the price.
- *Robinhood Chain mainnet (4663), proposed.* Chainlink publishes no average there, and a stock feed writes a round only on a move of 0.5% or once a day, so its age says nothing about whether it is right (read on Oct 9: SPY 19 hours between rounds on a normal day). The average is `contracts/src/price/PoolAverageFeed.sol`, one per asset, with no owner, no setter and no storage: the asset's time-weighted average price over the last hour in its Uniswap v3 pool against USDG, in the Chainlink feed's decimals and behind `latestRoundData`. Check 8's distance then reads "Chainlink within `priceDevBps` of where the token traded over the hour": a second source, which Solana does not have. Nobody of ours writes a price.
  - It gives no answer, and the vault reads that as `AssetNotPriced`, when the pool's liquidity in range is under a floor now or as the hour's average (`ThinPool`), when the pool has less than an hour of history (`ShortHistory`; it is deployed only over a pool that keeps more observations than the hour has seconds, so trading the pool every second cannot cause it), when Chainlink's own rounds of the last hour cannot be read or their time-weighted average is more than a band from the latest (`FeedRounds`, `FeedJumped`: a feed that has just stepped waits), when Chainlink gives no price (`FeedDown`), when the pool's tokens or anyone's decimals are not what they were at deployment (`PoolChanged`), or when the average is not a price it can state (`PriceOutOfRange`).
  - Its answer is stamped with the block's time: the average runs up to the block it is read in. So `maxAge` on this slot always passes, and what stands for an age is `ShortHistory`. `maxAge` on the feed itself stays 26 h and means only that Chainlink's network is alive.
  - The check is an AND. Moving the pool can stop the keeper; it cannot make it accept a price Chainlink does not state, and to pass a wrong Chainlink price the pool has to be held at that price for most of an hour. A push past the last of the pool's liquidity takes the hour's average liquidity to almost nothing, which is `ThinPool`.
  - Starting values, to be set again from a dry run before any asset is switched on: one hour, 150 bps for `priceDevBps` and for the band on Chainlink's own hour, at most 12 rounds read, a floor of half the pool's liquidity in range, session 15:00 to 19:45 UTC. The keeper's dry run on mainnet (`apps/keeper`, `--dry-run`) logs both prices per asset per round for that purpose and sends nothing.
  - Not covered: a sequencer stop (both freeze together; no uptime feed exists), and a feed and a pool both wrong and steady (the range). Gold stays owner-signed: its Chainlink feed is itself made from pools.
  - The tick-to-price maths is `contracts/src/price/TickPrice.sol`, written here under the repository's licence (Uniswap v3's own is GPL-2.0-or-later and is not used); it rounds down.

**The weekly cap is a leaking counter, not a sliding window.** A full counter drains over the same seven days, so the most a keeper can lose in any seven days is the cap plus what drained in that time: the cap itself if the losses come at once, and just under twice the cap if they are spread to follow the drain. I2 of section 13 is stated with that bound. What a person is told is "no more than 2% of the vault in any seven days" (decided on Oct 4), so the parameter starts at 100 bps, half of it; at its hard bound of 500 bps the same sum is 10%. A leg that loses starts the seven days again for everything left on the counter. That errs toward refusing: the counter drains more slowly than each loss would on its own date, so the figure a reader shows does not clear seven days after a loss while later legs go on losing. A strict bound of one cap in any seven days needs the losses kept per day (eight daily sums fit in the vault's reserved bytes); that is not built and not decided.

**New versions.** After `effectiveAt`, anyone may call adopt on an auto-follow vault if the version only changes weights among accepted assets. If it adds an asset, the call fails with `NewAssetNeedsOwner` and the owner taps accept. Accept and create both name the version the person reviewed and revert with `VersionMismatch` if the active version has moved on. Accept before `effectiveAt` reverts with `VersionNotEffective`. An accepted asset is one the vault has a target above zero on. An asset a version drops stays in the vault as a position with a target of zero while the vault holds any of it, so the keeper can sell it, and it is no longer an accepted asset: a later version that brings it back needs the owner. On Solana adopt is a keeper path and the guardian's pause stops it; the owner's accept is never stopped.

**Solana specifics.**

- `anchor_spl::token_interface` and `transfer_checked` everywhere, which covers classic SPL and Token-2022 (the newer token program with optional extensions).
- SPYx carries pausable, a freeze authority, a permanent delegate, a scaled UI amount, and a transfer-hook extension with no program set. So `tracked` is a hint that `sync_balances` repairs from the one derived token account; `upsert_asset` rejects a mint with a hook program; `withdraw` forwards extra accounts in case the issuer adds one.
- One swap leg per transaction. Mainnet's instruction stack limit is 5 levels, counting the transaction's own instruction as level 1 **[C 15]**; the spike's route reached level 4, and so did `route_v2` through `owner_swap` on Oct 3. The spare level is kept for a deeper route or a transfer hook, so a keeper leg is never wrapped in another program.
- Scope is parsed by hand (its crate is BUSL-licensed). Kamino can remap an index, so the keeper re-derives each asset's `(account, index)` before a run. That check is off-chain. The comparison of the mapping entry at the pinned index with `source_check` is not built (SOL-3): the field stays zero, and `keeper_leg` refuses an asset whose `source_check` is not zero rather than pass a check it does not make. The gap is listed in `programs/README.md` ("Not built yet"), and belongs in `docs/vault/SECURITY.md` when that file is written. The price and average positions of the ten stock tokens on mainnet are in `fixtures/solana-vault/scope-indexes.json`, taken from Kamino's own reserves.

**EVM specifics.** `deposit` takes an amount and no token: the vault reads the cash token from its config each time, so there is no way to name another. `withdrawAll` walks the tokens that came in by a vault function: the cash token and both sides of every swap; a token sent in from outside leaves through `withdraw(token, amount)`. Raw units everywhere; the feeds already include the multiplier. Decimals (18 on Robinhood Chain, 8 on Base) are passed in config, never read from the token.

The owner trades through any router on the config's list, which also says how each router takes its input: directly, or through Permit2. The vault never takes the router's word. It approves exactly `amountIn`, calls the router, takes the approval back and reads that nothing is left on the token or inside Permit2, then checks its own balances: at most `amountIn` spent, at least `minOut` received, no other token it tracks lower. A token the platform took off its list can be sold and withdrawn, not bought. A token, Permit2, the factory, the registry, the beacon and a vault are never routers: the config refuses them and the vault checks again for itself, Permit2 and the tokens it holds. A router that re-enters the vault mid-swap is refused by one guard over every state-changing function; `multicall` is outside the guard and each call inside it is under it.

Robinhood Chain swaps go through Universal Router 2.1.2 (`0x204FAca1764B154221e35c0d20aBb3c525710498`), pull mode 2, on hookless pools. Checked on a fork at block 77,417,307 with the real router and Permit2: 10 USDG bought 0.04328 NVDA, with nothing left approved; as transactions of their own, a first buy of a token costs about 334,000 gas, a later one 253,000, a sale 281,000, `createVaultAndBuy` with one swap 792,000, `createVault` 399,000, `deposit` 76,000 and `withdrawAll` of two tokens 137,000 (its gas limit must still leave 420,000 before each token). The chain is an Arbitrum Orbit chain, where `block.number` is an estimate of the L1 block: the contracts read `block.timestamp` only. Base uses our hardened `SlipstreamAdapter`, pull mode 1: it checks `pool.factory()`, sends output only to `msg.sender` and holds nothing. Robinhood Chain has no sequencer feed; that risk is accepted.

## 6. Shared-portfolio registry and creator limits

- Onchain and small: creator, family id, version, components, effective time, `metaHash`, creator counters. Names and copy stay in Postgres.
- A shared portfolio published on more than one chain is a family with one recipe per chain, tied together by `familyId`. A plan uses the recipe of its own chain, and a shared portfolio with no recipe on that chain is not offered to that person.
- A family row is created or renamed only after its publish transaction confirms and its creator matches the signed-in wallet. Names are ASCII in the MVP. Uniqueness is checked on a folded key (lower case, spaces and punctuation removed, look-alike digits mapped to letters), and the launch names are reserved.
- Cards, the approval page and the server-written summary show the creator address and a platform badge, set from the platform creator address. Shared portfolios rank by value following. The follower count includes only vaults holding $10 or more, since an empty vault costs almost nothing to make.
- Following is the vault pointing at a recipe.
- The 500 holds one asset, so it is not a registered shared portfolio. It sits on the shelf as a single-asset portfolio (`kind: 'single'`): a vault with one target and no recipe. The registry keeps one rule set and no admin exception.

Limits, checked by the registry. Constants, not per-portfolio settings. Shape limits and `maxWeightBps` apply from version 1. Version 1 takes effect at once and is exempt from the frequency and turnover rules, which apply from version 2. Decided on Oct 2: four simple rules. Dropped from the earlier design: a 10-point change per asset, a rolling 7-day turnover counter, and a rule for a ceiling that falls below a live weight. With one version per 48 hours at 20% each, an author moves at most about 70% of a portfolio in a week, which costs followers at most about 1.75% at the widest tolerance, inside the vault's 2% weekly cap.

| Limit | Value |
|---|---|
| Assets | 3 to 12, platform list only, never the chain's cash token, each 2% to 50%, in 50 bps steps |
| Turnover | 20% of the portfolio per version, compared without dividing: the absolute weight changes against the version in effect add up to at most 4,000 bps (twice 2,000). A version that waited and has taken effect is the one in effect |
| Frequency | One version per publish delay (48 hours after `launch()`); none while one is pending |
| Delay | `publishDelay`, 48 hours after `launch()`; computed by the registry, checked by the vault. One delay, not two: it is both the notice a follower gets and the least time between two versions. `Limits` has no `minInterval` |
| Cancel | The creator or the guardian can cancel a pending version. A cancel does not give the slot back, and the cancelled version's number is not used again |

**As built on EVM (EVM-2).** `IndexRegistry` checks the limits in `create` and `publish` and reverts with `CreatorLimit(reason)`, where `reason` is the number of the lowest rule broken, 1 to 14, as `fixtures/creator-limits/README.md` numbers them. A list must come sorted by token address: one that is not is `NotSorted`, which is not one of the fourteen; an asset listed twice is then two equal neighbours, rule 6. Rule 9 is `min(5000, the asset's maxWeightBps)`, read from the factory when the version is published; the factory itself refuses a ceiling above 5,000. Rule 14 is the factory's `cashToken()`, which on EVM is always a listed asset, so the refusal is `CashNotAllowed` and never `AssetNotListed`. An asset the admin took off the list is rule 5.

`previewPublish(id, next)` answers `(err, reason, turnoverBps, effectiveAt, allowedAt)` with no transaction: `err` is zero, or the selector the call would revert with; for an id that does not exist yet it answers for `create`. It takes no fee and no flags, so rules 1 and 2 are only seen by `create` itself. `allowedAt` is the shared one of 3.6: set only when waiting is all it takes, that is when the version is refused for being too soon and breaks no later rule, and then it is the end of the wait; zero otherwise, a version that is waiting included.

The cancel is the creator's, the guardian's or the admin's, since the admin may make every guardian call. A version whose time has come is in effect and cannot be cancelled. A version that is waiting when the delay changes keeps the time it was given, `launch()` included: before launch the team lets waiting versions take effect or cancels them.

Open, and the same on every chain since it follows from the four rules: a portfolio can be left unable to publish. An asset taken off the list cannot be in a new version (rule 5), so every removed asset leaves in the same version, and a version moves 20% at most (rule 13). When the removed assets weigh more than 20% together, no version passes: two at 15% each do it, though neither is above 20%. The same happens if an asset becomes the cash token, or its ceiling is set more than 20 points under its weight. The way out is the admin's: one of the assets is listed again, and the author steps down over two versions. Until a rule for it is decided, assets are not removed while a live portfolio holds them above 20% together.

A Foundry test drives the registry through all 87 cases of the shared file, building each case's state with real calls at the case's times, once against the factory and once against a list that holds the file's ceilings unchanged (10,000 included, so the registry's own cap of 5,000 is seen at work). It expects the file's turnover and effective time, or `CreatorLimit` with the file's `reasonId`, and the same from `previewPublish`. A second test checks the six cases of `meta-hash.json` against `sha256` in Solidity.

**The cap from measured exit capacity.** Each listed asset has `maxWeightBps` in the onchain asset list. The registry rejects a component above `min(5000, maxWeightBps)`. An ops script sets it from Rodrigo's measured curves, which are the one source for it (gate `EXIT-SOURCE`):

`maxWeightBps = 10_000 × shareOfDepth × exitCapacity(asset, τ) / indexCapacityUsd`, rounded down to a 50 bps step,

with `shareOfDepth` 0.25 and `τ` 1% (his values), and `indexCapacityUsd` $250k on Solana and $50k on the EVM chains (from `creator-limits.md`). An asset with no measured curve falls back to the ceiling of its shelf tier, which rests on price impact alone, and that cap is labelled as a fallback. A launch portfolio that holds an asset above its cap changes before it is published: on the measurements of Oct 1 to 2, selling $50,000 of the Tesla token costs about 0.6% against the 0.35% its tier assumed, which puts its cap near 6%, and two launch portfolios hold it at 14 to 15%. The first values are written in the deploy session on Oct 5.

The ceilings are written at deploy and do not move during the MVP, so no rule is built for a ceiling that falls below a live weight. The plain rule covers it: every weight of a new version is checked against the ceiling of the day, whether the weight changed or not.

`previewPublish` and `limits()` let an agent check before paying for a transaction. The TypeScript check, the Solana program and the EVM registry share one file of test vectors, `fixtures/creator-limits/vectors.json`, including a version published too soon, a weight above its ceiling and a non-zero `flags`. The README beside it defines each rule and names the reason a refusal carries.

**The registry on Solana, as built** (SOL-2; `programs/basket`, accounts in 3.7).

- A shared portfolio is one `Recipe` account at seeds `["recipe", creator, family_id]`; that address is its `onchainId`. It holds the version in effect (`current`) and at most one that waits (`pending`). History is in the `RecipePublished` events, which carry the components, the effective time, the turnover and the meta hash.
- `publish_recipe` writes version 1, in effect at once. `update_recipe` writes the next version, in effect `publish_delay_s` later. `cancel_pending` takes back a version that waits; the creator or the guardian signs. A vault takes a version by `create_vault`, by the owner's `accept_version`, or, for an auto-follow vault and a version with no new asset, by `adopt_version` (3.7, section 5).
- A version that waited and whose time has come is the one in effect with no transaction: `create_vault` reads it that way, and `update_recipe` moves it into `current` before it measures the new version against it. So the `current` field alone does not say which version is in effect; a reader compares `pending.effective_at` with the chain's clock.
- A version number is never used twice, on either chain (decided on Oct 3, after the SOL-2 review). The registry keeps the highest number it ever gave out (`Recipe.last_version`) and the next version takes the one after it, so a cancelled version keeps its number and the version that waits can be the one in effect plus two. Otherwise an author could publish version 2, cancel it, and publish other weights as version 2: a create or an accept that names the version a person reviewed would be consent to weights they never saw. An account written before the counter existed holds zero there, and numbers from the version in effect; `cancel_pending` writes the number it takes back into the counter, so such an account cannot hand that number out again either (SOL-3).
- The four limits are checked in the order of the vectors' reason numbers, and a refusal is always `CreatorLimit` (6020). Which rule it was goes in the transaction's log as `creator limit: reason=<number> <name>`, with the numbers of `fixtures/creator-limits/README.md`. All 87 cases pass against the program, each driven through real publishes in LiteSVM (`programs/tests/creator-limits.vectors.test.ts`).
- The ceilings are `max_weight_bps` in the asset list, written by `upsert_asset`. The cash mint may be on the list and is refused as a component by its own rule (`CashNotAllowed`, reason 14).
- There is no view call: to check before paying, simulate the publish and read the log. `last_publish_ts` is the time of the last publish, cancelled or not, so the next version is allowed at `last_publish_ts + publish_delay_s`.
- The delay a version waits is the one in force when it is published. A version published before `launch()` under a short delay keeps its short wait.

**The routes, as built (API-3, Oct 5; Robinhood Chain since AGT-4, Oct 6).** Publishing and following are orders on `POST /v1/orders`; three reads serve the shelf. The logic is in `apps/api/src/orders/shared.ts`, the tables in `orders/families.ts`, the reads in `routes/v1/shared.ts`, and the answers are named in `packages/schemas/src/shared-api.ts`.

- **A new portfolio's id is a rule, not a number the server hands out**: `familyIdOf(slug)`, the SHA-256 of `family:<slug>`, in `packages/basket` and repeated in `packages/sdk` (held together by `tests/meta-hash.test.ts`). So the publish form works out the family id it shows, and hands the guard that id and the text it shows, never the API's answer (AGT-4). An update names the family the creator already has. A publish may send `familyId`; one that is not the id the server works out is refused (409).
- **A publish** is an order of type `publish` with one `publish` step per chain and the consent `publish`. Its summary is the server's and holds no word of the creator's. Before anything is built it is held to: the creator is the signed-in person's wallet; the slug is lower-case letters, digits and dashes; the name is plain ASCII with no space at either end; name and description at most 280 characters each, with no link (a scheme, `www.`, a bare host such as `evil.xyz/airdrop`, or an email address) and no character that cannot be seen or turns text around (format and control characters); refused, not stripped: the text hashed is the text the form showed; another creator's slug is refused, and so is a name that folds to the key of another family (lower case, look-alike digits read as letters, nothing but letters, `rn` read as `m`, and `i`, `l` and `1` as one); and the four creator limits against the version the chain has now (`checkCreatorLimits` of `packages/basket`, with `CREATOR_LIMIT` and the rule's sentence), except the wait between versions, which the chain says when the step is built. A step is built as `buildPublishRecipe` with the hash of the order's own text: `publish_recipe` for a first version, `update_recipe` for the next.
- **The family is written only once a publish step has confirmed**, when the order is read or reported: the recipe as the chain has it, the version, and the text, only if one of the recipe's versions carries the hash of the order's text. Bytes that published other words under the family id leave the server's text as it was. A slug or a look-alike name taken meanwhile is logged and not written. Two creators can each publish a recipe under one new slug's id onchain: the one first recorded here keeps the family (not the first to land onchain), and writes of one family run one at a time under a lock on its id, so the family's text and its recipe are always one creator's.
- **A buy of a portfolio** (`buy` with `family`) is the follow that opens a vault, on one chain (gate `ONE-CHAIN`): the chain the request names (`chain`, with `family` only, gate `CHAIN-AT-THE-PLAN`), which a wallet of the person has to sign on (409 `NO_WALLET_FOR_CHAIN`), else the person's current chain; a portfolio with no recipe there is refused with 422. `GET /v1/funding` and `POST /v1/testnet/fund` take the same `chain` with the slug. A vault that follows the version in effect (`create_vault` with the recipe and that version, no targets, auto-follow off), then a swap per asset. Its number is `basketIdOf(familyId)` (the SDK's `basketIdOfPlan(familyId)`), so a second buy adds to the same vault, and is refused with `VERSION_CHANGED` while that vault follows an older version. The request is stored with the version in effect, or the one the person named; once another is in effect the order is refused when made and when its vault is built.
- **A follow** (`follow`) points a vault the person has on their chain at a portfolio: `accept_version` where the vault does not follow the version in effect (with the consent `new_asset`, which the guard asks of every accept), and `set_auto_follow` where the switch changes (on needs `auto_follow_on`; off goes before the accept, on after it). Nothing is traded. Auto-follow on, asked for or already on in the vault, is refused (422, with the fix "follow it with auto-follow off") when the portfolio does not offer it: at the order, when the switch is built, and when the accept is built if the vault has auto-follow on by then: a version in effect or waiting holds an asset whose `autoFollowEligible` is false, or the chain runs no keeper path (gate `GOLD-ONE-TAP`). Another person's vault answers 404, a vault address of the other chain family 422, a stale version `VERSION_CHANGED`.
- **A withdrawal** (`withdraw`, gate `WITHDRAW`, `orders/withdraw.ts`) takes tokens out of one vault of the signed-in person, in kind, to the vault's owner: `{ type: 'withdraw', vaults: [address], sellToCash: false, withdrawals?: [{ asset, amountRaw? }] }`. With no list it is everything the vault holds; a listed token leaves in full, or by `amountRaw`. `sellToCash: true` is refused (422, the sentence the screen shows), a second vault too. A vault whose owner is not a wallet of the person answers 404, as a vault that is not there. An amount over the balance, or a token the vault does not hold, is refused with 409 when the order is planned and again when a step is built, from the vault as it is then: nothing is built. One `withdraw` step per token on Solana and one for all on an EVM chain (everything there is the vault's own `withdrawAll`); each step carries `withdrawals: [{ asset, amountRaw, heldRaw }]`. A vault with auto-follow on, withdrawn from in part, gets the warning `AUTO_FOLLOW_ON`. The web's screen is `/vaults/[chain]/[address]/withdraw` (`WithdrawScreen.tsx`), reached from the portfolio's vault panel and, for the owner, from the vault's page; its terms (`terms.ts`, kind `withdraw`) are what the guard and `order-check.ts` hold the order to.
- **The reads**, keyless (`auth: 'public'`, the standard budget): `GET /v1/shelf?chain=` from the store only (`source: 'cache'`), with a recipe on a chain that is off left out and `chain` keeping the portfolios with a recipe there; `GET /v1/indexes/{slug}?chain=`, each recipe read from its chain for the answer (`source: 'chain'`), the store written on the way, and the stored versions where the chain does not answer; `GET /v1/indexes/{slug}/versions?chain=`, every version the server has seen, newest first. Since WEB-4, `GET /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}` reads any vault from its chain for the public vault page, and `GET /v1/funding` takes `family` in place of `proposalId`. Each recipe says whether the auto-follow switch is offered (`autoFollow`: `no_oracle` with the assets, or `switched_off`) and which version the family's text is the text of (`textMatches`). A version that waited and is gone is `cancelled` when nothing later took effect since, and `superseded` otherwise: with no events read, a version that took effect and one taken back before a later one are not told apart.
- **What a follower's screen holds a follow to** (the review of #56; built in WEB-4, section 11). The server's answer is read, never trusted for what is signed. The screen (1) reads the recipe account from its own node; (2) reads it at `recipeAddress(program, creator, familyIdOf(slug))` and refuses an account that is not the program's recipe of that creator and family; where it has a node and a deployment and the read fails anyway (no answer, another account, another creator, a creator that is no address of the chain), it offers nothing, as for a portfolio the chain does not hold, and says the portfolio could not be verified and may have been tampered with: only the mock, no node and no deployment fall back to the server's terms, marked not checked (the review of #62); and it works out `familyTextHash` of the name and description it shows, saying the text does not match when no version on the chain carries that hash; (3) shows the weights, the version and the creator from that read; (4) offers to sign a buy that follows only when every trade buys an asset of that version, in its weights; (5) hands the guard its own `{ recipeOnchainId, version }` and the vault number `basketIdOfPlan(familyIdOf(slug))`. Who owns a slug stays the server's word, so the screen shows the creator's address in full, and the platform badge only for a creator in a list committed to the app (`apps/web/features/shared/platform.ts`), never from the server's `platform`. A portfolio whose id is not its slug's is not offered for following until gate `FAMILY-ID` is settled.
- **Not built:** Robinhood Chain's half (a publish there answers 501 until EVM-3's registry calls and ADE-2's adapter; the shapes are per chain already); a cancel of the version that waits (the adapter builds it and the guard signs it, but no intent asks for it yet); the order behind the one-tap prompt (accept the new version and trade to it); value following on the shelf, which needs the vaults cache to carry the recipe it follows; the reserved launch names.

## 7. Personalization engine

Rodrigo's `solve()`, parser, registry and `ConstraintSheet` are not edited; they are tied to reais and eight Solana assets. A new module `packages/engine/src/personal/` sits beside them and reuses `isEligible`, `pickPrimaryYield`, `parseGoalRules`, `YieldObservation`, `LiquidityProvider` and `Language`. His baseline snapshot test stays green.

Who builds it: one of Thom's agents ports the working prototype (`docs/vault/research/design-v2/personalization-proto/`) into the module on Oct 2 and 3 with placeholder numbers, as new files only, so the three-profile test is green early. Rodrigo then reviews it and owns the parameter table and the template wording. The engine's `index.ts` exports the module (one line, added on Oct 3).

Three pure steps. **Exposure:** how big each sleeve is (stocks and crypto, dollar yield, gold) and what is inside it. **Placement:** which token carries each exposure. **Packaging:** one recipe in basis points, a reason on every line, the five-field card. A plan lives on one chain, the chain of the wallet the person signed in with (decided by Thom on Oct 3), so placement never chooses a chain.

| Input | Rule | Reason shown |
|---|---|---|
| Goal | What the plan may hold, by the asset registry: income, dollar yield and cash; protect, dollar yield, gold and cash; grow, everything | "Storm Cellar is left out: it holds SPY, and the asset list does not allow SPY in a plan for a goal to protect" |
| Goal, risk | A table gives the three sleeve sizes | "For a goal to grow at medium risk, the starting share of dollar yield is 15%" |
| Mix | When the person states what they want held (gate `EXPLICIT-MIX`, Rodrigo, Oct 6), `PersonalSheet.mix` (stocks and crypto, dollar yield, gold and cash, of the whole plan, adding up to 100%, with an optional credit share within dollar yield) replaces the table row in `sizeSleeves`. The limits follow it (`riskForMix`, flag `limits_from_mix:<risk>`): **the plan takes the lowest risk at which it holds the most in stocks and crypto that it holds at any risk.** It is found by making the plan itself at each risk and reading what each holds (`riskOfMix` in `compose.ts`, three plans at the most), to a cent for each line a plan may hold, which is the rounding of its parts; a tie goes to the lower risk. With the caps of the table, which rise with the risk, that is the lowest risk at which the plan holds as much as it would at the highest. Nothing is read from what a plan says kept money out, so whatever keeps stocks out at one risk and not at the next counts: the cap on one issuer, the cap on one stock, and what that cap moves to names with no room for it, by their ceiling, the number of lines or the least a line can be (the second review of Oct 6; the rule before it tried the mix alone at a size where no ceiling binds and went up only where a cap by risk was the cause named). So the risk depends on everything the plan is made from: the mix, the portfolios, the chain, the amount, the figures read (yields, exit capacity), and the withdrawals, the holdings, the date and the limits of the person. Whatever leaves the plan fewer stocks can lower it, as a date does. The mix on its own is the same sheet with no withdrawal, no holding counted, no date and no limit of the person's (`aloneOf` in `mix.ts`), under the same figures: it is what a read-back can state, by `riskForMix` on that sheet. Where the plan takes a higher risk than that, its stocks and crypto say so in one sentence (`MIX_LIMITS_RAISED`) and the plan carries the flag `limits_raised:<the mix's own risk>:<what raised it>`. What raised it is found by putting each thing back by itself (`withdrawals`, `holdings`, `cannotHold`, `limits`, `date`): the ones that take a higher risk than the mix alone are named, and all of them where none does by itself. A read-back asked with other figures than the plan is made with (no yields read) is not something the plan can know, and it says nothing of it. `MIX_LIMITS` names the share the limits are taken for: what the plan sets out to hold in stocks and crypto once what is set aside and the floors have had theirs. A stated credit share is the credit budget, filled first. Withdrawals keep their rule: what the next months owe is set aside first, in full. It counts first as the class of the mix it is held as (cash, or dollar yield where a rate leg holds it), then as the other of those two; only what the two together cannot give comes out of stocks and crypto, then gold, and each class that gave says how much and what that leaves it. The date, what may be needed and what must not be lost still apply; so do the caps per asset, per issuer and the exit ceiling, each said on the line, with the rest in the nearest holding allowed. Stocks and crypto, gold and dollar yield each take their share rounded down to the cent, and the odd cents of the split stay in cash, which has no cap; where the mix asks for no cash, the cash line says what they are (`MIX_ODD_CENTS`). A cap is whole cents rounded down, so a share that sits at a cap is under it or at it. A mix with stocks for income or protect is refused (`PROTECT-NO-STOCKS`), and a mix is not set with a split. The three candidates take the plan's limits, found once for the three, and say the same of them. Cover and Spread that would hold another share than Carry of a class the mix gives a share to are not shown, and the sentence names both shares; Carry is the plan made for the mix as it is | "You asked for all of it in stocks and crypto." "To hold 82% of the plan in stocks and crypto, the plan uses the limits for high risk: at most 35% in one stock or crypto asset, and 100% with one issuer." "On its own, this mix takes the limits for low risk. Because of what is set aside for your withdrawals, the plan uses the limits one step up, for medium risk: at lower limits it would hold less in stocks and crypto." "$1,800 of the 100% you asked for in stocks and crypto is set aside for your withdrawals from October 2026 to March 2027 instead, which leaves 82% of the plan for stocks and crypto: what your withdrawals need in those months is set aside before the mix is held." "$0.01 is left over once each share of your mix is written in whole cents, and stays in cash." |
| Sleeves, or goal and risk | The person may split the plan into sleeves (a goal with dates, a theme, the safest liquid yield), each with its share; otherwise a table by goal and risk gives the sleeve sizes. Whether a grown sleeve is brought back to its share is the person's choice (decided on Oct 5, gate `SLEEVES`). A goal sleeve and a safe-yield sleeve are built (ENG-3 slice 2), and a theme sleeve from a curated list (slice 4) | "50% AI theme, 50% dollar yield: your split" |
| Time frame | A floor on dollar yield that rises as the date nears; what dollar yield has no room for stays in cash | "At least 40% is kept out of stocks, crypto and gold, in dollar yield or cash: you need this money in 18 months" |
| Themes | Decide what is inside each sleeve. Which tokens count for a theme on each chain is curated by the team; a social side comes later (decided on Oct 5, gate `THEMES`) | "From Sand to Server" |
| Holdings | Target is set on amount plus holdings, then holdings are subtracted | "No NVDA: you already hold $4,000" |
| Risk | Cap per single stock or crypto asset, by risk. Cap per issuer, by risk: no issuer holds more of the plan than that, whatever the plan holds with it (stocks, crypto, gold, dollar yield or a leg in another currency; the plan's own cash is not counted). What is set aside, the dollar yield and the gold are placed first, and the stocks take what is left of their issuer's room; where an issuer's cap cuts a portfolio's stocks, each is cut in proportion to its weight. A portfolio held whole is booked before the rest, and gives way the same: where its issuer then holds more than the cap, the plan is made again with it held part by part. Money that stocks could not take is not held with their issuer past that cap: it goes to another issuer's dollar yield, or stays in cash (the second review of Oct 6; before it a plan on Robinhood Chain held 60% with the one issuer at low risk and said 50%). Dollar yield, gold and a leg in another currency also keep the plan's own cap: one issuer at most 50% of the plan, counting those only (gate `SOLVER-CAPS`, Rodrigo, Oct 5), and the sentence names them. | "No more than 70% of the plan with one issuer at medium risk: Robinhood is at that limit." "No more than 50% of the plan in dollar yield, gold and other currencies with one issuer: Jupiter Lend is at that limit." |
| Dollar yield | The banded fill (gate `SOLVER`): ranked by yield after haircut; yields within 0.5 points count as equal and share evenly, each token up to its cap (by symbol or leg type, lowered to its exit ceiling), its issuer's and the credit budget (by the person's credit tolerance, `limited` when unsaid). A token with no yield reading or no leg type is left out with the reason. What none can take stays in cash. The fill is exact while the caps nest (token, issuer, plan; credit inside the plan); where one issuer holds both a credit and a non-credit token both limits can bind across each other and the fill can leave money a linear program would place (`fill.test.ts` pins the case; the launch shelf has none today) | "No more than 25% of the plan in tokens that lend to borrowers or trade a spread" |
| Amount | Dollar ceiling per token = 0.25 × its measured exit capacity (gate `EXIT-SOURCE`). Where nothing is measured, the ceiling of its tier, said on the line and flagged. Overflow goes to dollar yield on the same chain, then to cash | "GLDx is limited to $10,000: beyond that, selling it would cost too much" |
| Country | No rule since Oct 6 (gate `COUNTRY-REMOVED`, Rodrigo): the engine reads no country and ignores `blockedCountries`, which stays on the asset as information. Who may hold an asset is to be enforced at sign-up and in the terms of service | (none) |

- A prototype over the launch shelf passes the handoff's test: three people, three plans, and each input alone moves the plan and adds a reason naming it. Its numbers are placeholders for Rodrigo.
- The engine offers three candidate plans from one goal, each a different setting of the same engine inside the person's limits; the person chooses, and none is pre-selected (decided on Oct 5, gate `THREE-PLANS`). The rules that keep them distinct are in `docs/vault/research/portfolio-method.md` section 2.4. Built in slice 3 of ENG-3.
- A plan holds at most 8 lines in the MVP (`maxLinesPerChain`: the shared type's name, and with one chain per plan it is the plan's limit). The vault itself allows 16. **Where the person's plan is two sets, each has the limit to itself (gate `LINES-PER-SET`, Rodrigo, Oct 7):** a sheet with a stated mix or a split of the money counts stocks and crypto as one set and the rest of the plan (dollar yield, gold, a leg in another currency) as the other, 8 lines each and 16 at most in all, which is the vault's own limit (`linesPerSet`, `linesInAll` in `params.ts`; `Book.linesFree`). A plan from the table by goal and risk, with no mix and no split, keeps the one limit of 8. Before, a shared portfolio of seven stocks left the other 80% of a plan one line, and what that one token could not take stayed in cash (55% of the plan in the case that raised it). The sentences that say a name is left out for want of a line say which limit it is (`MAX_LINES`, `OVERFLOW_MAX_LINES`, `NOT_WHOLE_PARTS`: "a plan holds at most 8 parts", or "stocks and crypto, and the rest of the plan, each hold at most 8 parts"). The packaging of a plan as vault targets allows as many lines as the plan may hold. Tests: `lines-per-set.test.ts`.
- **The card:** money needed today; expected return (a yield range on the dollar-yield share; stocks and gold assume no return, plus the dollar loss in a 20% fall); total term (none for a goal with no date, `BasketSheet.horizonOpen`, Oct 6); cash-flow pattern; when you can get out. Income goals add a verdict with the gap and each way to close it.
- **The model** fills `BasketSheetDraft` (every field nullable), asks the person about each field the text leaves open or unclear, and says back what it understood before the confirm step (gate `GUIDED-INTAKE`). It never sets weights, picks assets or states a figure: Claude Haiku 4.5 on Anthropic's Messages API with structured outputs, about $0.002 a call **[C 11]**. The call lives in `apps/api/src/llm.ts` with a 6-second timeout and a daily budget that counts calls, not goals: each turn of a conversation is a call, since a follow-up is read again with the text before it (`followUps`, Oct 6), so a goal settled in three turns costs three calls. A turn whose text was read before is answered from the cache and costs none.
- Checks after the model, in pure code: the amount and time frame must appear in the text; themes must be shelf slugs; any disagreement with the regex parser is flagged per field. The form is always the confirm step. Model down: the regex parser pre-fills it. That fails: it opens with defaults.
- Shared portfolio names never reach the model. Explanation text is one template per rule, in English and Portuguese. An evaluation set of goals guards the parser: 12 on Oct 3, 18 since ENG-3 slice 4.
- A test bans "recommend", "suitable" and "best for you" in templates; the disclaimer stays in its one constant (section 17). This is positioning only: a plan built from a person's circumstances can count as advice whatever the wording.

**As built (ENG-2, Oct 3).** New files only, in `packages/engine/src/personal/`. Outside the folder: one export line in the engine's `index.ts`, `@colosseum/basket` in the engine's `package.json`, and `fast-check` as a root dev dependency. Every number below is a starting value: Rodrigo sets them in `packages/engine/src/personal/params.ts`, where each carries a mark (`starting` or `set`) and where it came from. The wording is his too, in `templates.ts`.

| File | What it holds |
|---|---|
| `params.ts` | The one parameter table, `PERSONAL_PARAMS`, and `PERSONAL_PARAMS_STATUS` |
| `registry.ts` | A class's sleeve, and eligibility: each token is given the registry row the structurer's `isEligible` reads, and that function decides |
| `exposure.ts` | Sleeve sizes (`sizeSleeves`), the shared portfolios a plan starts from, holdings, the cap on one stock |
| `mix.ts` | Gate `EXPLICIT-MIX`: the risks in order, the lowest risk at which a plan holds the most (`lowestThatHoldsMost`), the mix on its own (`aloneOf`) and what a person adds to it that can raise the limits (`RAISERS`, `withOnly`). `riskOfMix` and `limitsOf` in `compose.ts` make the plan at each risk and settle the limits; `riskForMix` there is the export |
| `placement.ts` | Which token carries each exposure on the person's chain, within ceilings, issuer caps and the number of lines; dollar yield through the banded fill (`Book.fillBanded`, ENG-3 slice 1). `Book.issuerLimit` is the one place an issuer's room is read: the cap by risk over all it holds, and for dollar yield and gold the plan's own cap as well |
| `fill.ts` | `bandedFill`: the pure fill of gate `SOLVER`, in whole cents. Ranked by yield after haircut, ties by id; yields within the band share equally, each up to its room and its groups' room (issuer, credit) |
| `leg-types.ts` | The leg types of each dollar-yield token (rate, credit, basis, market deposit), with source and date; a local table until slice 2 puts them on the shelf asset |
| `set-aside.ts` | What the next withdrawals need, set aside before anything else, and the coverage check (ENG-3 slice 2) |
| `schedule.ts` | `scheduleOf`: the plan month by month in the goal's currency, each leg sold at its measured exit cost (ENG-3 slice 2). The old `schedule/` is not edited |
| `rebalance.ts` | `proposeSleeveRebalances`: rebalance proposals per sleeve for a stored plan and its vault (ENG-3 slice 4) |
| `packaging.ts`, `card.ts` | Lines, the one recipe (checked through `flattenReport`), the card (its exit figure from `rollUp`) |
| `compose.ts`, `world.ts` | The entry and its validation |
| `templates.ts` | One template per rule in English and Portuguese, and the sentences of the card and the verdict |
| `draft.ts` | `draftFromRules`: what the structurer's rules parser reads of a sentence, as a `BasketSheetDraft` |
| `fixtures/goals-eval.json` | The goal evaluation set (12 goals; 18 since ENG-3 slice 4) |

| Parameter | Starting value | From |
|---|---|---|
| `sleeves` (stocks and crypto / dollar yield / gold, the rest cash) | grow 60/30/10, 80/15/5, 95/5/0; protect 0/75/25 at every risk; income 0/100/0 at every risk | The prototype for grow. Income is new: the prototype gave it a stock sleeve. **Changed on Oct 3, for Rodrigo to read:** the protect rows were 20/55/25, 35/40/25 and 50/25/25, and their stock share went to dollar yield (`PROTECT-NO-STOCKS`), so risk moves a plan to protect only through the issuer cap |
| `glideFloor` (least dollar yield by months left) | 6: 80%, 12: 60%, 24: 40%, 36: 20%, 60: 10% | The prototype |

| `openEndedHorizonMonths` (the time frame of a goal with no date) | 120 months, with no glide; never said back | Gate `GLIDE-OPT-IN` (Rodrigo, Oct 6) |
| `capPerStockBps`, `capPerIssuerBps` | 10/20/35% by risk, for one stock or crypto asset; 50/70/100% by risk, for all the plan holds with one issuer | The prototype |
| `yieldBand`, `capPerAssetBps`, `issuerCapBps`, `creditShareBps`, `defaultCreditTolerance` | 0.5 points; syrupUSDC 40%, USDY 40%, else by leg type (market deposit 60%, rate, credit and basis 40%); 50% for dollar yield, gold and cash; none 0, limited 25%, accept 50%; limited | Gate `SOLVER-PARAMS` and Rodrigo's answers of Oct 5; marked `set` |
| `setAsideMonths`, `driftBandBps`, `switchDays` | 6, 5 points, 7 days | Gate `SOLVER-PARAMS`; read from slice 2 and 4 |
| `fxMaxAgeDays` | 3 days | New in slice 4 (rebalancing): an FX reading older than this does not count a withdrawal in another currency for the monthly refill. Marked `starting`, for Rodrigo |
| `shareOfDepth`, `tau` | 0.25, 1% | The structurer's values: the share of a measured exit capacity a line may hold, and the cost it is read at |
| `tierCeilingUsd` | A $50,000, B $10,000, C $1,500 | The prototype, from the sizes that define the shelf's tiers. A fallback since `EXIT-SOURCE`: read only where no capacity is measured |

None for protect since Oct 3: Storm Cellar, which it started from, holds stocks. Gold is a list since Oct 5 (gate `GOLD-PAXG`): the first the person can hold on the chain, so PAXG on Solana and GLD on Robinhood Chain |

`minExitSamples` left the table on Oct 3. It was compared with the sample count of a whole curve, so at 1 it never bit; what counts as measured is the risk layer's to say, per time of the week, and the engine reads its answer. One thing stays in the logic and is not a setting: a capacity that comes with no sample at all is not a measurement.

Two numbers are not in the table. The card's exit figures come from `rollUp` in `packages/basket`, which reads exit cost over its own window (`EXIT_WINDOW_DAYS`, 7 days; the engine reads capacity over the same one) and flags a short capacity at its own cost (`EXIT_TAU`, 1%). `rollUp` takes neither as an argument, so `tau` in the table moves the ceilings and not that flag. `params.test.ts` fails if the table's `tau` and basket's are set apart. The same test reads the folder's logic and wording for numbers of its own; it cannot see arithmetic on the numbers it allows.

What the code does where the text above left a choice:

- **Four sleeves.** Cash is the fourth. A row of the table may add up to less than 10,000; the rest is cash. The date raises a floor on dollar yield and one on cash, each filled from stocks and crypto first, then gold. A nearer date never gives less cash, nor less in cash and dollar yield together, whatever the table (a floor is the largest step the date has not passed). With `rules.glide` off the date sets neither floor. The glide is opt-in (gate `GLIDE-OPT-IN`, Oct 6): the intake turns it on only when the text asks to take less risk as time passes or names a date the money is needed by.
- **The person's limits.** `mustKeepUsd` is a floor on dollar yield plus cash. `mayNeedInMonths` sets the cash floor when it is sooner than the date. `cannotHold` removes a class, a ticker or a token; what is left of the sleeve takes its place, so the sleeve keeps its size.
- **Income plans hold dollar yield and cash.** Stock tokens are refused by the registry's own `isEligible`, as CLAUDE.md asks. Gold, commodities and crypto are given the same registry kind (`equity`: "stocks and gold pay no coupon"), so they stay out too. Risk still moves an income plan, through the issuer cap. An income plan that holds nothing in dollar yield has no cash flow, and its verdict is not met with the gap equal to the target.
- **Plans to protect hold dollar yield, gold and cash.** The same function refuses the rest: the registry row of a stock, a crypto asset or another commodity does not carry the profile a goal to protect is checked under. A table that gives a plan to protect a stock sleeve changes nothing: that share is held in dollar yield, and the plan says what was refused. With nothing chosen, a plan to protect is built from its sleeves: dollar yield by its yield, gold from the table's ticker.
- **What a sleeve starts from.** The shared portfolios the person chose, each in its version for the person's chain. One that holds a token the goal does not allow is left out whole. When none is chosen, or a sleeve is not filled by what was chosen, the table's portfolio for the goal if there is one and the chain has it, then the table's ticker (for gold, the first of its list the person can hold on the chain). The reason says which of the two cases it is.
- **Following.** A shared portfolio made only of stocks and crypto is held whole, as one `index` component, when every part fits: a token the person can hold and does not already hold, within its ceiling, the issuer cap, the cap on one stock and the lines left. The issuer cap counts what the plan holds with that issuer beside the portfolio (what is set aside, dollar yield, gold), which is placed after it: the plan is made again with the portfolio opened where that leaves the issuer over its cap. Otherwise it is opened into its parts, in the weights of the plan; the lines say they no longer follow it, and why it could not be held whole.
- **Holdings.** A holding under `holdingMinBps` of the amount is ignored. One that counts sets the target on the amount plus the holding and takes the holding off; every line it moved says so. A name it left with less that then gets no line (what is left is too small, or has no room) says it where the plan lists what was left out, beside why it is out: a holding never moves a plan with no word of it (Oct 7; before, the sentence went with the line, and a plan in which no name it moved had a line said nothing of the holding).
- **Placement order.** Shared portfolios held whole, then dollar yield (best yield after haircut first), then gold, then stocks and crypto. What no token takes goes to dollar yield, then to cash, and the line that takes it in says how much, meant for what, and what kept it out.
- **A cap keeps a portfolio's shape.** The units of a sleeve are placed together. Where an issuer has no room for all that is asked of its tokens, each unit gets its share in proportion, so no name is held whole beside one cut to a part. The order things are tried in is fixed: the largest share first as the sleeve gave it, before any holding or cap moved it, and ties by name. A unit whose token has no line takes one of the lines left, in that order; one left under the least a line can be is left out and the rest share again: first a unit whose own ask (its ceiling, the cap on one stock) is under that least, so it shares none of an issuer's room it cannot take, then the last in the order. So the order a shelf lists a portfolio's parts in decides nothing, and a small holding does not swap one name for another.
- **Lines and the recipe.** A line's share of the plan in whole basis points is its target, exactly, and a target is never more than its token's ceiling: where rounding would pass it, the basis point stays in cash and the cash line says so. A line's dollars are what placement gave it; they add up to the amount, and agree with the target to within two basis points (two more for each shared portfolio held whole that feeds the line).
- **Figures.** Every yield and liquidity figure that shaped the plan is in `observations` and counts for the provenance flags, whether or not its token is in the plan. `inputsHash` covers the sheet, the time, the shelf's content (not the order it is listed in), every holding and yield given, the provider's capacity and measured times of the week for each token of the chain, its cost for each line, and the parameter table.
- **The verdict.** Monthly income is the dollar-yield lines at their yield after haircut. Met means the plan pays the target or more; a gap is written up to the next cent. A way to close a gap is listed only if it does: the smallest larger amount that meets the target, found by running the engine again, and a lower target. When no amount can, the verdict says so in `noAmountCloses`, beside the ways and not among them.
- **The card's exit cost.** From `rollUp`, written up to the next whole basis point. A sale measured above the reference price comes as a cost under zero: the card shows zero and says how, and the plan carries the flag `exit_cost_below_zero`.
- **What a plan says is checked against the plan.** The tests hold each sentence to it: a floor a line names is held, an issuer called "at that limit" is at it, money said to be held in dollar yield or cash instead is no more than those two hold, a limit called a tier is one. With a mix, what a class holds less than asked has to add up: what it gave to what is set aside, what the floors took from it (worked out from the table apart from the engine, beside withdrawals as without them) and what its sentences say was kept out or moved. One thing is taken on the sentence being there and no more: what a holding moves, which no sentence states as an amount.
- **Not built here:** the model call and the checks after it (`apps/api`), a dollar-aware rules parser, and `/v1/baskets/parse`.

**As built (ENG-3 slice 2, Oct 5): currency, sleeves, withdrawals.**

- **The person's split.** A goal sleeve and a safe-yield sleeve, each a share of the plan. The goal sleeve's share scales the table's row and the date's floors (rounded up).
