The safe-yield sleeve is placed before the goal's dollar yield, by the banded fill over rate-only tokens; what none takes stays in cash and says so (on Solana today there is no rate-only token, so it is all cash, flagged `safe_yield_no_rate_leg`).
- **The goal's currency.** Dollars unless the sheet says. For another currency the matching leg is the chain's cash token counted in it (`BasketAsset.currency`), whatever the currency: no code knows reais. The plan carries `fx_open:<cur>`, and `no_matching_leg:<cur>` when the shelf lists none. Every line counted in another currency than the goal's says its value moves with the rate (`FX_OPEN`); a dollar goal has such a line only when it holds a token in another currency for a withdrawal in it. An FX reading (`context.fx`, pair `USD<cur>`, validated like a yield, the latest per pair) is needed only to convert a withdrawal or to write the schedule; a withdrawal in a currency with no reading is refused (`InvalidContext`), never guessed.
- **Setting aside.** Cash for the liquidity window is the cash floor above, from how soon the money may be needed. Then the withdrawals of this month and the next five (`setAsideMonths`), in dollars, come off the goal sleeve before the table is scaled, and are placed before anything else: in the matching leg for a withdrawal in its currency, in rate-only tokens for one in dollars, the most liquid first (by measured capacity in one window; the unmeasured last, by id), each up to its cap, its exit ceiling and its issuer; the rest in cash. The lines say the amount, the months and each withdrawal. What is set aside is its share of the amount rounded up to the cent, and the goal sleeve shares out what that leaves. Where the plan states a floor on cash, the cash still holds that floor's share of the whole amount, rounded down to the cent: the cent or two the rounding would leave it short come from the sleeves the floor was filled from, in the same order (stocks and crypto, gold, dollar yield). Withdrawals larger than the goal sleeve set all of it aside, flagged `set_aside_short` and said. A withdrawal before this month counts for nothing and is flagged `obligations_past`.
- **The coverage check (C6).** For each month of the window, what is owed to then must be paid by cash and the matching legs at par and by what the dollar-yield legs can sell by then after the cost of selling: one window's capacity at `tau` a month (the window is 7 days, so one a month is the cautious reading), and legs on one pool share the pool's figure (`PooledLiquidityProvider`). A leg with nothing measured sells up to its tier ceiling a window at `tau`, flagged `coverage_from_tier:<asset>`. Stocks, crypto and gold are not counted. Where a month falls short, the part of a leg that cannot be sold in time moves to cash, the largest first, then stocks, crypto and gold; the moved lines and the cash line say why (`coverage_moved`). A line it moves whole (what would be left is under the least a line can be) is no longer there to say so: its name is listed with what was left out, with the sentence (Oct 7; before, the name was nowhere on the plan). A shared portfolio held whole that this takes one line's money from is no longer in the weights it publishes: it is then held part by part, and its lines say so (Oct 6; before, the line's target stayed the published weight and its dollars did not). What still falls short is said and flagged `coverage_short`. The safe-yield sleeve's holdings are not moved.
- **The schedule (C7).** Present when the sheet has withdrawals to come and the goal's rate is known (else `schedule_no_fx:<cur>`). Month by month in the goal's currency, at one rate for the whole schedule (FX stresses come with the status, slice 3): dollar yield accrues at its yield after haircut; the month's withdrawals draw on the matching legs, dollar cash, the dollar-yield legs most liquid first, then the rest, each leg at most one window's capacity a month and at its measured exit cost (at `tau` where nothing is measured), never at par. A month not paid in full is flagged `schedule_unpaid` and counted in `shortfall`. Against the same draw with every cost at zero (the par draw), months paid fall or hold, never rise: `withdrawals.test.ts` holds it on random plans.
- **Hashed.** A provider's pools are in `inputsHash` where it names any; a plan with none hashes as before.
- **Through the API.** Plans are in US dollars for now (gate `USD-ONLY`, Thom, Oct 6): `POST /v1/baskets/personalize` and `propose` refuse a goal or a withdrawal in another currency with 422, code `CURRENCY_UNSUPPORTED`, "Plans are in US dollars for now", before anything is read, and the goal screen says so. The engine's handling of other currencies above stays for when this is lifted.

**As built (ENG-3 slice 3, part 1, Oct 6): the status.** `statusOf` in `status.ts`, from the schedule's own inputs, on every plan with withdrawals to come (`PersonalProposal.status`, an engine type). No odds and no projected return (section 2.5 of the research note):

- **Covered now:** months paid at the rates observed, with `observedOn`, the date of the latest yield reading the plan counts.
- **Covered under stress:** the same under each named stress that applies, each only where the plan holds what it moves: yields fall (`stress.carryFallBps`), a credit or basis leg gated (`creditGateMonths`: neither sold nor accruing), stocks, crypto and gold fall (`fallBps`), and for a goal not in dollars its currency up and down (`fxMoveBps` over `fxMoveMonths`, the matching leg and the withdrawals in that currency moving with it). A dollar goal has no FX stress (C19). The sizes are the old engine's, marked `starting` for Rodrigo.
- **Carry needed beside carry observed:** the least flat yearly rate on every dollar-yield line at which every month is paid, by bisection to a hundredth of a basis point (0 when no yield is needed, null when no rate up to 100% pays); the observed figure is the dollar-yield lines at their yield after haircut, over the whole plan.
- **Met:** every month paid in the base case and under every stress.

**As built (ENG-3 slice 3, part 2, Oct 5): the ways, the three candidates, the scorecard.**

- **Ways to close a gap** (`PlanStatus.ways`, C12). A status not met lists each way that gives a plan that is met, each found by running the engine again with one input changed and the rest fixed, as the plan was made (the same candidate): the smallest larger amount, in steps of `wayStepUsd` (bisection, the answer tried); and the largest share of each withdrawal, in steps of `wayScaleStepBps` (each amount scaled and rounded down to the cent). When no amount up to the sheet's largest does, the status says so in `noAmountCloses`, beside the ways, flagged `status_no_amount_closes`. A later start and a monthly contribution have no field on the sheet, so they are not tried. The runs that ask "is it met?" skip the carry-needed search.
- **Three candidates** (`candidates(sheet, shelf, context)` in `candidates.ts`, gate `THREE-PLANS`, C10). The same engine three times, each with the table moved toward its aim and never past the person's limits (`tableFor` in `world.ts`): **Cover** sets `candidates.cover.setAsideMonths` (12) of withdrawals aside, holds at most half of the person's credit limit in credit and basis legs (gate `COVER-CREDIT`; its own limit, under the person's, said as the plan's: `CREDIT_BUDGET_PLAN`, or `CREDIT_NONE_PLAN` where half rounds to nothing) and reads exit capacity at half the cost (`tau` 0.5%) and half the share of depth, while a sale with nothing measured is still counted at the person's `tau`, never cheaper (`unmeasuredCost`); **Spread** puts every dollar-yield token in one band, filled equally within its caps (`SHARED_EVENLY`), with one issuer at most 30% of dollar yield, gold and cash; **Carry** is the table as it is, and is the plan `compose` makes. The numbers are a `candidates` row in the table, marked `starting` for Rodrigo. Then, in the fixed order Cover, Spread, Carry: one closer than `distinctBps` (1,000) to one already shown is not shown; then one that another matches or betters on every line of the scorecard and betters on one, or one that is better than every other on no line, is not shown, the last in the order first, until none is. Each one not shown says why (`candidatesNotShown`). None is marked, selected or ranked; the answer has no such field. With the launch shelf, a plan to grow often shows two (Spread changes nothing a grower's largest issuer depends on) and a plan to protect shows three.
- **The scorecard** (`scorecard.ts`, C11), on each candidate's plan: months of withdrawals that cash and the matching legs pay at par, in order; months paid now and under each stress (a stress that does not apply to a plan, such as a credit gate with no credit leg, counts its months at the rates observed when candidates are compared); carry observed; measured exit cost at the person's size and the share measured, and concentration by issuer and class (both `rollUp`); credit and basis share; open FX (for each currency other than dollars, what is owed in it beyond its matching leg), present wherever something is owed in another currency than dollars, left out otherwise. Not on it: yield confidence and primary redemption, which need data the shelf does not carry.
- **Through the API.** `POST /v1/baskets/personalize` keeps `id`, `proposal` and `rollUp` as before and adds `candidates` (each stored in `proposals`, all in one transaction, so `POST /v1/orders` buys it by its `id`; Carry has the plan's inputs and so the plan's row and `id`) and `candidatesNotShown`. **Open, for Thom:** the top-level `proposal` is the table's plan, the same as Carry, and stays the answer the web shows until it reads `candidates`, even where Carry is not shown; whether to drop it then is his call (gate `THREE-PLANS`: none pre-selected). The web shows the one plan until it reads `candidates` (gate `THREE-PLANS` is met on screen only then).

**As built (ENG-3 slice 4, the theme sleeve).** Gates `SLEEVES`, `THEMES`, `THEME-AI-SOLANA`, `THEME-FIRST`.

- **The lists are data.** One file per theme per chain, `content/themes/<chain>/<slug>.json`: slug, name in English and Portuguese, chain, version, curator, status (`proposed` or `confirmed`) with the day it was set and the gate that records it, and each member by the token symbol the chain lists it under, with why it is on the list in both languages. `ThemeList` (an engine LOCAL TYPE, `theme-list.ts`) validates a file and `parseThemeList` refuses one that does not. Every list in the folder is confirmed. Solana's AI list was confirmed by Rodrigo on Oct 5 (gate `THEME-AI-SOLANA`): NVDAx, MSFTx, GOOGLx, METAx, AMZNx, TSLAx, AAPLx. The stock labels of Oct 6 are lists too, 13 on Solana and 17 on Robinhood Chain, and he confirmed them all on Oct 7, each with the members it was proposed with (gate `LABELS-CONFIRMED`; see "the stock labels and the matched theme" below). A new list is `proposed` until a person confirms it, and fills no sleeve until then. A slug that starts with `matched-` is never a list's.
- **How the engine gets them.** `compose` reads no file: the lists come in the context (`ComposeContext.themes`), as the yields and the measured exit do. The server reads the files of the person's chain in `apps/api/src/theme-lists.ts` and hands them in through `bearingPlanInputs`, outside the `/v1` route reach (it reads files). Only the lists the sheet's theme sleeves name are in `inputsHash`: a plan with no theme sleeve hashes exactly as before, and editing a list re-hashes no plan that does not use it.
- **Eligible names.** The list, with the token the chain lists under each symbol (not a dollar-yield or cash token; where two tokens share a symbol, the first by id the person can hold), that the person can hold (what they cannot hold, what the goal allows; no country since gate `COUNTRY-REMOVED`) and whose exit ceiling can take a line of the least size (`THEME_TOO_THIN` with its measured or tier ceiling). A plan to protect or for income holds no stock (`PROTECT-NO-STOCKS`): every name is left out with `NOT_FOR_GOAL`, the sleeve is held in dollar yield and cash, said (`OVERFLOW_NOT_FOR_GOAL`) and flagged `theme_empty:<slug>`; the sheet is not refused. A theme with no list on the chain, or a list only proposed, holds no name and says so (`THEME_NO_LIST`, `THEME_NOT_CONFIRMED`, flagged `theme_no_list:<slug>`, `theme_not_confirmed:<slug>`).
- **Themes first (gate `THEME-FIRST`).** The theme sleeves are placed before the goal sleeve's stocks and crypto, in the order of their slugs: where one issuer's cap cannot fit both, the theme the person asked for keeps its share and the goal's stocks shrink, within the issuer cap by risk; the goal's line says the issuer cap and that the theme came first (`ISSUER_CAP_THEME`, naming the theme), and the money it could not hold is said the same way on the dollar-yield or cash line (`OVERFLOW_ISSUER_THEME`). On Solana every stock token is Backed's, so at medium risk (70%) a 50/50 goal and AI split holds the AI $5,000 in full, the goal's gold $250 and SPYx $1,750. A shared portfolio the goal follows whole is placed earlier, so until the themes are placed their issuers keep room for them (`Book.reserved`): only as much as the names that can be held and sold can take (each up to its exit ceiling and the cap on one stock), and no more than the theme's share. That room counts as taken when the portfolio is asked to give way (`Book.followedOver`, once the set-aside, the dollar yield and the gold are placed and before the room is let go): where the issuer would then be over its cap, the plan is made again with the portfolio held part by part, as on a plan with no theme. The cap on one stock counts what a theme holds of it when the goal's stocks are placed. The goal's dollar yield, gold and set-aside are placed before the themes.
- **What the person holds.** The holdings rule above applies to a theme sleeve too, with `rules.useHoldings`, and a holding counts once in the plan: the themes first (in the order of their slugs), the goal sleeve what is left (`claimHoldings`). A theme fills its names to one level counting what is held of each, so a name and its holding come to the level of the others; the theme counts of a holding up to that level. A name held to the level takes nothing (`THEME_HELD_NONE`), one held in part takes less (`THEME_HELD`, with the amount the theme counts), and the others say they are larger for it (`MORE_BECAUSE_HELD`). Where a name held in part then gets no line (no line left, or what is left of it is too small), `THEME_HELD` is said where the plan lists it as left out, as the goal's names do (Oct 7). The goal's units then read what is left of the holding. A sentence always quotes the person's whole holding: where a theme counted part of it, it says both ("of the $3,000 of it you hold, $2,167 counts here": `ALREADY_HELD_PART`, `ALREADY_HELD_NONE_PART`, `MORE_BECAUSE_HELD_PART`, `OVERFLOW_HELD_PART`, and `THEME_HELD` in the theme); `claimHoldings` returns the goal's part and leaves the world's holdings whole. What is set aside for withdrawals is its share of the whole amount rounded up, so a small goal sleeve never sets aside a cent short.
- **Weights and lines.** Equal parts (but for holdings), each name up to its exit ceiling and the cap on one stock; where one stops, the others take what it leaves in equal parts. An issuer short of room scales its names down together; the room is read per name where every placement reads it (`Book.issuerLimit`): the cap by risk counts all the plan holds with the issuer, whatever the name is, and a name of gold or another commodity is held to the plan's own cap as well, the smaller room binding and its sentence being the one said (the issuer rule of the second review of Oct 6; before it a name of gold read the plan's cap alone). The themes take the lines left, less one kept for each goal stock that needs a new line; a kept line whose stock then takes no money (its issuer full, say) is given to the themes, by making the plan again keeping only the lines used. What no name of a theme takes is held in dollar yield, then cash, only after the goal's stocks have their lines, so it never takes a kept line. The themes' lines: a name already in the plan keeps its line, and when names outnumber the lines left, the easiest to sell take them, measured names first by measured capacity, then names on a tier by its ceiling, ties by id (`THEME_EASIEST`; the others `MAX_LINES`). A new line under the least size is dropped, the last in that order first. Each line says the theme and its share (`THEME_SLEEVE`) and why the name is on the list, with the list's version and curator (`THEME_MEMBER`), and the limit that holds it where one does.
- **What no name takes** is held in dollar yield, then cash, with the limit that kept it out on those lines; `split` records what each theme sleeve holds by token, those included. The goal's withdrawals do not sell a theme's holdings (the coverage check leaves them, as it leaves the safe-yield sleeve's), and what must not be lost cannot be more than the sleeves outside the themes hold (`PersonalSheet` refuses the sheet).
- **Checked.** `themes.test.ts` (C17 among them); `violations()` holds a theme sleeve to its dollars, its names to the list, equal parts unless a limit is said on the line, and each name's line to its theme sentences; the property tests generate theme sleeves (one with a list, one without) beside goal and safe-yield sleeves; the three candidates are run with a theme sleeve.

**As built (ENG-3, the stock labels and the matched theme).** Gates `THEMES`, `THEME-MATCHED`, `THEME-NONE-YET`. A person asks to invest by narrative ("semiconductors", "space", "defense", "obesity drugs"). Three things serve it, in this order: a curated label, a filter over sourced attributes, and an honest "nothing yet".

- **The labels.** A label is a theme list like the AI list, one file per label per chain: Big Tech, Semiconductors, AI infrastructure, Crypto economy, Fintech and brokers, Space, Quantum computing, Electric vehicles and self-driving, Cloud and software, Emerging markets and Asia, Commodities and real assets, Broad market, Retail favourites, Defense, Health care and Social media, and an AI list for Robinhood Chain with Solana's seven names. A chain where no tracked stock fits a label has no file for it (Solana has none for quantum computing, emerging markets or health care). Every one is confirmed: Rodrigo confirmed them on Oct 7, each with the members it was proposed with (gate `LABELS-CONFIRMED`, "Confirm all"). The membership is a person's call and only a confirmed list fills a sleeve, so `tests/stock-labels.test.ts` holds each list by name with the gate that confirmed it, its day and its members: a status or a name changed in a file alone fails. A member is a tracked token of its chain, which may be one the shelf does not list yet (GMEx, a thin token of Robinhood Chain, a stock of the cut): the sleeve then holds the names the shelf lists and says the others are left out, and a label none of whose names is listed holds nothing yet, confirmed or not. On the launch shelf that is Retail favourites on Solana, and Quantum computing and Health care on Robinhood Chain; and a label lists one name alone for Semiconductors, Space, Social media and Commodities on Solana, and for Space, Social media and Retail favourites on Robinhood Chain. A list of one listed name is still held, being a person's list: the name up to the most a plan holds in one stock at its risk (or with one issuer, for a gold fund), and the rest of the sleeve in dollar yield, then cash, each line saying so (`SINGLE_STOCK_CAP`, `OVERFLOW_STOCK_CAP`).
- **The attributes.** `content/stocks/<chain>.json` has one row per tracked stock: on Solana the 18 stocks of gate `UNIVERSE` and the launch shelf's stock tokens (19 rows), on Robinhood Chain the tokens of listed securities in the launch shelf's seed and the stocks of the 80% cut of RU.3 (42 rows). A row says why it is tracked (`sets`: `universe`, `cut`, `shelf` for a token the shelf lists, `thin` for one the seed measured too thin to list) and gives the token's symbol and ticker, the kind of security (common, ADR, preferred, fund), the company, where it is based, a sector, industry and sub-industry in the names of the GICS structure, 3 to 8 business-line keywords (never a brand or a product name, which would name one company), what a fund tracks, its sources and the day they were read, and under `unverified` the fields no source read supports, or that two sources read disagree on. What a token is comes from its own issuer on each chain (Backed's page on Solana, Robinhood's registry there); the facts of the security are the same on both. It records no figure. `StockAttributesFile` (an engine LOCAL TYPE, `stock-attributes.ts`) validates a file. The classification names are MSCI's and S&P's: whether the product may store and show them is open for the founders (section 17).
- **The filter** (`market-filter.ts`, the one contract between the intake and the theme sleeve) names one attribute and the value it must carry: `{ by: 'sector' | 'industry' | 'sub_industry' | 'keyword', value }`. Two writings of one value are held equal: case, accents, punctuation and spacing aside, and "&" or "and" dropped. No plural and no synonym is folded, and a value written in letters outside the Latin alphabet has no key and matches nothing. It travels in the sheet as the theme sleeve's slug, `matched-<by>-<key>`, where `<by>` is `sector`, `industry`, `subindustry` or `keyword` (`matched-industry-aerospace-defense`, `matched-keyword-glp-1`), so no shared type changes and a stored sheet says by itself what it was matched by. A slug that starts with `matched-` and names no filter is a theme with no list.
- **Who picks.** Pure code (`matchStocks` in `matched-theme.ts`): the rows whose attribute carries the value, in the order of their symbols, the same whatever order the rows come in. Three rules keep a match honest. A field a row marks `unverified` is never matched on, since the plan says the match is from sourced attributes. A fund and a preferred stock are found by keyword only: a fund has no sector, and a preferred stock carries its issuer's. A keyword selects nothing unless at least two tracked stocks of the chain carry it (`STOCK_KEYWORDS.carriers`): a keyword one stock alone carries is that stock's name by another word, and whoever named it would have picked the stock. The model, or the intake's fixed word lists, name only the filter. A model is shown at most the values a filter can select by (`attributeVocabularyOf`), never a symbol or a company's name, and picks no asset (`GUIDED-INTAKE`).
- **How the sleeve holds it.** The attributes come in the context (`ComposeContext.stocks`), read by the server in `apps/api/src/stock-attributes.ts` and handed in through `bearingPlanInputs`, as the lists are. `themeListOf(slug)` gives the curated list of a slug, or for a slug that names a filter the stocks it matches. A matched list has no curator and no status, and fills the sleeve when the shelf lists one of its stocks; from there every rule of the theme sleeve applies unchanged: equal parts, the exit ceiling, the cap on one stock, the issuer cap, the least line, the lines left, holdings counted once, `THEME-FIRST`, and `PROTECT-NO-STOCKS` through the asset list. Its lines say it is matched, by what, and from which version of the attributes read on which day, and never the words of a curated list (`THEME_MATCHED_SLEEVE`, `THEME_MATCHED_MEMBER`: "AMZNx is matched by keyword: advertising (attributes version 1, read 2026-10-06), not from a curated theme"). A list the sheet's sleeves do not name changes nothing in a plan, whatever it names: only a list that fills a sleeve of the sheet takes lines. `split` carries `matched: { by, value }` on such a sleeve, and `inputsHash` pins what each filter matched, so a plan with no matched theme hashes as before.
- **Nothing yet (gate `THEME-NONE-YET`).** Where no stock the shelf lists carries the value, the sleeve holds no name, its money is held in dollar yield, then cash, and the plan says "There is no stock for {theme} on {chain} at the moment. We will be adding more soon." (`THEME_NO_MATCH`, `OVERFLOW_THEME_NO_MATCH`, flagged `theme_no_match:<slug>`). A chain with no attributes file (Base today) says something else, since nothing is known to match or not: "Nothing is held for {theme}: the stocks of {chain} have no sourced attributes to match by yet." (`THEME_NO_ATTRIBUTES`, flagged `theme_no_attributes:<slug>`).
- **Checked.** `matched-theme.test.ts`, `matched.test.ts`, `stock-attributes.test.ts`; `violations()` reads the slug by the contract and holds a matched sleeve's names to what its filter matches by its own matching code and its own number, the three rules above included; the property tests generate matched themes on each chain; `tests/stock-labels.test.ts` holds the content: every member of a label is a tracked token of its chain, the tracked tokens are the 18 of `UNIVERSE` by the rule on the frozen registry, the stocks of the cut on the frozen Robinhood discovery and the launch shelf, one security carries the same facts and one label the same name and reasons on every chain, and each confirmed list is the one a gate confirmed, with that gate's day, who confirmed and the members it confirmed.

**As built (ENG-3 slice 4, rebalancing; revised after the review of #73).** Gates `SLEEVES`, `SOLVER-PARAMS`, `ONE-CHAIN`, `EXIT-SOURCE`. `proposeSleeveRebalances(plan, context)` in `packages/engine/src/personal/rebalance.ts`: pure, the time an argument. It reads a stored plan (`sheet`, `lines`, `split`: a `BasketProposal` fits) and a context: `now`, the plan's vault as read (`VaultState`, on the plan's chain or refused), the sleeve book, prices, the chain's asset list, a `deposit` or `withdrawal` about to move (raw units of the vault's cash), `paidThrough` (the last month whose withdrawal is paid), dated yield readings and the provenance they must have (`readingsFrom`, live unless said), FX readings, Bearing's `LiquidityProvider` (as `compose` takes it) and the risk layer's `LiquidityAssessment`. Every input is validated with zod; what it cannot use is `PersonalInputError` `InvalidContext`. The output is proposals the person taps, in the shapes the order layer uses (`RebalancePlan` and `Trade`, sales first, every trade through the vault's cash), each with its kind, its sleeve (or the whole plan), the drift before, the targets after, its reasons and `bookAfter`. Nothing is sent, and the keeper does not rebalance on drift in the MVP.

- **The sleeve book** (LOCAL TYPE `SleeveBook`). A vault holds tokens, not sleeves; which sleeve owns what is the book: per sleeve, the raw units of each token, cash included, adding up to the vault token by token, in bigint. Each sleeve plans on its own rows only, so no value crosses between sleeves unless the person chose to restore the split. The book is an input. Left out: with one sleeve it is the whole vault; with more, it is the plan's proportions only on the caller's word that the vault has not traded since it was bought as this plan (`boughtAsPlanned: true`), refused when the vault shows otherwise (a keeper trade on any position, or a version accepted by a vault that follows none; it cannot see an owner-signed trade, a deposit, a withdrawal or a transfer), and only while it is within `driftBandBps` of the plan, flagged `book_from_plan`; with no book and no such word the call is refused and asks for the book (the re-review of #73: a vault that sold $400 of one sleeve's stock is still within the band, and reading it from the plan moved $100 to the other sleeve). A book holding more of a token than the vault is refused; what the vault holds and no sleeve owns is reported in `unowned` and flagged, never assigned. Each proposal returns `bookAfter`, the book once its trades land and its sleeve's withdrawal has left, at the given prices and no cost; `settleBook(before, executions, vaultAfter, flows)` reconciles it trade by trade: each trade's real amount in leaves its sleeve's row and its real amount out enters it, so a cost or a fill that differs from the plan lands on the sleeve that traded even when several proposals are carried out in one batch; a breach's sales are shared by ownership, a restored split by its `bookAfter`; the deposit and the withdrawal go by `flows`; what no execution explains (an accrual, a rebase, a loss) is shared by the token's holders, a gain by what each held of it before, a loss by what each holds; only a token no sleeve holds or held is reported in `unowned`. The answer also carries `book` (as read, with the deposit) and `flows` (each sleeve's part of the deposit and of the withdrawal). Found by the review of #73: sharing every token out in the plan's proportions on each call handed value to the wrong sleeve after any trade that changed the mix.
- **The table** is the one the plan was made with: `tableFor(params, plan.candidate)`, as `compose` builds it (Cover's share of depth, `tau` and months set aside; Spread's issuer cap, and its one band, so Spread never switches). `BasketProposal.candidate` (shared, for Thom) carries the candidate; a plan whose `paramsHash` is not that table's is refused.
- **Drift inside a sleeve** is half the sum of absolute differences between what it holds and its targets in force (the stored ones, switched and refilled where they are), in basis points of the sleeve. At `driftBandBps` (5 points) or more, a rebalance is proposed that brings it back (`REBALANCE_DRIFT`); under it, nothing. The proposal reports the drift from the stored targets. Dust is `minLineUsd`; the cost a batch counts on is `tau`.
- **Deposits and withdrawals first.** New money and a withdrawal move only through the cash: new money buys what is under target, in proportion to how far under, and a withdrawal sells what is over, in proportion to how far over (`REBALANCE_DEPOSIT`, `REBALANCE_WITHDRAWAL`). Only if the sleeve is still at the band after that is the rest rebalanced. With `restoreSplit` off, the money is shared between the sleeves in proportion to what each holds, so the split stays as it is; with it on, new money goes to the sleeves under their share first and a withdrawal comes from those over it. No sleeve gives more than it holds with its part of the deposit (a capped share-out, exact in raw units).
- **The safe-yield switch** (`switchDays`, `yieldBand`). A token the sleeve holds moves to another rate-only token the person can hold on the chain (goal, what they cannot hold, a price; no country since gate `COUNTRY-REMOVED`) only when, on each of the last `switchDays` days, the other's latest reading of the day after haircut was ahead by more than `yieldBand` (in hundredths of a basis point, so exactly the band is not ahead). Only readings of the accepted provenance count: live, unless the caller says otherwise, and a switch decided on other readings carries `provenance: 'mock'` or `'sandbox'` and says MOCK or test network (`SWITCH_MOCK`, `SWITCH_SANDBOX`). Readings of one time are ordered by value, then source, so their order decides nothing. A day counts only with a reading of both; a missing day breaks the run. The last day is the day of `now`, or the day before when either of the two has no reading of that day yet. Of several that qualify, the highest on its last day, ties by id. The move stops at the smallest of the new token's cap per asset of the plan, its exit ceiling (gate `EXIT-SOURCE`: `shareOfDepth` of its measured capacity at `tau`, or its tier's ceiling where nothing is measured, flagged `ceiling_from_tier:<asset>`) and the room left with its issuer across the plan (`issuerCapBps`); what does not fit stays where it was and the proposal says which limit held it (`SAFE_YIELD_SWITCH_CAPPED`, `_EXIT`, `_TIER`, `_ISSUER`; `switch_capped:<asset>` when nothing fits). The proposal carries the readings it was decided on. A switch carried out is not proposed again.
- **The goal sleeve's set-aside**, refilled each month. What is owed in this month and the next (`setAsideMonths` in all), less what is paid (`paidThrough`), in dollars. A withdrawal in another currency is counted at its latest `USD<cur>` reading, which travels in the proposal (`fx`, with its source, time and method); with none, or one older than `fxMaxAgeDays`, nothing is counted and the answer is flagged (`refill_no_fx:<cur>`, `refill_fx_stale:<cur>`). The goal sleeve's targets hold at least that in cash and rate-only tokens, taken from its other targets in proportion. When the cash and rate legs it owns are short of it by `minLineUsd` or more, the shortfall is moved to cash from what is over (`SET_ASIDE_REFILL`).
- **Between sleeves**, nothing moves unless the person chose `restoreSplit` (gate `SLEEVES`). With it on, once the sleeves are `driftBandBps` away from the shares the person set (after the deposit or withdrawal), one proposal for the whole plan (`RESTORE_SPLIT`) brings every sleeve to its share and to its own targets, and its `bookAfter` gives each sleeve its targets; no sleeve proposes on its own. The answer always says the split drift (`splitDriftBps`).
- **A likely liquidity breach comes before everything**, as in the policy engine (`proposeRebalance`): when the assessment finds one and names orders, each sells its token to cash, no more than the sleeves own, each sleeve selling its part in proportion to what it owns and keeping the cash (`LIQUIDITY_BREACH`). It is the only proposal; the others are listed in `waiting`. The assessment is the caller's (`LiquidityProvider.assess`): the engine's folder does not import `packages/risk`.
- **Order and sameness.** Breach; else the split; else each sleeve (goal, safe yield, themes by slug), at most one proposal each: a switch, else the set-aside refill, else a deposit or withdrawal, else drift. A sleeve owning a token with no price proposes nothing and is flagged `sleeve_not_weighed:<sleeve>`. Same inputs give the same proposals, and so does any order of lines, sleeves, book rows, positions, prices, assets and readings.
- **Checked.** `rebalance.test.ts` (43; re-review: the book refused for the $400 repro without the caller's word, Cover's table holding the switch to half, Spread not switching, another table refused, two sleeves sharing cash settled in one batch at a 1% cost with each keeping its own cost, and the random suite at 0.3% a trade in one batch): the band; a deposit only buys and a withdrawal only sells; every switch rule above, each limit, readings that are not live; the monthly refill, with a recent and a stale FX reading; the review's two repros (the goal 70% and safe yield 30% with $500 a month settles after one round with no value crossing sleeves; after a switch the next call proposes nothing); the book derived only for a vault just bought, refused otherwise, more than the vault refused, an unowned token reported; `settleBook` with a 1% cost on the trades adds up exactly and leaves the other sleeve whole; every sleeve spends only its own cash; a property test on 40 random two-sleeve plans that share tokens, three rounds each, where each sleeve's value changes only by its own money and its trades are carried out with the book settled, and nothing is left to propose at the end; with `restoreSplit` on a deposit and a withdrawal together never take more than a sleeve holds; the breach first; reversed inputs; a plan from `compose` held as made.
- **What the route needs (not built):** the stored plan, with its `candidate` and the parameter table it was made with (by `paramsHash`); the plan's sleeve book, stored beside it, written when the vault is bought (from the plan, with `boughtAsPlanned`) and from then on only by `settleBook`, after every batch of trades with each trade's real fill, and after every deposit and withdrawal with the call's `flows` (the rule: never re-derive the book from the plan once the vault has traded); the vault that holds it (the `vaults` row whose `basket_id` points at the plan's `baskets` row) and the chain adapter's `getVault`, `listAssets` and `getPrices`; the dated live yield readings of the chain's rate-only tokens over `switchDays`; Bearing's `LiquidityProvider` and its `assess` over the vault's legs and the plan's withdrawals; FX readings for withdrawals not in dollars; and, once a switch is accepted, a stored plan with the new split. The keeper does not call it.

**The route (ENG-2, Oct 5).** `POST /v1/baskets/personalize` (`apps/api/src/routes/v1/baskets.ts`, the logic in `apps/api/src/orders/personalize.ts`) takes `{ sheet }`, a `PersonalSheet`: the shared `BasketSheet`, with the limits where the person set any, naming one chain. The route's zod schema validates it before anything is read, so the engine never runs on a sheet that did not validate (400), and `compose` validates it again. The person comes from the sign-in; the sheet names the chain their plans live on (`GET /v1/me`), another chain answers 422 and no chain yet 409. The shelf is that chain's: the assets its adapter lists and the shared portfolios with a recipe there, in effect. The figures come from `V1Deps.planInputs`; the server hands in `bearingPlanInputs` (`apps/api/src/plan-inputs.ts`), which reads Bearing's stored sell depth and the stored yields of each token by its own address. A test-network token that models a mainnet token (tjlUSDC models jlUSDC, tsyrupUSDC syrupUSDC) and has no reading of its own takes the stored live reading of that token, found by the token's `underlying` (on Solana the deploy record's `modelOf` without a trailing x) as the model's symbol, on the same family of chains (on EVM, any EVM chain: the assets table names the family), labelled `sandbox`, with a source that says whose reading it is and that it is applied to a test token, and the reading's own method and time (`apps/api/src/model-yields.ts`). No rate is made up: with no such reading the token is left out (NO_YIELD). Its exit figures are carried over the same way (`apps/api/src/model-exits.ts`), on a chain that runs as a test network only (the chain's own provenance `sandbox`, which the route passes in): the model's mainnet token is found on the token's own chain by the model's names in order, the xStock's first (SPY → SPYx, then SPY); on Solana the price index pins its mint (`fixtures/solana-vault/scope-indexes.json`), else Bearing's pool registry `risk_pools` gives the one with the most TVL; on an EVM chain the seeded `assets` rows of that chain (`robinhood:<sym>`), in any case, and its stored sell depth is read for the test token, with the provider labelled `sandbox` and its source naming each twin ("SPYx for tSPYx"). A test token whose model Bearing does not measure, or covers with curves still too thin to read a capacity from (`isMeasured`: the engine's own test, a capacity read from samples) (the dollar-yield stand-ins today: non-stock depth is PLAN-ANALYTICS item 17) takes its model's tier on the mainnet launch shelf (copied with its source and date into `fixtures/risk/launch-shelf-tiers.json`, tier A for jlUSDC and syrupUSDC; without the file it keeps C) in place of the test network's blanket C, and the plan says whose: a flag `tier_from_model:<asset>` and an observation `tier <asset>`, labelled `sandbox`, whose source reads "tier A of syrupUSDC on mainnet (…), applied to the test-network token tsyrupUSDC" (`withTiers` and `tiersSaid` in `orders/personalize.ts`). A token with neither (every token of the mock; a test token the shelf does not list) takes its own tier's ceiling, and the line and `flags` say so. Every test token is also counted under its model's issuer where the shelf names one (Backed for the xStocks, Jupiter Lend, Maple, Robinhood on Robinhood Chain; `issuerTwins`, the same fixture), in place of the test network's one name for every token, under which the cap on one issuer held all of a plan's dollar yield and gold to half together; the plan flags it, `issuer_from_model:<asset>:<model>`. The models the registry's feed does not read are read by `pnpm feeds:refresh-models` (`scripts/feeds/refresh-models.ts`): jlUSDC, realised over 30 days from its price path, under an `assets` row the seed writes (`scripts/seed-models.ts`, offered to no plan), and SGOV, realised over 30 days from its Chainlink feed on Robinhood Chain, stamped with the feed's newest round and refused where the feed is more than 4 days old, shorter than the window or in a distribution's fall (`scripts/feeds/model-rounds.ts`); a stand-in borrows a reading only on a chain that runs as a test network, from its model on that same chain; tSGOV has SGOV's leg type (`leg-types.ts`). SPY and QQQ are index funds on the devnet shelf as on Robinhood Chain's: the Solana record's `kind` is `etf` for tSPYx and tQQQx (off chain: the program keeps no kind), so one stock's cap does not bind them (gate `ENG-DEVNET-EXIT-TWIN`). The plan is answered in the shared `BasketProposal` shape: the limits leave the sheet, the sleeves and `noAmountCloses` are not in it, and a figure with no source or no time is left out of `observations` while its flag stays. Beside it the answer carries `rollUp` (`RiskRollUp` of `packages/basket`), from the same shelf, measurements and time: concentration by issuer, chain and class, and the exit figures, with the quoted exit null since a plan not bought has no stored quote. It is stored in `proposals` with the person's user row, and its `id` is what `POST /v1/orders` buys. Its rate class is `build`. `POST /v1/baskets/propose` (AGT-2, gate `AGENT-LINK`) makes the same plan with no sign-in, on the one chain the sheet names, stores it with no person and marked `from_link`, its candidates too (since the merge of ENG-3, each a row marked the same, so one call may store up to four rows of the day's allowance), and `GET /v1/baskets/{id}` reads only such a plan back; both answer only while `AGENT_SURFACE` is on. Not done: the person's holdings are not read (`rules.useHoldings` changes nothing yet), and no answer carries `GOAL_NOT_ACHIEVABLE`: `compose` always makes a plan, all cash at worst, and says why.

**The three-profile test since `PROTECT-NO-STOCKS` (Oct 3): one pair is under 3,000, and is Rodrigo's to settle in his table (ENG-3; the orchestrator's call on Oct 5, Thom may reopen it).** Ana (grow, Robinhood Chain) is 9,500 basis points from Bruno (protect) and from Carla (income), by sleeve as well as by token. Bruno and Carla, both on Solana, are 2,000 apart. A plan to protect now holds dollar yield and gold, and an income plan holds dollar yield, so on one chain the two differ by the gold share (25% in the table, 20% here: gold on Solana is measured to $10,000 a line) and by how the dollar yield is split. No starting row puts them 3,000 apart. The test pins the pair at what it is and says so; section 13 still asks 3,000 of every pair.

**The rules parser on the 12 goals (Oct 3).** `goals-eval.test.ts` runs the set against `parseGoalRules`, unchanged, through `draftFromRules`. It gets 0 of 12 wholly right, and the record is in the test. It never reads an amount (it reads reais only, and as the goal's target), a "protect" goal, a named portfolio, a chain or a country. It reads the language in 11, the goal in 8, the time frame in 5 and the risk in 4. It turns "no stocks" into high risk, reads "risco alto" and "risco baixo" as medium, and counts "for 5 years" only as the length of a monthly goal.

**The goal agent behind `/goal` (gate `RELAXED-INTAKE`, Rodrigo, Oct 8).** The relaxed intake reads the goal (`apps/api/src/orders/relaxed-goal-agent.ts`, from `scripts/relaxed/intake.ts`, `RELAXED-1`): one model call sees the chain's live catalog with each holding's cap, sourced yield readings and curated themes, talks to the person in its own words, says back what it understood (the person's own numbers included) and names holdings by id, with a shape (`pick`, `grow`, `income`, `protect`, `split`) and the facts it read (`stated`). Code then holds four rules: **(1)** only ids on the chain's catalog; an unknown id is dropped and said; **(2)** no stock tokens in a plan or pot to protect or to pay income (`eligibleForGoal`), dropped and said; **(3)** an equal split, or the shares the person gave as the server reads them in the person's own messages (`personShares`; what the model reports as a share sets nothing by itself); **(4)** every line measured against its cap: one above it is kept as asked and named in a warning on the plan (the deposit step warns with the measured figure and the person confirms; the contract refuses nothing for it). The sources are the server's and the projection (`relaxed-projection.ts`) is the server's arithmetic on sourced yield readings, past-rate and never a promise; the model types no figure, weight or source (a measured figure it names by reference is written by the server, as in the vault's conversation; see `FIGURES-BY-REFERENCE` below): a sentence of its that states a figure is cut by the check the vault conversation uses, and the projection starts only from figures the server finds in the person's words. The preview's one primary action is "Deposit" (`DEPOSIT-STEP`): it buys exactly the ids and shares the preview shows, through `goal/accept` with the goal and risk the server read in the person's words, then the buy screen, where the person signs; no engine picks other holdings (the vault contract accepts any composition, `ANY-COMPOSITION`). It answers `POST /v1/conversations/{chain}/goal/reply` whenever `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set; `GOAL_AGENT=model-led` opts back into the model-led conversation, and `RELAXED_MODEL` names the model (Sonnet 5.5 by default). Where it differs from what stands (the sheet is not confirmed field by field before the engine runs) is stated in the gate.

*Until Oct 8* the goal on `/goal` was read by the guided intake below, whose rule was two readers: a holding, a mix or a share was taken only where the model's reading and the text check (`intake-text.ts`) agreed. That rule is history for `/goal`; the guided intake and `POST /v1/baskets/intake` keep it for the API, the playground and agents.

**As built (ENG-3 slice 4, the guided intake, Oct 5; the narratives and the review of Oct 6; the second review of Oct 7).** Gate `GUIDED-INTAKE`. The pure part is in `packages/engine/src/personal/` (`intake.ts`, `intake-text.ts`, `readback.ts`, a block of its own at the end of `templates.ts`, and `market-filter.ts`, the one contract between the intake and the theme sleeve); the model call in `apps/api/src/llm.ts`; the route in `apps/api/src/routes/v1/intake.ts`.

**Sandbox shelf reconciliation (API-INTAKE-SANDBOX, Oct 7).** Intake and personalization share `preparePersonalInputs`: load the figures with the chain entry's provenance, apply the existing `withTiers` tier and issuer overlays, then version the prepared shelf. `bearingPlanInputs` translates sourced curated members and stock rows to the deployed symbols through `modelContent`, using the existing same-chain stand-in identity and ordered model names, only for sandbox tokens on a sandbox chain. Asset ids, addresses, symbols and provenance stay as deployed. The translation replaces known symbols and retains every sourced row, unlisted company, metadata field and keyword; it rejects ambiguous model ownership and symbol collisions. It adds no keyword carriers and leaves the engine's keyword, fund and unverified-field rules in charge. Both committed test-network records are checked with measured exits and without a provider; the intake risk and the constructed plan use the same prepared inputs. This changes no engine algorithm or API schema.

- **The model.** Claude Haiku 4.5 (`claude-haiku-4-5`) on Anthropic's Messages API with structured outputs: a JSON schema of the goal's fields, every one nullable, plus the portfolios the text names as written, the classes and the credit it rules out, the mix the person states (`mix`), the narratives they name, each by an id of the engine's own list (`markets`), one filter for a narrative the list has no id for (`marketFilter`: one attribute, its value, and the person's own words), and `unclear`, the fields it could not read. It is not shown the shelf: no name of an asset, a company, a ticker or a shared portfolio reaches it. Where a caller has them, the values the shelf's sourced attributes carry are sent with the goal (`IntakeVocabulary`: sectors, industries, sub-industries, keywords, and nothing else), so the model names a filter from that list; the same goal read with another vocabulary is another read. The route hands it the keywords of the stocks tracked on the person's chain and no sector, industry or sub-industry name (gate `MATCH-RULES`). Its instructions name every id of `markets` (`llm.test.ts` holds that), and tell it what the checks hold it to: what the person rules out, only asks about or holds elsewhere is no mix and no market, and a portfolio is only one the text writes. Temperature 0, a 6-second timeout and no retry, a daily budget of calls for everyone (`INTAKE_MODEL_DAILY_CALLS`, 1,000 by default, about $2 a day at the price above) and one per person on top of it (`INTAKE_MODEL_DAILY_CALLS_PER_PERSON`, 30 by default), and a cache by text (in memory, per process), so the same goal sent again is not read again and gives one sheet. A failed call is not cached: after a timeout or a spent budget, a later turn on the same text may get a model reply, and the draft can change then; `reader` says which read it each time. The key is `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, read from the environment at start. With no key, past either budget, on a timeout, a refusal or any error, the reply is null and the intake reads with the rules parser; the answer says which read it and why (`reader`). A reply replayed in a test is labelled `mock`.
- **The model's settings and what the log says (API-INTAKE-OPS, Oct 7).** The model, one call's time and the two budgets are set from the environment, each with the value above as its default: `INTAKE_MODEL`, `INTAKE_MODEL_TIMEOUT_MS` (500 to 60,000), `INTAKE_MODEL_DAILY_CALLS`, `INTAKE_MODEL_DAILY_CALLS_PER_PERSON` (`intakeSettings` in `apps/api/src/llm.ts`). A value that cannot be read stops the server at start with a message that names the variable and holds no value. Each time the rules parser reads in the model's place the API logs one line at warn, "the intake fell back to the rules parser", with `why` (`model_not_configured`, `model_timeout`, `model_not_authorized`, `model_error`, `model_budget_spent`, `model_person_budget_spent` and the rest), the model's id and the request's id, and never the text; the answer says the same to the screen in `reader.why`.
- **The vault conversation's settings (API-VAULT-AGENT-REL, Oct 8).** The vault and new-goal conversation (`apps/api/src/vault-agent-model.ts`) shares the intake's key and daily budgets but not its call settings: its model is `VAULT_AGENT_MODEL`, the intake's model when unset; one call may take `VAULT_AGENT_TIMEOUT_MS` (30,000 by default, 1,000 to 120,000) and write 16,000 tokens, since a proposal with its reasons runs past the intake's 6 s and 1,024 tokens and the Claude 5 family's thinking, which cannot be turned off, counts against the same budget; 16,000 keeps the call under the SDK's limit for a call that is not streamed. `temperature` is sent only to the older models known to take it (Claude 3, Haiku 4.5, Sonnet and Opus 4.5/4.6), and `output_config.effort` only to the models known to take it (the Claude 5 family, Opus 4.5 to 4.8, Sonnet 4.6), at `VAULT_AGENT_EFFORT` (`low` by default; `low`, `medium` or `high`); any other model id gets neither, and no `thinking` parameter is sent. When the reply fails one of the server's own checks (structure, a figure or an "applied" claim in prose, an unlisted or duplicate asset, a stock outside an income or protect goal that the person did not ask for, more than 16 assets besides cash, unknown evidence, a share the model reports without the person's words, picks that cannot meet a share the person stated), the model gets one repair call that holds its earlier reply and the failed check in plain words; the repair reserves from the same budget and runs only while the two calls together stay within the call time plus 15 s, with at least 5 s left. Validation is unchanged: a repaired reply passes the same checks or the person gets the failure, with one exception (API-PROSE-FIGURE-TRIM, Oct 8): when the repair attempt still states a figure in prose (a digit, a percent or currency sign, a written-out number with a unit, "guaranteed" or "risk-free", outside an exact catalog name or an attributed quote of the person), the server removes each sentence that states one and serves the rest, so no figure of the model's reaches the person. No cut is silent where a caveat may have gone with it: a message that lost a sentence ends with one fixed server sentence on a line of its own saying part of the reply was left out, and a list of tradeoffs or unknowns that lost an item or part of one holds that sentence once. A question left empty is dropped; an objective, a summary or a pick's reason left empty is one fixed server sentence saying that part was left out; a reply whose `message` has nothing left is still refused with `prose_figure`. "guaranteed" and "risk-free" count whether negated or not, since a negated form can still promise; a line break ends a sentence only after closing punctuation or before a list marker. Such a reply is logged at warn with `detail: prose_figure_trimmed` and `sentencesCut`, a count. For a broken stated limit the repair names the limit (its place in `allocationConstraints` and the person's quote, never the draft's weights), and if the repair fails too the person is asked about that limit, as before. Every 503 is logged at warn with `reason`, `detail` (the failed check's code) and `repair`; a reply that came after a repair is logged with `repair` too. No log line holds the person's words or the model's reply.
- **Any composition in the person's own vault (`ANY-COMPOSITION`, Oct 8).** The conversation does not apply a shared portfolio's ceiling (`maxWeightBps`) and no longer sends it to the model. Measured exit capacity as a share of the vault (`exitCapacityBps`) and a stock the person asked for in an income or protect goal (`requestedOutsideGoal`, read by the server from the person's messages clause by clause: the ticker as written in the catalog, the company name in any case unless it is an everyday word such as Meta, or stocks in general, after an asking verb in a clause with no refusal, exclusion, upper bound, sale, condition or question; the latest mention of a stock wins, so "No AAPL" withdraws an earlier "I want AAPL"; stocks only, other classes outside the goal stay refused) pass with a server-written entry in `warnings`: `over_exit_capacity` with the evidence id of the measured exit figure, or `outside_goal_requested` with the evidence id of the stock's catalog listing; the reply's `proposal.sources` carries both, and the reply schema refuses a warning without a proposal or on an asset or source the proposal lacks. The reader errs towards missing a request; the model is told to ask the person to confirm a stock they seem to want but have not plainly asked for. The screen writes the words. An exit share with no measured figure behind it is a server fault (`context_caps`).
- **Weights from the person's words, not the model's (`ANY-COMPOSITION`, Oct 8).** The model's reply names picks (`assetId`, `why`, `evidenceIds`) and no weight: its JSON schema has none, and a reply that sends one fails the structure check and gets the repair call. The server reads the person's shares itself (`personShares` in `apps/api/src/orders/vault-agent.ts`), from their messages and nothing else. These weights are a preview: money moves only after the person confirms explicit weights on a review screen, reached through the deposit step, where the weights can be edited by hand behind its own control (`DEPOSIT-STEP`), so the read is deterministic and never silent, not a full parser. A share is read from fixed patterns only, in one comma-separated piece of a sentence: a number with its asset straight after ("70% TSLA", "70% in TSLA", "70% em TSLA", "70% da carteira em TSLA"), an asset with its number straight after ("TSLA 70%", "TSLA at 70%"), or a ratio with its two assets in order ("70/30 TSLA and NVDA"). The number is "70%", "70 percent", "70 por cento", or "half"/"metade" as 5000, and a bound written before it sets the kind ("at least", "pelo menos", "at most", "no more than", "até"). The asset is a ticker (any case, except tickers that are everyday words such as META or PUMP, which count only as written), a company name, or a class word (stocks, cash, gold, crypto), which covers every listed asset of the class. Every number in the piece must pair the same way or none is read, and one number over two joined assets ("40% in TSLA and NVDA") sets nothing. The default is not to apply (fourth review of #189): unread and said back is always acceptable, applied or withdrawn wrongly is not. A sentence (or its side of "but", when that side has its own asking verb) sets shares only when every comma-separated piece of it is a plain ask: shares with nothing before them, matched start to end, but leads ("and", "ok", "like", "with", "please"), a first-person subject ("I want", "I'd like to", "quero", "gostaria de") and one asking verb ("put", "set", "make it", "give me", "allocate", "use", "add", "colocar", "pôr", "botar", "deixar", "alocar", "usar"); any other word there ("You put", "I'm scared to put", "My wife wants to put", "I want to reduce") makes it unread, nothing between them but "and", and nothing after them but "in this vault", "overall", "please" or where the rest should go. A piece that only asks ("I want EV stocks"), only says where the rest goes, or is a courtesy may stand beside them; any other piece ("70% TSLA is too risky", "Hypothetically, 70% in TSLA", "You gave me 70% TSLA", "I lost 30% in TSLA", "Increase TSLA 10%", "Cut TSLA to half") and none of the sentence is applied. The sentence is also checked whole: a question, a condition, someone else's view, a refusal ("don't want", "never", "não quero", "except", "sem") or a return, yield, price-move, fee or loss word ("a year", "earn", "upside", "dividend", "fell", "ao ano", "rentabilidade", "CDI") gives no share. A share is withdrawn only by these whole sentences: "forget/drop/remove [the] X [share/limit]" where X is the asset or class of a standing share, alone ("Forget TSLA", "Ignore that stock minimum"; with a number beside it, as in "Drop TSLA to 10%", the sentence withdraws nothing and is quoted as unread), "drop the minimum/maximum/limit" when exactly one such share stands, and "split it equally" / "equal split" / "partes iguais". A refusal that names an asset ("No TSLA please", "Remove NVDA and keep TSLA") withdraws nothing. "Make that at least 10%" gives a new number to the one share that stands. A later share replaces an earlier one only on exactly the same asset or class (a floor and a ceiling that fit both stand); a class share and an asset share stand together. The model still reports what it understood in `proposal.stated` (Rodrigo's field), and that sets nothing: a share there that the server did not read is named in one repair call, so the model asks the person, and is then ignored. The prompt's `allocationConstraints` is the server's own read, with the words it came from. `personWeights` (`packages/basket/src/weights.ts`) then sets `weightBps`: an equal split over the picks, the remainder basis point to the first picks (3 picks are 3334, 3333, 3333), unless shares apply; exact shares first, then minimums, then maximums, in a fixed order, so the result does not depend on the order the shares came in (a property test holds this); exact shares given for every pick are scaled to the whole. A pick the shares leave nothing for is dropped. Every step is said in the reply's `weightNotes`, server-written codes with the assets and the person's quote and no figure of the model's: `equal_split`, `stated`, `scaled`, `pick_dropped`, `share_unmet` (the picks cannot meet a share, and the person is asked about it, after one repair call) and `share_withdrawn` (a share the person's latest message withdrew, with its words) and `share_unread` (a piece of the person's latest message that reads as a share and set none, quoted: a share word such as "%", "percent", "half" or "mostly", or a bare number, a fraction, "all" or "double" beside a named asset; "the rest in gold" when the rest did not go only to gold; a bare return target such as "10% a year" is not one). A `stated` note carries the words the server read ("70% TSLA"), so the screen can show "from your words". The relaxed intake script (`scripts/relaxed/intake.ts`) calls the same split. Since Oct 9 the whole vault in one name is a share too, read only from a message that is that one request and nothing else (`oneRequest`: after its courtesies, one sentence with no question mark, no second sentence, no line break and no quotation, matched from its first word to its last): an optional "I want (to)" or "I'd like (to)", an optional put, place, invest or go, the whole ("all", "it all", "all of it", "everything", "all my money") and "in", "into" or "in on", or one of move, swap, switch, change, convert with the whole and "to", "into" or "in", then one listed name ("all in GOOGLx", "put everything in Google", "swap it all to tGOOGLx"). It is an exact 10,000. Such a message, and "100% X" as such a message, withdraws with `share_withdrawn` the earlier floors and exact shares on assets it does not cover; a ceiling and a share on an asset it covers stand. English only; "Quero tudo em TSLA" and "all in X" inside a longer message are still quoted as unread. Between an asset and its number a minus or a plus sign is never read ("TSLA -30%" is a price move).
- **The goal in the conversation (Oct 8).** The vault conversation reads the goal of the plan the vault was opened for on the server (`planSheetOf`, the read behind `POST /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}/targets`), never from the request, and sends it to the model as `currentGoals`, `eligibilityGoal` and `outsideGoal` (the listed assets `eligibleForGoal` refuses for that goal). After the model answers, a pick in `outsideGoal` is left out of the proposal unless it is a stock in `requestedOutsideGoal`: the reply carries the note `pick_outside_goal` with the assets left out, the model is asked once to write the reply without them, and if that fails the reply stands as the server corrected it (no proposal, with a server-written message, when nothing is left). A corrected reply carries no sentence of the model's that names an asset left out: those sentences are removed from every prose field (matched on the asset's id, its symbol in any case, its ticker and company name, and the word gold for gold), the summary is replaced, and the message ends with a server-written sentence saying which assets were left out: a stock because it is outside the goal and the person did not ask for it, any other class because a plan with the goal cannot hold it (a reply whose objective or whose reason for a kept pick is left empty fails instead). Both cuts can fall on one reply: the repair attempt first loses the sentences that state a figure, then those that name a pick left out, the message carries both server notes, and what is served is read whole for a figure once more. A stock counts as asked for only where an asking word (want, add, buy, include, keep, put, more, and the Portuguese ones) stands right before its name with only filler from the name to the end of that piece of the sentence ("I want NVDA removed" does not count), or in a bare list continuing an ask for a listed asset, stocks or a kind of asset ("I want bonds and NVDA", not "I want safety, NVDA"); a removal, a reduction, a swap, a comparison or a worry in the same piece of the sentence refuses it, and anything unclear does not count. Since Oct 9 a message that is one request and nothing else (`oneRequest`, above) asks with no asking word: the whole vault in one name ("all in GOOGLx"), "only GOOGLx" (with an optional "I want to" and hold, keep or have), and shares with no asking verb, for the shares `personShares` applied from that message ("100% Alphabet", "30% GOOGLx"; a ceiling or a zero asks for nothing). Nothing of this is read from a piece, a side of "but", a line or a sentence of a longer message, which is read by the asking-word rule alone. A name is the symbol, the ticker it models, the page's name for a test network's token (`shownName`: tGOOGLx is GOOGLx), the company, or a spoken name from a closed list (Google). A share on stocks outside the goal is withdrawn, and said, once the person's latest word on those stocks refuses them ("No stocks."). When the latest message is such a request for a stock outside the goal with a share and the reply drafts nothing for it, the model is asked once more (`asked_not_proposed`); if it still drafts nothing and the share is the whole vault, the server writes the draft in its own words (`askedDraft`), with the same weights, notes and warnings as any draft. The prompt's catalog rows carry `name`, the name the page shows, and the model is told to use it. A reply's message loses its last sentence only when it is surely the question sent in `question` (`withoutRepeatedQuestion`): it is the last sentence of the last line, that line is no list item, both end in a question mark, and one is the other word for word and in order with only filler words added; otherwise both are kept. A share the person stated on an asset the goal cannot hold gets `share_unmet` with a message that says the goal is why. So the conversation proposes nothing the targets review refuses for the goal. A vault with no plan of the person's on the server has no goal: nothing is checked, as in the targets review, and `proposal.unknowns` says the goal is unavailable. A stored plan that cannot be read fails the reply. The new-goal conversation has no stored goal either; the accept route checks the mix against the goal the person confirms there.
- **Prompt caching in the conversation (Oct 8).** The conversation's model call is ordered so that what every person on a chain shares comes first (`vaultAgentContent`): the system instructions, then one block with the catalog, the stock attributes and the listing's evidence (`catalog:`, `tier:`, `stock:`), sorted by id and with every object's keys in order, ending in a cache breakpoint. Bearing's analytics follow in a block with its own breakpoint only when they are at the reference size, which no vault's value enters. Prices, yields, measured exits, the vault, the goal and the messages come last, uncached. Nothing before a breakpoint holds a time, an address or a person. Each call's `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens` go to the server log with the model id and whether it was the repair call, and nothing else. The intake's call is unchanged: its fixed part is under the 4,096 tokens Haiku 4.5 needs to cache.
- **The conversation's analytics (API-AGENT-ANALYTICS, Oct 8).** Both conversation routes hand the model a few figures from each listed non-cash asset's Bearing fact sheet (`loadAssetFacts`), read by `apps/api/src/agent-analytics.ts`, which sits outside `/v1` and is injected like the plan inputs. The figures are the worst-regime exit cost and the exit cost in each other regime, the weekend ratio, the largest LP's share, the LP-exit cost, the capacity variation, the four-week volume, volatility and drawdown, one id per measure: `exit:<asset>:worst`, `exit:<asset>:<regime>`, `weekend:`, `lp:<asset>:top1`, `lpexit:`, `capvar:`, `volume:`, `vol:`, `drawdown:`. The capacity at the cost tolerance is the plan inputs' one: `liquidity:<asset>` at a vault's size, `capacity:<asset>` (size-free) for a new goal. A vault's figures are at its value rounded to two significant figures; a new goal's are at the reference size. Labels say the size, the regime, a larger measured size or a lower bound in words and hold no number, so the model cannot repeat one into its prose; the size and the cost tolerance go to the model as fields, and a size a figure was measured at goes in its method. A missing figure is a line in `analytics.unknowns` with its reason, never a zero. A test-network token reads its mainnet model's sheet (the twin `resolveExitTwins` finds, shared with the plan inputs), labelled sandbox with the model named; on a chain that is not live no figure says live. These figures explain; they limit nothing. Each sheet read is kept ten minutes and served once more while it is read again; one read per sheet however many turns ask, at most three at once and ninety waiting. A turn waits at most 2.5 s for reads it does not have; the reads carry on and fill the cache, and the assets still being read are named as unknown. A read with no answer after 60 s is given up, so it frees its place and a later turn reads again. An id names one figure: a second row for an asset, or a second figure under an id, is left out rather than failing the reply. The server reads every live chain's reference-size sheets from its start and every five minutes (`warm`, off in tests). A turn that went on without some or all figures logs at warn with a code (`analytics_timeout`, `analytics_failed`, `analytics_threw`, `analytics_partial_<n>_of_<m>`). The model reads the analytics as one block (`analytics.assets`: values by id, with a legend of what each id measures) and the other evidence as id, asset, label, value, unit and provenance; the server keeps source and method and writes them into the reply. Stock rows go without their source lists, which are evidence already. With every figure measured, the block for Solana's 28 listed assets is about 12,800 characters.
- **Figures by reference in the vault conversation (`FIGURES-BY-REFERENCE`, Oct 9; `apps/api/src/orders/vault-figures.ts`).** The model states a measured figure by writing `{{fact:<id>}}` where the number would stand, in any prose field (`message`, `question`, `objective`, `summary`, each tradeoff, unknown and pick reason); the id is one the server gave it in that request, an evidence id that carries a value. The system instruction says so. It is a constant, so the cached prefix is the same from one request to the next; but `VAULT_AGENT_SYSTEM` sits before both cache breakpoints, so each deploy that changes it (this rule did, and so did its follow-up) rewrites the cache once per chain on the first call after it. After the model answers, each reference is resolved against that request's evidence: a measured id becomes a figure (`value`, `unit`, `text` as shown, `source`, `fetchedAt`, `method`, `provenance`, `staleAgeSec` for a price past the age its chain accepts, `lowerBound` where the value is one); an id whose figure is not measured becomes its reason in words (`NULL_REASONS`) and no number; anything else (an id not given, an id with no value, a malformed token, any brace outside a reference, and a reference that does not stand alone, below) is an unbacked figure: the first attempt gets the one repair call (`figure_reference`), and the repair attempt loses the sentence with the existing note. `hasFinancialFigure` is unchanged and reads the model's words with the references taken out. What is served: the prose fields as plain text with each value written in, and, when a reply states any, `figures` with `prose` (the same fields with their placeholders) and `facts`. The ids a reference may name are the analytics' (above), `price:<asset>`, `liquidity:<asset>`, `yield:<asset>:<n>:quoted` and `:haircut`, and the vault's own state, built from the chain read and its prices with `view()`: `vault:value`, and per holding `holding:<asset>:amount`, `:value`, `:share` and `:target`; a value that needs a price the chain did not give is missing, not zero. These are the person's, so they travel after the cache breakpoints. A reference must stand alone, as the figure it is (the review of #216), by three rules. It is never inside quotation marks, since a quote is the person's words. It is bounded: before it the start, or a space after a word or plain punctuation, with at most an opening bracket; after it the end of the line, a stop that ends the sentence, or a space and then a word; so no sign, symbol, letter or digit touches it, two references have a word between them, and none stands beside a catalog name that holds digits. And its sentence, cut where the trimming cuts, holds no magnitude or percent word, no multiplier, fraction, sign or arithmetic in words, no number or currency word beside it, no rate or return word unless every reference in it is a `yield:` figure (a period word also with `vol:`), no rise or fall unless each is a `drawdown:`, no forecast, no promise word beside a yield, and no name of an asset other than the figure's (`assetNamer`; a class word names none). The figure check reads the model's words with a mark where each reference stood, one no person message or catalog name can hold, so the exemptions for a quote of the person and for a catalog name cannot be met through a reference; format characters and controls are taken out of the model's prose before anything is read or served. These lists refuse plain cases and cost some harmless sentences ("dollar-yield" beside a value is one); they are not a reader of meaning, and a claim worded around them is held by the instruction only. After the second review and the review of #227 (`API-CHAT-FIGURES-2`): a sentence says what its figure is. It names the kind of each figure it holds (`KINDS` in `vault-figures.ts`: worth or value; of the vault, share or target; units or tokens; price; cost, sell or exit; drawdown or fall; volatility; and so on), or at least an asset; one that does neither is cut. A sentence that names the kind of every figure and that figure's subject (an asset, or the vault for the vault's own value) stands on its own, whatever came before ("Your goal is to grow this money. Right now Nvidia is worth X."). Any other ("Nvidia: about X too.", "It is X of the vault.", "In all, X.") is read with the sentence before it, and a field's first sentence with the end of the field shown above it (the question and the objective under the message, the summary under the objective, a pick's reason and the first tradeoff under the summary, a list item under the item before, the first unknown under the last tradeoff): a rate, a period, a rise or fall, a magnitude, arithmetic, a forecast or a claim there cuts it, and the product's goal words (grow, income) do not. A sentence is not split after `approx.`, `e.g.`, `i.e.`, `vs.`, `est.` or an ellipsis that runs on. A `yield:` figure is served by a grammar (`yieldClauses`), not by what it avoids. A clause starts the sentence or follows another clause and must end the sentence: `[after the haircut | now-word] [its | the | the reserve's] (quoted | measured | observed | current | past | latest | historical) (yield | rate) [of | on | for | from the reserve] (is | was | of | at) [now-word] [about] Y [a year | annually] [now-word] [source]`; `[its | the reserve's] (yield | rate) (is | was) …` with a now-word before or after Y or a source after it; `… (yield | rate) at the last reading (is | was) Y`; `subject (yields | pays | has paid | …) [about] Y [period]` with a now-word before the subject, before the verb or after Y, or a source; `subject has a (quoted | …) (yield | rate) of Y [period]`; and the Portuguese `[o | seu] (rendimento | taxa) (cotado | medido | observado | atual | passado | histórico) (é | era | foi | está em) Y [ao ano]`. A now-word is now, right now, today, currently, at the moment, so far (hoje, agora, no momento, atualmente, até agora); a source is "measured from past rates", "at the last reading", "based on the latest quote" ("segundo a última cotação"). The subject and the owner are only it, the reserve (a reserva, ela) or a name of the yield's own asset, never another word ("Saving now pays Y a year" is cut, "The reserve now pays Y a year." is served); the period is a year, since the figure is a yearly one. What is left of the sentence beside the clauses is nothing or another figure judged on its own words ("It's worth X, and its quoted yield is currently Y a year."); free text there cuts the sentence ("There is no way to lose, and its current yield is Y a year."). Nothing else may follow the figure, so "…a year, forever" and "…a year, and you can count on it" are cut without a list; a promise word in the yield's sentence or in the one before or after it cuts it as a second line (count on, rely on, bank on, at least, next year, forever, for life, from now on, going forward, here to stay, keeps, steady, fixed, reliably, always, without fail, protected, risk, todo ano, não muda among them). "The reserve yields Y a year." with no word of measurement is cut. A claim no figure measures is cut with any figure (make you, a chance, odds, tax, fee, owe, over- or undervalued, safe, beats, worth more or less than, cheaper or higher than before, in the red). Neither the sentence nor the one it is read with holds a letter that is not Latin or a code point that is not shown; an emoji's variation selector is taken out of the prose first. "Up" and "down" are move words again, except in "makes up" and "up to date"; "yield" is a return word except in "dollar-yield reserve", "asset" or "token"; cash, gold and the reserve are named by their class words; the bold or italic marks around a reference are taken out; a drawdown and a volatility of one asset may share a sentence; a second reference may open a bracket after the first. Still passing, and accepted by review: plain comparatives beside a real figure (more than, nearly, only, per token), a wrong asset named by a nickname or a description, a figure between two named assets. A value that is not zero is never written as zero: where the unit's fixed places would show no digit, it is written to two significant digits. Follow-ups (`API-FIGURES-FOLLOWUPS`): in a sentence with two figures or more, every kind word (`BOUND_KINDS`: worth, of the vault, target, units, price, cost, capacity, drawdown, volatility and the rest) binds to the reference nearest it, and a figure whose bound words are all of another kind is cut ("priced at <a cost> and costs <a price>"); a figure no word binds to is left to the other rules. Where the kinds are named in a run and the figures given in another ("its price and value are X and Y"), as many words as figures, they pair in order. Swaps in other shapes can still pass, and a sentence with one figure is not bound at all. Beside a yield, the sentence before and the one after are read for the promise words as before, and for a forecast or a word that says it lasts only when the sentence speaks of the yield (`promisesTheYield`: it, that, this, the yield or the rate as its subject, or "you will" earn, get, keep or receive), or says of whatever stands beside it that it holds for good (perpetuity, nothing changes, year after year). For that forecast reading, a sentence about something else ("Nvidia remains your largest stock"), the agent's own ("I will show you the rest") and a caveat ("it will change over time", "that may not continue", "not a guarantee") are served: where the rule cannot tell, it serves.
