The promise words are read in every neighbour, whoever speaks and question or not ("I would rely on it forever.", "Safe and steady, right?" cut the yield); two honest shapes are spared: the clause in which the agent says what it cannot tell, promise or count on ("I can't promise it stays there"; what follows a comma, a colon, "but", "than" or "how" is read), the words would, should, keep and can't in an offer to the person ("Would you like the figure after the haircut?"), whose other words are still read. Most paraphrased promises beside a yield therefore still pass ("You can retire on it."). `kindRequired` stays off for the vault's conversation: with it on, twelve served cases of `vault-figures.test.ts` are refused, eight of them the reviewers' natural answers ("Cash makes up X of your vault (Y)", a bulleted breakdown under "Here's the breakdown:", "Your Nvidia position: X (Y of the vault)"), because a vault's value, share and amount are often said with no kind word of their own. The model is told not to add, subtract, convert, round or rank figures and not to say which of two is larger; the server does not state an ordering either. The log gets counts only: references resolved, missing and unknown. The screen (`features/vault-conversation/Figures.tsx`) draws each figure with `ProvenancePin`, the hatched mark where it is not live, and its age once the measurement is an hour old; a kept reply keeps its figures as served (`VaultConversationTranscript`, an optional `figures` on an app row: the template and the facts, 200,000 characters for a whole conversation, the oldest replies giving theirs up past it, each figure's place then reading "figure no longer kept", never a bare value; a reply whose `figures` the screen cannot read is not shown), and an earlier reply goes back to the model with its placeholders, not its values. On `/goal` the relaxed intake states figures the same way since the amendment of `RELAXED-INTAKE` (Thom, Oct 9; `relaxed-figures.ts`): the same resolver and sentence rules (`conversationFigures`, `withoutUnserved`), in its message only, over the measured figures of listed assets and none of a person. The request carries the ids, each with what it measures and never its value, for the holdings the conversation names (by symbol, what it tracks or company in any turn, by id in a draft the web resent, by a class word in the newest message), in the uncached part of the prompt: about 350 tokens a holding, where the whole catalog would be about 5,000 on every request; the other holdings are listed by symbol, and a reference to one of theirs is cut; a class word in the newest message loads at most six holdings beside those already named (`CLASS_LOADS`). Since the model sees no value here, the resolver is asked for two rules more (`kindRequired`, `noComparison`; off for the vault): a figure's kind word (`STRICT_KINDS`) stands in its sentence, or in the sentence before or its list's heading where its own has none, and a sentence with a figure holds no comparison. The relaxed prompt's cached block changed with this (rule 3 and what follows it), so its cache is rewritten once for each chain after the deploy; between requests the prefix is the same bytes as before. The model-led new-goal conversation states no figure: a reference there names nothing.
- **Checks after the model, in pure code (`runIntake`).** Each field of the reply is validated on its own; one that fails is null and flagged (`model_invalid:<field>`). An amount and an income must be written in the text, each in its role (the income as a rate a month, "$250 a month"; the sum put in never as one, so the two cannot be swapped: `wrong_role:<field>`), in dollars or with no currency beside them: "R$ 3.000,00", "3 mil", "$3k" and "3,000" are each read as 3,000, and the first only as reais, so a figure the model reads as dollars from a sum in reais is dropped and asked in dollars, naming the sum (`other_currency:amountUsd`). Euros, pounds, yen, francs, pesos and the dollar marks of other countries are read before or after a number; a bare amount counts as dollars only where the text writes no other currency anywhere. An amount in another currency never passes as dollars. A time frame must be written as one (months, years, a number in words, "half a year", after "for", "over", "in", "em", "por" and the like; or "by 2031"); "$300 a month" is not a time frame, and "35 years old" is an age, not 420 months. One figure given as both the amount and the income, written once, is doubted. A currency other than dollars must be written beside an amount. A shared portfolio the model names must be written in the text, by its name or its slug: the model reads what the person named, it picks nothing. One the text does not write is dropped and flagged, and nothing is asked about it (`no_cue:portfolios`; the review of Oct 6, finding 9). One that is written must be on the shelf of the person's chain, or it is asked (`not_on_shelf:themes`). A refusal ("no stocks", "sem ações", "no credit", "sem crédito") is taken from the text by code, with or without the model (`refusalsSaidIn`; found by the playground run of Oct 6, where with no model it was lost). It is read in the ways a person says one: after a bare negation ("no", "without", "never", "neither", "sem", "nem", "nada de"), as not wanting it ("I don't want any stocks", "não quero"), as not being able to hold it ("I can't hold stocks", "não posso ter"), and as keeping out of it ("stay away from crypto", "longe de"). One its clause states becomes the sheet's limits (`cannotHold.classes`, `creditTolerance: none`) and is said back for the person to confirm: it lowers what the plan may hold, so it is taken, not asked. A refusal of stocks ("no stocks", "no shares", "no equities", "sem ações") leaves out the funds of stocks with them, classes `stock` and `etf`: the product holds a fund of stocks as a stock token (gate `PROTECT-NO-STOCKS`; every `etf` on the shelf is a stock index fund), so without it the plan still held the S&P 500 fund. The read-back says "You left out stocks and stock funds." (`etf` is said "stock funds", "fundos de ações": the shelf also has funds that pay a dollar yield, which that class is not). A refusal of funds by their own name ("no ETFs", "no index funds", "sem fundos de índice") leaves out `etf` only, and single stocks may still be held; bare "funds" and "fundos" are money ("no funds needed before then") and name no class (Oct 7). The class that goes with stocks is no disagreement with the model, whichever of the two it gives. One its clause negates ("I can't do without stocks", "I have no problem with stocks"), says of something else ("my brother holds no stocks", "I have no stocks yet"), says of a part of a class or of a narrative ("no US stocks", "no stocks from China", "I don't want only stocks": a sheet leaves out a class, not a part of one) or only wonders about ("no stocks? not sure") is not taken (`refusal_<how>:<what>`); where the person is not sure, or the model read it as theirs, an assumption says it was not taken (`REFUSAL_NOT_TAKEN`). The model's `cannotHold` and `noCredit` are a check, both ways: what it gives that the text check does not confirm is not taken and is flagged (`not_in_text:cannotHold:<class>`, or `refusal_<stance>:<class>` where the text writes it in a clause that does not state it), and since the third review (Oct 7) it is asked once and not dropped: the question is the `limits` field ("Do you want to leave out stocks and stock funds?", in the words the read-back uses, its `read` the classes a yes would leave out), a plain yes takes it (`refusal_confirmed:<class>`), a plain no leaves the class in and one line says so (`refusal_declined:<class>`, `REFUSAL_DECLINED`), and the form's `limits` stand over it. A yes to it is the person's last word on the class: a mix, a narrative or a shared portfolio that would hold it is then not held, and never shares the sheet with it. With no model, a refusal the text states that a later mention of its class would take back ("No stocks." then "Remember, stocks are out.") is asked by the same question and not taken back on one reader's word: a yes keeps it, a no takes it back and says so; an answer that says what is held, or the form's limits, settles it without the question. "Do not buy stocks for me.", "I want nothing to do with crypto." and "Nada de bolsa." gave a plan with what was refused. What the text states that it missed is taken all the same (`disagrees_with_rules:cannotHold:<class>`). What goes with a refusal (the funds of stocks) is left out with it unless the text then holds it, and a class named after the refusal holds it only where its own clause says so (`classKeptAfter`): a plain ask, a contrast with the refusal ("no stocks, but ETFs"), or something said of it ("ETFs are fine"). A class the refusal only goes on to name carries it on ("including ETFs", "Same goes for ETFs.", "isso vale para ETFs"), which was read as "the funds are fine". With no model a refusal in words the fixed forms do not hold ("Stocks are off the table.") has no reader and is not read. Where the model and the rules parser both read the goal, the time frame, the risk or the language and differ, the field is flagged (`disagrees_with_rules:<field>`) and, but for the language, asked, with the model's reading as the form's start. A goal or a risk the model gives that the text has no word for (a goal or risk cue) is its suggestion, not a reading: it starts the form and is asked (`no_cue:<field>`). A chain the text names other than the wallet's is said (`other_chain:<chain>`); the sheet is on the wallet's chain. The second review (Oct 7) holds five of these checks tighter, each said in its bullet below: the amount (a bare number is not always a sum), the time frame (two in one message), the portfolio's name (said as a holding), the risk's word (one a negation is of) and the split's shares (each a share of the money); and a refusal leads its list and is said in a line of its own where the model missed it.
- **Since Oct 6 (gates `GLIDE-OPT-IN`, `MAX-YIELD-SLEEVE`; Rodrigo's first `/plan-chat`).** *A time to get the money out is not a time frame:* "can take up to 3 months to get out", "I may need it in 3 months", "posso precisar em 3 meses" are left out of the time frames the text holds (`exitTimesIn` reads them); a model that reads one as `horizonMonths` is overruled and nothing is asked (`exit_time_not_horizon`). Where the whole plan has it, the model gives it as `mayNeedInMonths`, held to the text and carried to `limits.mayNeedInMonths`; a time that covers one part only is not carried (the plan has no exit limit per part) and is said back as an assumption. *No date:* "no hard cap", "no deadline", "open-ended", "sem prazo" (`openEndedIn`, and the model's `openEnded` only with such words) make a goal with no date, unless the text also writes a date or a time frame, which wins ("no rush, but I need it by 2030": `date_in_text:openEnded`, and the date is asked if the model missed it). The sheet carries `horizonOpen: true` (shared `BasketSheet`, Oct 6), and `horizonMonths` holds `openEndedHorizonMonths` of the parameter table (120, a starting value) only so the engine runs: the glide is refused, no `goalMonth` is set and no reason names a date, the card's `termMonths` is null, a schedule runs to the last withdrawal only, the read-back says "with no date set", and the web says "No date set" and makes no due date (`PlanScreen`, `VaultGoalCard`, `dueOf`). `open-goal.test.ts` holds that no month or date made from the 120 appears in an open goal's plan. *A split in words:* the model gives `sleeves` (a safe-yield part and a goal part, each share as written); each share must be written in the text as a share of the money (Oct 7, below) and the two must make the whole, or the split is asked (`not_in_text:sleeves`). A percent beside "the other half" that with the half is not the whole ("70% ... the other half") is asked, naming both readings (`split_mismatch`, template `sleevesMismatch`), never guessed. *Risk per part:* the sheet has one risk; with a part kept safe it is the risk of the goal part ("as low as possible for the 70%, go crazy for the rest" is high), the risk question asks for that part only (`riskGoalPart`), and the read-back says the safe part holds dollar yield from a rate alone or cash whatever the risk. The risk is then not compared with the rules parser, nor where the rules parser's "medium" is its default (no word for medium in the text). Loose risk phrases ("go crazy", "go wild", "highest yield", "arriscar tudo"; never the bare word "crazy") are risk cues unless under a negation ("don't go crazy", "não pode arriscar"), and are said back as an assumption. With a part kept safe, the risk is still compared with the rules parser's, against the risky part, unless the rules parser read low (that word is the safe part's). A percent of the money the split does not hold ("70/30, but 50% in AI") asks the split again (`split_percent_unplaced`); a percent of a fall or a yield is no share. *Assumptions:* `assumptions` (from `ASSUMPTION_TEMPLATES`) says what was taken from the person's words: the loose risk words, no date, a time to get out, that a max-yield part is not built yet (`max_yield_asked`), and, when the glide is off and there is a date, that nothing moves toward cash unless they ask; they are also in the read-back before its last line. *Follow-ups in words:* the route takes `followUps`, the person's later messages, and each turn reads the first text with them (`conversationText`, which joins them with a blank line) through the same model and checks, so "70-30, I want to grow it" answers a question; the form's `answers` stay. *A share said in words (the review of Oct 6):* a reply of a few words ("70-30", "half", "all of it", "50%", "$500", "all of it in stocks") names no market and no question. Read with the rest of the text it was lost and asked again, or taken for a split of the plan, and only `answers.mix` carried it. So `runIntake` reads each message after the first as the answer to the `mix` question the messages before it left open, where they left one open and the message says only a share or a mix (`shareSaidIn`, `mixIn`; flag `mix_from_words`). Asked of a market, the share is the market's and the rest is cash; asked how the money is held, a pair is read in the order asked, stocks first. An answer on the form wins. A `mix` question that is there only because the model read a mix is not counted: the reply at hand is the model's reading of the whole conversation, that message included, so the question may never have been asked. Only the last few messages are tried this way (`INTAKE_LIMITS.turnsRead`). *The country* is neither asked nor read (gate `COUNTRY-REMOVED`, Rodrigo, Oct 6; it replaces `COUNTRY-ASKED`): the reader's schema has no country, the sheet carries none, the read-back says none, and neither `compose` nor `POST /v1/baskets/personalize` refuses a sheet for its country. Not changed yet: the web's goal form still starts the glide on when a draft does not say (`apps/web/features/goal/sheet.ts`, with two web tests that hold it), against `GLIDE-OPT-IN`.
- **What the person says to hold (gate `EXPLICIT-MIX`, Rodrigo, Oct 6).** When the text states what to hold and the model's reply reads it too, the plan holds it and no risk question is asked; with no model it is asked once, with what the text states as the start (the second review, Oct 7, below). *A mix:* "all of it in stocks", "only stocks", "stocks only", "all stocks", "100% stocks", "100 percent stocks", "70% stocks and 30% cash", "70% in stocks and the rest in cash", "60/40 stocks and bonds", "half stocks, half cash", "only credit", "only high yield", "all in gold", "tudo em ações", "tudo na bolsa", "só crédito" are read by code (`mixSaidIn` and `mixIn` in `intake-text.ts`, English and Portuguese) into `mix` on the sheet (`PersonalMix`: `growthBps` for stocks and crypto, `dollarYieldBps`, `goldBps`, `cashBps`, adding up to 10,000, and `creditBps`, the most of the plan in credit and basis legs within dollar yield, for "only credit" and "only high yield"). Every share must be written: two parts with no shares ("only stocks and gold"), shares that are not the whole, a percent of the money outside the mix, or words that say no share ("mostly stocks") give no mix here. A mix is of the whole plan, so no split is read or asked beside it. *How its clause says it (the review of Oct 6, finding 1):* a mix is taken only where its own clause states it as what the person wants held. A clause starts at a sentence break, a comma, "but" or "because", and at "so" or "and" before a word that starts one ("and I want"). A clause that rules the mix out gives no mix and no question (`mix_negated`): "I wouldn't put all of it in stocks", "I can't have everything in crypto", "I want more than just stocks", "instead of only stocks I want a balance", "Putting everything in gold is too risky for me". So does one that says it of something else (`mix_aside`): what the person holds elsewhere, someone else, another time ("I already have everything in stocks at my broker", "my brother is all in crypto", "I was all in stocks before"). A mix the person only wonders about ("Should I put all of it in stocks?", "maybe all in stocks") and one said of a part of the money ("70% safe, and the other 30% all in stocks") are asked once, by the `mix` question, with the mix wondered about as the form's start (`mix_wondered`, `mix_part`, `mix_asked:<why>`). Nothing is built from the opposite of what is written. English "so" is not Portuguese "só": "I am retired so no stocks please" is a refusal, and "so" with no accent is read as "só" only before a Portuguese word ("so acoes"). "Cash out", "cash flow" and "credit card" are no part of a mix. *The model's mix (finding 6):* the model gives `mix` in whole percents, and it is never taken on its own. Where the text states one and they differ, neither is taken (the third review, Oct 7; the text's was): the difference is flagged (`disagrees_with_rules:mix`) and the mix is asked once with no start (`mix_asked:differs`), since either reading may be the wrong one ("My advisor wants 60/40 stocks and bonds, but I want all in stocks" held the advisor's, and "Make the cash 50%." after a mix was lost). The person says the mix and it is taken as said; a plain yes has no start to take. Where the words cannot confirm it (`no_cue:mix`: another language, "mostly stocks"), the `mix` question is asked once with the model's reading as the form's start (`mix_asked:model`), and the risk is not asked in its place; an answer of `mix: null` says there is none. It is not asked where the text rules out that very mix, nor where the text gives the share to a narrative it names ("all of it in AI"). Where the text refuses a class the model's mix holds ("no stocks please"), the question has no start. *The risk (finding 4):* with a mix the risk is never asked and never marked unclear. The sheet takes the lowest risk whose cap per issuer (`capPerIssuerBps`) admits the mix's stocks and crypto, low for none (`riskForMixEstimate`; 100% stocks is high, 70% medium), whatever risk the person said: that is the risk the engine will use (`risk_from_mix`). The read-back says it in one line ("To hold “all of it in stocks”, the plan uses the limits for high risk."). Where the person also said a risk, on the form or in words the text has and the model read, and it is another, the same line says both: "You said low risk, but to hold “all of it in stocks” the plan uses the limits for high risk." (`risk_said_not_used:<risk>`). A risk the person gave is never replaced in silence. On engine/plans the engine derives the exact risk from the tokens the mix would hold (`riskForMix`, reason `MIX_LIMITS`), which replaces the estimate. The read-back says each part of the mix (`MIX_GROWTH`, `MIX_DOLLAR_YIELD`, `MIX_CREDIT`, `MIX_GOLD`, `MIX_CASH`). *A conflict:* a goal of income or to protect with stocks in the mix (gate `PROTECT-NO-STOCKS`) is asked once, as the goal question with template `goalMixConflict` (a goal to grow, or the plan with no stocks; `mix_conflicts_goal`), and the risk is not asked meanwhile; an answered goal of income or protect keeps the goal and drops the mix, said so (`mix_dropped_for_goal`, assumption `MIX_DROPPED`); an answered `mix` with no stocks keeps both. The answers take `mix` (null for none). *A soft time frame* ("I don't have a term, but I would say 5 years", "about 5 years", "uns 5 anos") is a time frame, with no glide: the date it gives wins over the "no term" words.
- **A market, an industry or a trend (gates `EXPLICIT-MIX`, `THEMES`, `THEME-MATCHED`, `THEME-NONE-YET`; Oct 6).** *The narratives:* `NARRATIVES` in `intake-text.ts` holds eighteen, each with fixed words in English and Portuguese: `big_tech`, `us_market`, `ai`, `semiconductors`, `ai_infrastructure`, `crypto_economy`, `fintech`, `space`, `quantum`, `ev_autonomy`, `cloud_software`, `emerging_markets`, `commodities`, `broad_market`, `retail_favourites`, `defense`, `health_care`, `social_media`. The words are read as an ask (`marketMentionsIn`). One its clause rules out ("no big tech", "I would never invest in big tech", "anything but AI", "sem big tech") is not read, and a list under one negation is ruled out item by item. Nor is one said of the person, or of what they or someone else hold ("I work in software", "I already invest in the S&P 500 through my pension", "I am already heavily invested in US stocks"). "Crypto" and gold alone are no narrative. "Space", "defense" and "health care" alone are read only after a word that puts money there ("invest in space"), and "social media" alone only with a verb or a share before it ("put half in social media"). The model gives `markets` by id, and never a portfolio, a label or an asset. One the text has no word for is dropped and the portfolio asked (`no_cue:market:<id>`); one the text rules out is dropped and flagged, and nothing is asked (`market_negated:<id>`, `market_aside:<id>`). *What a narrative reads to:* code decides it on the person's chain, in this order, and `narratives` in the answer says which. First its shared portfolio, where it names one and the shelf holds it (big tech: `the-seven`; the US market: `the-500`), the plan's starting point as before. Then its curated label, when the label is confirmed and lists a name on the chain (gate `THEMES`). Every label of Solana and Robinhood Chain is confirmed since Oct 7 (gate `LABELS-CONFIRMED`), so a narrative whose label lists a name there reads to its label, and its filters are reached only where the chain has no label for it or the label lists none of its names. Then its filters over the sourced attributes, in the order the table writes them: the first that matches a name listed on the chain fills the sleeve (gate `THEME-MATCHED`). Then nothing. The filters follow the stock data as sourced on Oct 6. Semiconductors: industry Semiconductors & Semiconductor Equipment. AI infrastructure: keyword data centers. Space: keyword launch services. Quantum: keyword quantum computers. Electric vehicles: keyword electric vehicles, then sub-industry Automobile Manufacturers. Cloud and software: keyword cloud. Emerging markets: keyword emerging markets. The broad market: keyword index fund. Defense: industry Aerospace & Defense, then keyword defense. Health care: sector Health Care. Social media: keyword social media. Big tech, the US market, AI, the crypto economy, fintech, commodities and retail favourites have none. The caller hands in the labels of the chain (`IntakeInput.labels`) and what a filter matches there (`matchOf`). A match must write the same value as the filter (`attributeKey`) and list at least two names (`INTAKE_LIMITS.filterMinListed`, Oct 7): a filter is never used to pick one stock, and where it matches one name alone the narrative reads to nothing and the line says so ("There is only one stock for “…” on Solana at the moment, and a theme is not made of one.", flag `filter_one_name:<id>`). A curated label is a person's list and may hold one. A label that lists nothing on the chain, or one that is only proposed (none is since Oct 7), is flagged for the operator (`label_not_listed:<slug>`, `label_proposed:<slug>`), and the narrative goes on to its filters. *A filter from the model:* a narrative the list has no id for ("obesity drugs") may come as `marketFilter`. It must be a valid filter (`MarketFilter`), with its value and its words within `INTAKE_LIMITS`, and the words must be written in the text as an ask, where no fixed word already reads them and they are no part of a shared portfolio's name. Otherwise it is dropped and nothing is asked (`model_invalid:marketFilter`, `no_cue:marketFilter`, `market_covers:marketFilter`, `market_negated:marketFilter`). With no model only the fixed words are read. A filter that passes these is taken as a narrative only where the person's words write its value (Oct 7, below). *Nothing on the chain (gate `THEME-NONE-YET`, Rodrigo, Oct 6):* the founder's sentence is said, naming the person's words and chain: "There is no stock for “health care” on Solana at the moment. We will be adding more soon." Where the shelf has one of the narrative's nearest offers, a shared portfolio or a usable label, the sentence names the first: "There is no stock for “quantum computing” on Robinhood Chain at the moment, and we will be adding more soon. The nearest today is AI, which you can choose." Nothing is held for it. No mix and no sleeve is made from it, its share is never asked, and no line about limits is said for it. The rest of the intake goes on as if it had not been named, so the risk is asked as for any goal. This holds for big tech, the US market and AI too. *The share of a narrative that is held:* `marketShareIn` reads it from the words before the narrative, only in its plain forms (Oct 7, below): the whole ("invest in big tech", "all of it in AI"), a sum ("put $500 in US stocks"), a percent or a half ("put 50% in big tech", "30% in AI", "half in AI"). A percent said of a narrative is its share, not a split of the plan. For a shared portfolio the share is held as a mix: that share in stocks and crypto, the rest in cash (`mix_from_market`). For a label or a filter it is a theme sleeve of the sheet (`{ kind: 'theme', theme, shareBps }`, the slug a label's, or `matched-` with the attribute and the value, as `matched-industry-aerospace-defense`), with the rest in the safe-yield sleeve, one sleeve for each narrative with a share (`sleeves_from_market`). The risk follows what is held and is never asked (`risk_from_mix`, `risk_from_themes`), with the same one line as for a mix. A narrative with no share said ("I like big tech") asks exactly one question, "How much of the $2,000 for big tech?" (field `mix`, templates `marketShare` and `marketShareNoAmount`, `market_share_unclear`), never the risk. The answer is a `mix`, and for a theme its share in stocks is the theme's. A sum waits for the amount it is a share of. A mix and sleeves are never combined by guessing. Several themes whose shares are not all written, a theme beside a shared portfolio the text also names, or a theme beside a written split that is not the plain form with the rest's figure written (the next point) ask the split once (`sleeves`), by a question that names the themes where they stand alone (`themeShares`, Oct 7). On a goal of income or to protect a narrative's share is not read and a theme is not held, and that is said. A narrative that reads to a shared portfolio is not put in the sheet there either, by the same flag and the same line (`themes_dropped_for_goal`, `MIX_DROPPED`; the third review, Oct 7: "Invest in the S&P 500." on a goal to protect started the plan from The 500 with no question); a shared portfolio the person names by its own name is not a narrative and is read as before. *How a matched theme is said:* as matched, never as a curated one. "Names", not "stocks": a fund can be matched too. The read-back and `narratives[].name` say "names matched by keyword: advertising", and the assumptions say "No curated list covers “advertising companies” on Solana, so the plan holds the names matched by keyword: advertising. Matched from the sourced attributes of each, not a curated theme." With every label confirmed, no fixed word reads to a filter on the launch shelf of either chain today: a matched theme comes from a filter the model names. The kinds of filter are said sector, industry, sub-industry and keyword. While the person has no chain nothing is resolved and nothing is said of what a chain has (`market_unresolved:<id>`): the chain is asked first.
- **A share written beside the share kept safe (gate `STATED-SHARE`, Rodrigo, Oct 7).** "8k safe and liquid, the 20% rest in a stock portfolio that follows big tech" was read as a part kept safe and a part for the goal, the risk of the 20% was asked, and at low risk the plan held 12% in stocks. A share the person writes for what they want held is held as written, and the risk is asked only where they write none. *The rule in `runIntake`:* where the model reads a split of one part kept safe and one other part, each share written, and the text gives the other part's share to what is held, the split says what the plain form says with the rest's figure written ("80% safe and liquid, 20% in big tech" is "20% in big tech and the rest kept safe"), and it is taken as that form is: a shared portfolio as a mix, that share in stocks and the rest in cash (`mix_from_market`); a label or a filter as a theme sleeve with the rest in the safe-yield sleeve (`sleeves_from_market`); stocks or gold with no narrative ("80% safe and liquid, 20% in stocks") as a mix of that share and the rest in cash (`mix_from_split`), where its words name no class the person rules out and are all the message says of money and holdings. *The pair is taken only in its plain form (the independent review of the first build, Oct 7, 325 sentences: "I used to keep 80% safe, 20% in stocks", "20% in stocks no way", "80% safe. Of the remaining money, put 20% in big tech", "80% high risk, 20% in stocks" and a later "Actually put the 20% in gold" were taken):* the safe part's figure is written once and its own words say it is kept safe (`KEPT_SAFE_AFTER`: safe, liquid, cash, seguro, líquido, caixa); the clause of each share states it; one narrative is named, once, and the share that leads into it is the last one written before it in its sentence; between the two shares there is nothing but the words that join them (`JOINS_SHARES`); nothing follows the second up to the end of its sentence; and no later message says anything that is not the answer to a question. Anything else is asked. The risk is not asked, the sheet takes the risk the holding's limits need (`risk_from_mix`, `risk_from_themes`), and the read-back says it once (`MIX_LIMITS`; with a risk the person said that is another, `MIX_LIMITS_OTHER_RISK`), followed, where the limits are those for medium or high risk, by "We count this as a plan that accepts more risk." (`MIX_MORE_RISK`, for every stated holding). The safe part's own figure is part of the form and is not counted as saying more (`share_not_alone`); a third percent, another holding, or a share that is not the other part's leaves the split and its questions as they were. *A market that reads to a shared portfolio, named beside a split whose share the text does not give it in a plain form* ("20% in a stock portfolio that follows big tech", "30% to grow, I like big tech", a share corrected in a later message, a split the person answered in the form): how much for it is asked once (`mix`, `market_beside_split`), and never the risk in its place, whether or not a risk was answered before. The form has a start, which a plain yes takes, only where the text ties the share to the market: the split is one part kept safe and one other part, the last share written before the market's words in their sentence is the other part's, the clause states it, nothing follows the market's words in the sentence, and it is the last word ("20% in a stock portfolio that follows big tech"). Otherwise there is none ("20% to grow. Big tech scares me", "Should I put 20% in big tech?", "20% in big tech and gold", a split only the form answered). `mix: null` leaves the split as written, and then the risk of its part is asked. *With no share written for what a part holds* ("20% to risk"), the risk of that part is asked, as before. The model is told that a part kept safe beside a share in a market, in stocks or in gold is a split, never dollar yield or cash of a mix (`INTAKE_SYSTEM`): a mix's dollar yield may hold tokens that lend to borrowers, which is not what "safe" says. Tests: `stated-share.test.ts`. **Not built:** the part kept safe written as a sum ("$8k safe") is not read as a share, so the split and the market's share are asked; for a shared portfolio the rest is cash, where a theme's rest is the safe-yield sleeve (a rate token or cash), because a sheet holds a mix or sleeves and not both, and a shared portfolio is not a theme sleeve; an answer to the share question that is less than the other part's share leaves the difference in cash, not in the goal.
- **How much a plan needs (gate `AMOUNT-NEEDED`, Rodrigo, Oct 7).** "I want $2k a month for a sabbatical year in three years. How much should I invest?" was answered "I can't size the amount for you", and after an amount was given, in round hundreds (`wayStepUsd`). `amountToMeet(candidate, sheet, shelf, context)` in `compose.ts` gives what a plan needs to pay what the person asked of it, to the cent: `withdrawalsUsd`, the least amount at which every withdrawal is paid at the rates observed (`status.met`), and for an income goal with a target `incomeUsd`, the least amount whose income alone reaches it (`verdict.met`); each null where no amount a sheet allows does it, and the whole null where the sheet has nothing to pay. It is found by making the plan, never beside it: the same engine, as the same candidate, with only the amount changed, by halving the range between the least and the most a sheet may hold; the answer was tried and met, and one cent less was tried and did not. The sheet's own amount is not read. It is a figure of the plan: it carries the plate of the yields behind it and changes when they do, and it is not a recommendation of an amount (the disclaimer stands). *Where it is shown:* the playground gives it for the three candidates (`GoalRun.needs`, `needs` in the JSON, "What the plans need" on the page) once the sheet is whole, and before that while the amount is the one answer missing, by reading the rest of the sheet with a stand-in amount that `amountToMeet` does not read; while the risk is missing too, at each risk, each row saying which, so no risk is picked for the person. The `/plan-chat` skill says it from there. Tests: `sizing.test.ts`, `tests/try-plans.test.ts`. **Not built:** the API's intake route and the web do not return it yet (it needs a field on the route's response, a shared type); the reader has no field for withdrawals, so in the chat they are written as an answer and with the API they come from the form; the ways to close a gap stay in steps of $100; the search takes the yields as observed and no stress.
- **Who must read a holding before it is taken (the second independent review, Oct 7; scripts in `review2-intake`; the guided intake's rule, no longer `/goal`'s since `RELAXED-INTAKE`, Oct 8).** The fix for the first review was a list of the ways a clause turns a holding down, and the second showed that such a list cannot be finished: of 136 new sentences that state no holding, 112 were still read as one. So none of the rules below is a list of phrases to refuse. Each says who must read a thing, or in which forms it is read; anything else is asked or left, never taken. *1. Both readers (with a model).* A mix, a narrative or a share of the money is taken with no question only where the model's reply reads it and the text check confirms it. Words the text check finds that the reply does not read are not taken, not asked and not said (`text_only:mix`, `text_only:market:<id>`), and the shares of such a mix are no split of the plan either: "I have all my money in stocks and want to diversify", "I have a cloud of doubt", "I'm done investing in AI" build nothing. The words of a filter the model names count as the model naming the market those words are. The model decides whether something was named; the code decides whether that may be taken. *2. No model.* A holding the text check reads is asked once, with its reading as the question's start, and is never taken: a stated mix by the `mix` question (`mix_asked:rules`), one narrative by "How much of the $5,000 for AI?" with the share the text gives it (`from_rules:market`), several themes by a question that names them in the person's words ("How do you want to split the money between AI and semiconductors?", template `themeShares`). A question has a way out and does not come back by itself once answered: "none", "zero", "0%" or "nothing for AI" leave what was asked about out (`none_from_words`, `market_left_out:<id>`), "half each", "50-50", "60/40" or a share for each by name answer the split (`sleeves_from_words`), and the form's `mix: null` leaves a narrative out too. A plain yes or no (`yesOrNoSaidIn`: "yes", "that's right", "ok", "sim", "isso"; "no", "não") says no share of its own and is read against the start of the question about what is held, with a model or with none: a yes takes the start, the share the text states (`mix_confirmed`), and a no leaves out what was asked about, as "none" does. Only where that is the one question asked (with another open, nothing says which the word answers), and not for the question that asks which of two things stands; with no start a yes answers nothing and the question stays. "The one question asked" is counted with the form as it stood when the word was sent, not as it is now (the third review, Oct 7: "Put 30% in big tech.", then "no" with four questions open, then the form filled in, left big tech out for good): the caller says it (`IntakeInput.answersThen`, the form's answers at each of the last messages; the route takes it as `answersThen` in the request, one entry for each of `followUps`, and the playground hands its file's answers for every message). For a message the caller says nothing of, the form is counted as empty, so a yes or no answers only a question that was the one open whatever the form says: one question more, never an answer applied to a question nobody asked. The same holds for the yes or no that answers the `limits` question and the one that asks of a shared portfolio by its name. *3. The last word wins.* The text is read message by message (`mixSaidInTurns`, `TURN_BREAK`). The last message that says a mix decides, and a mix whose class a later message refuses is dropped (`mix_refused_later`). A narrative a later message only rules out is not asked for. The last message that names a narrative says its share: where it names it and gives no share in a plain form ("invest $5,000 in AI", then "Scrap the AI idea"), the share an earlier message gave no longer stands, and how much is asked once with no start, whoever still names it (`share_not_last:<id>`). A refusal is taken back by a later message that names the class as held or as no problem ("No stocks.", then "I changed my mind, stocks are fine": `refusal_withdrawn:<what>`, said with `REFUSAL_NOT_TAKEN`), and with a model only where the reply does not read the refusal either. Where the text writes more than one time frame, the last message that writes one decides; one message that writes two ("over 5 years ... and in 2 years I buy a car") is no reading of one, and the date is asked with no start (`horizon_several`; `horizon_not_last` where the reply gives an earlier message's, with the last one as the start). A duration that says what it is the length of ("After 5 years of marriage", "in 2 years of day trading", "depois de 10 anos de empresa") measures that thing and is no time frame (the third review, Oct 7). Within one message any ask stands. *4. A refusal.* A refusal the text states is taken, with or without a model. It leads its list ("no stocks, crypto or gold" leaves out the three), and "leave out", the intake's own words, is one. One the model did not read is said in a line of its own that names the person's words, so a clause read wrongly is seen and corrected (`REFUSAL_TAKEN`: "I read “avoid stocks” as leaving out stocks and stock funds. Say so if that is not what you meant."; `REFUSAL_TAKEN_CREDIT`). A refusal and a holding of the same class in one conversation ("No stocks in my IRA, so here I want all stocks"; "No stocks. Invest in AI.") are one question by the `mix` field, with no start, that names both in the person's words (`refusal_conflict:<what>`, template `holdOrLeaveOut`), and never a sheet with both: a share for the holding takes the refusal back, and that is said; "none" leaves the holding out. Since the third review (Oct 7) the holding is whatever would hold the class: a mix stated or answered, a narrative, a shared portfolio the model names where no narrative reads to one ("No stocks. Start from The Seven." gave a sheet with both; the portfolio is not where the plan starts while the question is open, nor after "none"), and for a refusal of stock funds a narrative too ("No ETFs. Put it all in index funds."). A mix answered on the form that holds what the text refuses is the person's own word on it, whatever the text names beside it: the refusal is taken back and said (the generated-goals test found a sheet with both once it looked at every holding, a theme, a shared portfolio and a mix, and not at theme sleeves only). *The last word wins over an answer (the third review, Oct 7).* What a message answers in words is kept apart from the form's answers, with the holdings it was about. It closes its question until a later message names a narrative (the same again, or another beside it), states a mix where the mix was answered, asks again for a narrative answered "none", or refuses a class the answer holds: the answer is then dropped (`answer_reopened`), the message is no answer to the question it reopens, and the conversation is read again without it. The share is then asked once more and not taken, with the new reading as its start, which a plain yes takes ("I like AI.", "all of it", then "Put 20% in AI." asks with 20% as its start; "none", then "On second thought, put 30% in AI." asks with 30%); a refusal is asked against the holding ("I like AI.", "half", then "Also, no stocks."); and a holding taken back after its answer is no longer held ("Gosto de IA.", "tudo", then "Pensando melhor, não quero IA."). A share said of nothing ("Make that 40%.", "Make it $1,000.", "Hmm, make it half.": one figure, a percent or a sum of money, or one part of the whole in words, and nothing else about money or holdings, whatever leads into it) where nothing is asked and one holding stands is a new share for it: asked the same way. The same share said again asks nothing, and "none" said of the one holding that stands is asked too, with no start. The words of a message that answered a question are no split of the plan where the question is asked again. A message that says nothing of the holding leaves the answer as it is, and an answer on the form is never reopened by a message. A refusal of a part of a class ("No stocks from China": `REFUSAL_OF_A_PART`, flag `part_refused`) and a name ruled out of a list the plan holds ("invest in AI but no Tesla": `namesRuledOutIn`, `CANNOT_LEAVE_OUT`, flag `cannot_leave_out`) are not applied, and a line says so. A capitalised word after a negation is such a name only where it is a company the chain's sourced attributes know, by its name, the words its name starts with, its ticker or its token's symbol (`IntakeInput.names`, which the route and the playground hand in from `companyNamesOf`); any other word says nothing, and with no names handed in no such line is said (the third review, Oct 7: "No IRA involved" and "not in January" were said back as a company left out). *5. Plain forms.* A share is taken only in its plain forms, the whole too. The words that give it are a verb in the form that asks ("invest", "put", never "investing" or "invested"), what is put ("it", "all of it"), a sum, a percent or a half, and "in"; they open their clause, or follow the person's own words of wanting ("I want to", "I'd like to", "I have $5,000 to", "let's", "quero", "vou"). After any other word they give no share, and how much is asked once: "I can lose 30% in AI", "at least 30% in big tech", "I put $500 in AI last year", "I'm afraid to invest in AI", "70% of experts say invest in AI". It is a list of what is read, not of what is not: a form missing from it is asked, never taken. A list carries what leads it: in "Invest in AI and defense" the bare word is read as the next item of the list, what is said of the first item is said of each ("I work in AI and defense"), and two things named after one share take it to neither, so the split between them is asked. Where the text says where the rest of the money goes and that is not cash or kept safe ("30% in AI and the rest in stocks", "half in stocks and half in AI"), or carves a sum out of the share ("all of it in AI except $1,000"), the rest is not put in the safe part by guessing. Since the third review (Oct 7) this is one rule and no list of such phrases: a share is taken only where its message says nothing else about money or holdings (`saysMoreIn`, flag `share_not_alone`). Whatever the plain form did not account for, a sum written as money that is not the goal's own, a percent, or a word for something to hold that its clause states or wonders about, means the share is asked ("All of it in AI except for a $1,000 cushion", "30% in AI and the balance in stocks", "Invest in AI, but only 10%", "Invest in AI, and some gold too"). The goal's own sum, a time frame, a rate a month, the rest kept safe, a refusal, a mix the text states (read by its own rule beside a narrative) and a holding its clause rules out say nothing of a share. A percent written with a decimal mark is a share too ("0.5%", "2,5%"). The share and the rest are asked (`restOfMoneyIn`, `carvedOutAfter`, flag `rest_said`, template `themeAndRest`). A percent is a share of a split only where it opens its clause or follows a word that asks for it, and says where it goes ("70% safe", "30% in cash", "70% of it in"): "I can lose 30% and I am 70% sure", "70% of experts", "maybe 20%" and "20% at most" write no split, for a model's `sleeves` and with no model alike, and the percents of a mix the text writes are the mix's however its clause says it. A share smaller than the least a line of a plan can be (`minLineBps`, and `minLineUsd` over the amount: $25 of $5,000) is no share a plan can hold: "$20" in answer to "How much of the $5,000 for AI?" is not taken, the question stays, and one line says that least (`share_too_small`, `SHARE_TOO_SMALL`). The least is said as the floor it is ($5 for a plan of $300 or of $700, where it was said as $5.01 and $5.04 by rounding up to a basis point), and a share of that floor is taken when the person then says it (the third review, Oct 7). While the question that says a match is open (`matchedShare`), no line says yet that the plan holds the matched names (`MARKET_MATCHED` is said once they are held), and in Portuguese that question's way out is said as "Diga nenhum", the answer it is, not "Diga nada". A bare number ("5") is no sum, and a hedge or a bound is no answer. *6. A filter never picks one stock,* and *7. what a caller hands in*: `riskOfSleeves` and `riskOfMix`, the engine's own rule for the limits a sheet held in themes or in a mix takes (`riskOfMix` is handed the mix, the shared portfolios and the amount of the sheet, since the engine's rule depends on the amount); and `shelfKnown`, false where the caller could not read what the chain lists, in which case nothing is resolved, no sentence says the chain has no stock, no sheet is made from a goal that names something only the shelf can settle, and one line says so (`shelf_unread`, `SHELF_UNREAD`). *A filter from the model* is the model's reading, and the text check is its second reader only where the person's words write the value it must carry (`wordsWrite`: "defense stocks" for Aerospace & Defense, "insurers" for Insurance). The person's words for the thing must be the value's words (the third review, Oct 7: one shared everyday word was enough, so "my financial future" wrote Financials and "my emergency fund" wrote index fund): they write one whole item of the value, every word of it (a value lists its items with "&", "," or "and"; "the markets" is not "Capital Markets"), and they say nothing else, where every other word of theirs is a word of the value or says a kind of holding ("stocks", "sector", "empresas": the list `KIND_WORDS`). The same holds where a filter's words cover a fixed word of a narrative: they name that market only where the market's words are all they say ("a software course" does not name software). Where they do not ("obesity drugs" for keyword GLP-1, "seguradoras" for Insurance, "my future" for sector Consumer Discretionary), the link between the two is the model's alone: it is never taken, and is asked once by a question that says what it would be matched by (`filter_not_written`, template `matchedShare`: "I read “obesity drugs” as names matched by keyword: GLP-1. How much of the $2,000 for them? Say none if that is not what you meant."), with the share the person wrote as its start ("Invest $2,000 in obesity drugs" starts it at the whole), so that a plain yes holds what they wrote; a share in the answer replaces it, and "none" or a plain no leaves it out. *A shared portfolio's name* must be said as a holding, as a narrative's everyday words must (`portfolioSaidAt`): written as the shelf writes it or by its slug, after a word that puts money there or picks it ("starting from the seven", "put it in the 500"), or alone as an answer. Words that are the name and words that only could be are told apart (the third review, Oct 7: "The 500 dollars I saved should grow", "We want the seven of us to retire", "Home Team lost again"): written as the shelf writes it counts inside a sentence, where a capital is the name's own and not the sentence's and no capitalised word runs on from it ("The 500 Club"); any other writing needs a word that picks it before it and must end its clause; a name that opens its sentence has something said of it, which picks nothing. What fails this is `unsure`, and is asked by its name where the reply names it. "The seven of us are saving" and "the 500 reasons I save" are never taken (`no_cue:portfolios`); where the model's reply names the portfolio and the text writes its words with nothing that says they name it ("My pick is the seven."), one reader reads it alone, so it is asked once by its name (field `themes`, template `startFrom`: "Do you want to start from the shared portfolio The Seven?", its `read` the slug; flag `portfolio_asked`), never taken and never dropped with a flag (the third review, Oct 7): a plain yes takes it, from then on a holding like one the text states (`portfolio_confirmed`), a plain no leaves it out (`portfolio_left_out`), `answers.themes` stands over the question, and a later message that writes the name again is read anew. With no model a portfolio's name the text says as a holding ("Start me off from The Seven.") is asked by the same question with it as the start (`from_rules:themes`), as a mix is, and never taken; one the person only wonders about is asked by the portfolio question there too; one its clause rules out or says of someone else is dropped (`portfolio_negated`, `portfolio_aside`); one the person only wonders about is asked (`portfolio_wondered`). *A risk word a negation is of* is no word for that risk (`risksRuledOutIn`: "I can't take high risk", "Not low risk", "I am not aggressive"): a reply or a rules reading that gives it read the opposite of what is written, and the risk is asked with no start (`risk_negated:<risk>`). The negation may come after the word, in its own clause ("High risk is not for me.", "Risco alto não é pra mim."). And a word of degree alone ("low", "high", "medium", "alto", "baixa") is a word for the risk only where it is said of the risk ("low risk", "risco alto", "my risk tolerance is high"), ends a clause that names nothing else ("keep it low"), or stands beside another degree: "low fees", "a high tax bracket" and "interest rates are high right now" say nothing of the risk, and a reply that gives one is asked (`no_cue:risk`; the third review, Oct 7). A negation of something else is not of the risk ("No stocks and low risk" is low). *A number that is no sum:* a bare number is the amount only where it can be one. One said of the person ("I am 35"), or bare where the text writes another sum as money ("$5,000"), is not (`not_a_sum:amountUsd`), and the amount is asked. No code reads which of two figures is the sum put in, so where the last message that writes one writes several that could be it ("I have $5,000 and owe $2,000 on my card"; "My daughter is 12 and I want to invest 5000"), the reply's is one reader's and the amount is asked once with it as the start (`amount_several`, `sumsWrittenIn`; the third review, Oct 7). A sum that leads into what it is put in ("$500 in big tech", "$1,000 in cash") is a share and not one of them; bare numbers count only where no sum is written as money, and not one said of the person, one of a pair ("70/30") or one inside a market's or a portfolio's words. A later message that writes one sum settles it. *A year gone by* ("I got burned by big tech in 2022") is no date the money is needed by and does not turn the glide on.
- **Any language (gate `EXPLICIT-MIX`).** The model reads text in any language and gives its ISO 639-1 code; `language` stays the language of the questions and the read-back, en or pt, and any other is answered in en (`language_other:<code>`; with no model, a text with Spanish or French words is answered in en too): the chat translates. The checks after the model read English and Portuguese only, so in another language they fail safe: a value they cannot find in the text (an amount in euros, "sur 10 ans", "todo en acciones", "grandes tecnológicas") is asked, never taken.
- **Questions.** One per field still open or unclear, in a fixed order (goal, amount, the split, what is held, income for an income goal, time frame, risk, then the currency or a portfolio when unclear, and the chain while the person has none), from `QUESTION_TEMPLATES` in the person's language. What is held (`mix`) is asked in place of the risk, never beside it; its `read` is the mix it asks about, where there is one. The model writes none of them. The time-frame question asks for a date and takes "no date" as an answer. Goal and risk carry their choices. Answers come back in the person's words (`followUps`) or by field (`answers`), each field held to the sheet's own schema; answers by field are the person's entry and are not checked against the text, but a portfolio answered off the shelf is refused and asked again.
- **The read-back.** Once nothing is left to ask, the sheet is built (holdings counted, as the form starts it; the glide off unless the text asks to take less risk over time or names a date the money is needed by, gate `GLIDE-OPT-IN`; dollars unless a currency was read) and validated as a `PersonalSheet`. `readBack` says it back sentence by sentence from `READBACK_TEMPLATES`, in the sheet's language: the goal, amount, time frame (or "no date set") and risk, the time frame said the way the person said it (in years for "about 5 years", never "over 60 months"; in months where they wrote months or the months are an answer the text does not write; as a date for "by 2031", "by January 2031"), while the sheet holds months; the income; the currency; the portfolios by name; the chain; the holdings rule, and the glide only when it is on; the limits; each withdrawal; each sleeve, a theme by its label's name or by what it was matched by, the risk of the part not kept safe, and the restore choice; and the confirm line. Amounts are written with their cents, so no figure is rounded to one the sheet does not hold. `readback.test.ts` holds C18: every numeral maps to a value of the sheet once the locale is undone, and every capitalised word is a value of the sheet or a word of the templates; a planted wrong number, swapped chain, country or month, an added name each fail.
- **The route.** `POST /v1/baskets/intake`, signed in, rate class `parse`: `{ text, language?, followUps?, answers? }` in; `{ reader, language, draft, limits, questions, flags, disagreements, sheet, readBack, assumptions, mix, narratives }` out. It builds and stores nothing. The person's confirm sends `sheet` to `POST /v1/baskets/personalize`, which validates it again.
- **The evaluation set** has 18 goals, nine in each language: the twelve, plus "no credit" and "sem crédito", two amounts in one sentence in each language, "3 mil" and "R$ 3.000,00" (a goal in reais, whose amount in dollars is asked), and "no crypto". `expectLimits` is what the intake reads of each goal's refusals. `fixtures/intake-replies.json` holds a reply per goal and one for each check, goals that name a narrative with their replies, and conversations turn by turn, all written by hand in the schema's shape and labelled MOCK: no live model was run over the set yet. `intake-review.test.ts` holds every sentence of the review of Oct 6 with the reading it must have, read by a faithful reply, by one that errs toward the misreading, and with no model; and every sentence of the second review of Oct 7 (its scripts b1 to b7: 56, 56, 56, 36, 20, 29 and 24 cases, 277 in all), each with the reply its script used and with no model, and the share sentences also with a reply that names what the text asks for. Through the recorded replies the intake reads all 18 as the set expects; the rules parser still gets none wholly right.
- **With no model**, the rules parser fills the draft, and every field it read (goal, time frame, risk) is put to the person once with its reading as the start (`from_rules:<field>`): it misreads ("for 5 years" as 61 months, "no stocks" as high risk) and nothing checks it. A risk it reads from a word a negation is of has no start. What lowers what the plan may hold, or sets no date, is read by code the same with no model and is taken: the refusals the text states, and "no date". A holding is not (Oct 7): a stated mix and the narratives the fixed words read are asked once, with what the text states as the start, and never taken, since the text check is then the only reader.
- **What the route and the playground hand the intake (Oct 7).** Both read the person's chain as the plan route does (its tokens, the shared portfolios in effect, the curated lists and the sourced attributes of its stocks) and hand in: the labels (`shelfLabelsOf`) and what a filter matches (`filterMatchOf`); the keywords a model may name a filter by; `riskOfMix` and `riskOfSleeves`, the engine's own rules (`riskForMix`, and `riskForSleeves` in `sleeve-risk.ts`: the plan is made at each risk in turn by `compose`, and the risk is the lowest at which its theme sleeves hold the most in their names that they hold at any risk, a tie to the lower; what a sleeve keeps in dollar yield and cash because no name took it is not counted, and a cent for each line a plan may hold is the rounding of its parts, as for a mix), each asked at the person's amount where the intake knows it and at a fixed $10,000 until then; and `shelfKnown: false` where the route could not read what the chain lists (its adapter refused or did not answer): the goal is still read, nothing is resolved on the chain, and no sheet is made from a goal that names something only the shelf can settle. The playground's two sources cannot fail to read a shelf (a fixture in memory; the mock chain's own list), so it hands `shelfKnown` only for a source that says so. Where the engine cannot make the plan of a sheet held in themes, the intake's estimate on the issuer caps stands.
- **Not built:** a mix said of a part of a split ("70% safe, and the other 30% all in stocks") is asked and not read; a refusal of a part of a class ("no US stocks", "no stocks from China") is not applied, because a sheet leaves out a class and not a part of one (a line says so where the clause states it); a bound on a holding ("at least 30% in big tech", "no more than 20% in AI") is asked or left and never applied, because a sheet has no bound per holding; a whole allocation across a theme and other parts ("50% in AI, 30% in gold and 20% in cash") is asked by the split question, because a sheet holds a mix or sleeves and not both; a `mix` question raised by the model's reading alone is not answered by a later message in words, it is asked again with the new reading as its start; the web does not call the route yet (`apps/web/features/goal/read-goal.ts` still reads with `POST /goals`); the cache is per process and is lost on a restart; the model does not read withdrawals, sleeves or the restore choice, which come from the form as answers; a sum in words ("cinquenta mil") is not read by the checks, so a model reading it is doubted and the person asked. **Limits of the rules of Oct 7, as built:** two readers can both be wrong. The plain forms are built for a narrative's share and a split's percents, not for a mix: a mix the text check reads as stated ("I have all my money in stocks and want to diversify") is taken where a reply that errs gives the same mix. The last word is counted by message, not by sentence: within one message a later sentence that takes a narrative back in words the text check does not read ("Invest in AI. On second thought, forget AI, just all in stocks.") leaves the first sentence's share standing where the reply still names it. With no model a question's start is the text check's reading even where the clause turns the holding down ("I'm tired of being all in on stocks" asks the mix with all stocks as its start): asked, never taken. With no model, "No stocks." and then a later "Stocks scare me" is asked by `limits` since the third review, and no longer taken back. A filter the model names for words that name no market ("my future") still says the one sentence where it matches nothing or one name. Where one message writes two time frames the date is asked even where one is plainly the plan's. A bare number is still taken as the amount where the text writes no sum as money and does not say it of the person in the forms read ("I am 35", "aged 35"). A later paragraph is a later message: with no model, "Put 30% in big tech." and then "All of it in stocks." in two paragraphs reads the second as the answer to the first's question.

## 8. Risk sheet

**Per asset.** Rodrigo writes one sheet per issuer family in `content/risk-sheets/` (xStocks, Robinhood stock tokens, Coinbase stock tokens, each dollar-yield token, gold, the crypto majors: about ten files). Each covers the issuer, what backs the token, how you exit, issuer powers (pause, freeze, seize), who can hold it, incidents, sources and the date reviewed. The per-asset fields (address, price feed, token extensions, decimals) are generated from the asset list and chain reads. Liquidity numbers are computed, not written.

**Roll-up** (`rollUp`): concentration by issuer, chain and class; exit cost at the person's size; flags. It shows two exit numbers, because the audit found they differ several times over on Solana:

- **Quoted:** the latest stored quote at the nearest size, shown with its time. On Solana these are his 15-minute Jupiter snapshots; on EVM the collector's rows. A live quote is made only when an order is planned or a leg is built.
- **Measured, worst regime:** `LiquidityProvider.exitCost`. What the measured pools alone would give on a bad day.

The roll-up states the share of the plan that is measured.

**Solana.** His risk layer as it stands: four pool decoders, cost curves by time-of-week regime, LP concentration. It covers xStocks only; dollar-yield tokens show the quoted number alone. Rodrigo's side fixes three things before anything public reads it: the request that freezes the API (`POST /risk/positions/assess`), the hourly import that grows until it runs out of memory, and a regime with too few samples counting as zero capacity.

**EVM depth source** (`scripts/risk-evm/collect.ts`, free RPC):

- v3-style pools (Uniswap v3, Aerodrome Slipstream): a 30-line quoter that reverts inside the swap callback, injected with an `eth_call` state override and never deployed. v4 pools: the deployed v4 Quoter, eight sizes in one Multicall3 call.
- It picks the deepest dollar pools per token from DexScreener, quotes his size grid, keeps the best single pool per size, and writes `risk_asset_snapshots` rows in his shape with `method_version = 'evmq-0.1'`. Cost is against the pool's own mid, because the feed lags.
- His `scripts/risk/compute.ts` already fits every row. It needs about six lines so curves keep the snapshot's method version; that change is his.
- Measured today in session, selling $10k and $50k: Robinhood NVDA 0.01% and 0.07%; Base NVDAc 0.03% and 0.14%. The method is "best single pool", so cost is overstated when liquidity is split.
- Those two hand measurements leave the pool fee out. The collector (`scripts/risk-evm/`, running hourly since Oct 2) includes it, as Rodrigo's curves do, because the fee is part of what a person loses on the way out: NVDA came to 0.07% and 0.15%, and most Robinhood Chain stock tokens, whose deep pools charge 0.3%, to 0.3% to 0.4% at $10k. It measures only pools the vault can reach (Uniswap v3 pools the factory confirms, and v4 pools without hooks), each against its own mid price. Exit cost shown anywhere in the product is on this basis: fee included.
- Planned change (gate `UNIVERSE`, Oct 5; `docs/risk/PLAN-UNIVERSE.md`): the tokens come from an asset list built by the 80% rule, every pool of a listed token is kept, and the Chainlink feed is read at the same block. Until that is built, the collector is as described here.
- The collector runs hourly from Oct 2, which gives weekend and weekday regimes by Oct 5. A 28-day backfill on dRPC's free archive works but is out unless a stream is idle.

**Where the data runs.** His collectors stay on his Mac. The hosted database gets a dated dump of his curves first; an hourly copy job is a later add. Every sheet shows the date of its curves; stale curves are never shown as live.

## 9. Wallets and signing

- **Privy**, free plan (0 to 499 monthly users) **[C 7]**. Login methods `passkey` and `wallet`, through Privy's hooks that open no window of its own (WEB-1): `useSignupWithPasskey` and `useLoginWithPasskey`, two calls, so a person with no passkey can make one, and `useLoginWithSiwe` and `useLoginWithSiws` for a wallet found in the browser. Privy makes no embedded wallet by itself after a sign-in through its hooks, so `createOnLogin` is off and the web's driver makes a Solana and an EVM wallet for a person who signed in with a passkey (`useCreateWallet`): one job per person and attempt, one call at a time, with Privy's "User already has an embedded wallet." read as a wallet that is there. Until both exist the port is `loading` and says a wallet is owed, never `ready`: with one wallet the API would offer one chain, and the choice cannot be undone. `showWalletUIs: false`. Each EVM chain is a viem `defineChain` made from its chain config, with a public RPC URL that carries no key. Privy is told both networks of each (Robinhood Chain 46630 and 4663, Base Sepolia and Base); the default, and the only one the wallet signs for, is the network `NEXT_PUBLIC_CHAIN_NETWORK_<CHAIN>` names, the test network when unset. `local` is refused in the browser: a signature for a copy of mainnet is valid on mainnet. At start the web asks `GET /v1/config` and compares networks and chain ids; if they differ or the API does not answer, sign-in is off and says why.
- **One origin, decided by Oct 4 evening.** Passkeys bind to a domain and Privy does not accept a wildcard such as `*.vercel.app` **[C 7]**. Either a custom domain (about $10 a year, a person's decision under "free tiers only"), which lets the host change later, or one fixed Vercel project URL confirmed with Privy on Oct 2. No real wallet is created before that, and each demo wallet's key is exported once as the recovery path.
- **Packages:** `@privy-io/react-auth` 3.46.0 and `viem` 2.56.0, pinned exactly, and `@solana/kit` 5.5.1 in `apps/web` only **[C 8]**: Privy asks for 3.0.3 or newer, and 5.5.1 is the copy its own dependency already brings. Privy's code also imports `@solana-program/memo`, `system` and `token`, so the web lists them (0.10.0, 0.10.0, 0.9.0). Wallet-adapter and `@solana/web3.js` 1.x stay until his screens are rebuilt (WEB-2), then go. Three kit versions coexist and never meet: 2.3 in `chain-solana`, 5.5 in the web, which gets bytes, and 8.4.0 at the root as a dev dependency only, the one `litesvm` 1.5.0 brings for the root tests of the Solana adapter (ADS-2). Adding it moved `@solana/sysvars` under the web's tree (through `x402` and `@solana-program/token-2022`) from 7.1.1 to 8.4.0 in the lockfile.
- **One plan, one chain.** A person has a current chain, where a new plan is made; each plan is proposed, funded and traded only on its own chain (gates `ONE-CHAIN`, `CHAIN-SWITCH`). A person who signs in with an outside wallet starts on its chain; a person who creates a wallet picks one. Either can switch to a chain a wallet of theirs signs on, and plans already made stay where they are. A vault's owner is the wallet that opened it and never changes. One EVM address serves both EVM chains; with Base not deployed, a connected EVM wallet's chain is Robinhood Chain. Assumed, not decided (section 17): a person who signs in with a different wallet on another chain has a separate plan there. As built, a passkey sign-in makes an embedded wallet of each family, and the sign-in screen asks for the chain (WEB-1, two bullets down). The API's side is built (API-2): `GET /v1/me` says the current chain and what may be switched to, and `PUT /v1/me/chain` picks or switches it (3.3).
- **API sign-in.** `plugins/auth.ts` verifies two Privy tokens locally with `jose`: the access token, which names the user, and the identity token (`privy-id-token`, ES256, switched on in the dashboard), which lists the linked wallets **[C 7]**. Wallets are read only from the identity token, never from what the client says. The `aud` claim is pinned to the app id of each environment. No Privy secret on the server.
- **The pick, as built (WEB-1).** After sign-in the web asks `GET /v1/me`. While `chain` is null it shows the pick, with what it means and that it stands, and sends the answer to `PUT /v1/me/chain` (API-2); a person who connected a wallet has a chain already and is never asked; a second device reads the same answer and does not ask. From then on the web shows and uses only the wallet of that chain (`port.active(chainFamily(chain))`): the other family's embedded wallet exists and is never shown. The API offers the pick to a person with outside wallets of both families too, and the screen says why in their terms. On an API without the two routes the screen says it cannot tell the chain yet, and nothing is built for a guess.

| Wallet | A buy |
|---|---|
| Passkey (embedded) | Reads our review screen, taps once. Each leg is built fresh, checked by the guard, signed with no pop-up and sent |
| External, Solana | One prompt signs up to three legs; they are relayed in order; a leg that misses is rebuilt |
| External, EVM | First buy of a plan: `approve`, then `createVaultAndBuy`. Later: one `multicall` |

"One confirmation" is literally true only for the passkey wallet. The demo uses it.

- **No session signers and no Privy agent authorization.** Both hand a server or an agent the owner's full signing power, and the owner path has no limits in the vault.
- **The guard.** A passkey wallet signs with no pop-up, so the guard is the only check between a compromised API and the owner's key. It lives in `packages/sdk/src/guard/`, with the program id, the discriminators, the selectors and the vault proxy's creation code generated from `idl/basket.json`, the ABIs in `idl/evm/` and the contracts' build, and it runs inside the one leg executor that the web and the SDK share.
  - Solana, top level: compute budget; associated-token create-idempotent for the owner or the owner's vault; our program's instructions except `set_keeper`, with the owner as signer and fee payer. No System or token-program instruction at the top level; deposits move tokens inside our program.
  - EVM, by address and selector: the factory's two create calls; the person's own vault, with the address derived locally, for `deposit`, `withdraw`, `withdrawAll`, `ownerSwap`, `setTargets`, `acceptVersion`, `setAutoFollow`, and `multicall` decoded recursively; the cash token's `approve` only to that vault's address, for the stated amount, and never for everything. There is no `setOperator` (EVM-3 left it out of the interface), and a call to it is refused as any function the table does not name. `value` is zero. `ownerSwap` and `createVaultAndBuy` carry a deadline ahead of the guard's own clock by at most 30 minutes.
  - Swap amounts in the bytes must equal the `inRaw` and `minOutRaw` shown on the review screen. The transaction states them per trade in `preview.minimums`, and the leg's `expected[i].minOutRaw` is the same number once the leg is built.
  - A call that turns auto-follow on, accepts a version, or publishes, updates or takes back a shared portfolio is refused unless the executor was handed a consent the UI itself collected for that order.
  - Limits: the guard runs in the page it protects, so it does nothing against script injection. `apps/web` ships a nonce-based content security policy and a lint ban on `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. A compromised API can still show a bad price; the review screen prints the minimum received per leg.
  - As built (AGT-1). `guardTransaction({ step, tx, deployment, consents })` reads the bytes itself and never the preview, and throws a `GuardRefusal` whose code names the one check that failed (`GUARD_CHECKS`); `approvedSteps(order, plan, deployment)` turns the order the review screen showed into one approved step per leg. It has three readers: Solana bytes, EVM call data, and the operation of `packages/chain-mock`, which a deployment marked as the mock is held to in the same way.
    - Every address is derived from the person's own and none is read from the transaction: the vault (the program's seeds on Solana; on EVM `vaultOf`, CREATE2 from the factory, the beacon and the proxy's creation code), the config, the asset list, and each associated token account. A withdrawal goes to the owner's associated token account and nowhere else. The one exception is a withdrawal of everything: its bytes choose which tokens it takes, one instruction or call each, and the guard holds that choice to the tokens the deployment lists and those the caller says the vault holds (`held`, from its own read of the chain, each with its token program on Solana). Each token is taken once; its token program and every account are still derived, and a withdrawal never opens an account of the vault.
    - On EVM the guard passes a call, not a transaction: the target, a value of zero, the chain id, the call data, and the nonce and gas limit if stated. A transaction that carries any other field (a type, a fee per gas, an access list, an authorization list, `from`, a second `data`) is refused, and the pass is rebuilt from the fields read, so nothing reaches the signer that the guard did not look at. The signer sets the rest itself, and must: a legacy or EIP-1559 transaction, its own fee per gas under a ceiling, no access list and no authorization list. The web's port does all four (WAL-1); an outside agent's signer has to.
    - The step's instructions or calls are exactly the step's, in order: one more, one fewer or another is refused. Amounts, minimums, targets, the version and the auto-follow switch equal the step's. An approval names the plan's vault, for the step's amount, and an approval of 2^255 or more is refused whatever the order says.
    - The message hash is recomputed by the rule of 3.3, the preview's minimums must be the step's, and the label and the EVM chain id must be the deployment's. A fee ceiling bounds what the bytes can cost: on Solana the signature fee plus limit times price (0.005 SOL unless the deployment says otherwise), on EVM the gas limit and the fee the transaction states.
    - A creator's registry calls (AGT-4, gate `SHARED-FULL`), on Solana: a `publish` step is the first version (`publish_recipe`), the next one (`update_recipe`) or taking back the version that waits (`cancel_pending`), as the review screen showed it (`PlanTerms.publish`: the action, the family id, the assets and weights, the text the screen shows (slug, name, copy, kind), the version), and needs the consent `publish`. The shared portfolio is the registry's account derived from the creator, who is the step's owner and the one signer, and the family id; the assets and weights and the family id in the bytes are the step's, the text hash is the one the guard works out from the text shown (the encoding of 3.1, `familyTextHash`), never a hash handed over, an order that publishes has that one step and no other, and the fee cap and the flags are zero. On Robinhood Chain `IndexRegistry`'s `create`, `publish` and `cancel` are refused as unsupported until its interface is final (EVM-3), and so is a publish on the mock. Not signed here: the keeper's two kinds. `accept_version` and `set_auto_follow` are signed on both families, each with the consent the step needs (`new_asset` for an accept, `auto_follow_on` for the switch turned on): on Solana since the keeper path reached `idl/basket.json`, on EVM since it reached `idl/evm/` (EVM-3). `sync_balances` is no step of an order: beside one it is an instruction the step does not have, and refused. `pnpm --filter @colosseum/sdk tables` writes the tables; `packages/sdk/scripts/tables.test.ts` fails while they are not what the files give.
    - The same calls on EVM (AGT-4, Oct 6): `IndexRegistry`'s `create` for the first version, `publish` for the next and `cancel` for the one that waits, each a call with no value to the registry the deployment names (`registry` in `packages/sdk/deployments/<network>.json`; with none, a publish is never signed). The id is keccak256(abi.encode(creator, familyId)), derived from the step's owner and family, never named; a create is held to the family id, the weights, the hash of the text shown, a fee cap and flags of zero; a publish to the id, the weights and the hash; a cancel to the id (codes `target`, `function`, `recipe`, `targets`, `limits`). `packages/sdk/src/guard/evm/registry.test.ts` (each negative refused by the one check that names it, and the committed 46630 registry), `tests/evm-vault/guard.test.ts` (the adapter's own create, next version and cancel on a copy of 46630) and `tests/evm-vault/index-id.test.ts` (the id from viem and from the guard).
