    - What it cannot check: the route of a swap (its bytes and its accounts go to the router as they are; the vault holds the trade to what it spends and receives), anything that needs the chain (that an account exists, what `Config.router_program` is, which network a Solana blockhash belongs to, the nonce an EVM wallet signs on), and whether a price is fair. Nor, from the bytes alone:
      - whether an earlier signature of the same step can still land. The executor keeps that rule (one approved step, one signature) with the caller's own read of the chain.
      - when a signed EVM step that does not trade stops being good. A trade does at its deadline (EVM-3: `ownerSwap` and `createVaultAndBuy` take one, the guard passes it at most 30 minutes ahead of its clock, `deadline`). The other steps take none and can land while their nonce is unused: none of them trades, so what it does does not depend on when, and the wallet's next transaction takes that nonce.
      - what a followed shared portfolio holds beyond its id and its version: the weights and assets of that version are the registry's, and the step names only the number the person accepted.
      - the rent of the token accounts a transaction opens. The fee ceiling counts the signature and the compute price; each account a step may open (at most one per token it moves, held to the rules above) costs the person about 0.002 SOL of rent on top.
      - the fee per gas of an EVM step, which is not in the call it passes: it bounds the gas limit and the fee the transaction states, and the signer bounds the rest.
      - that an order is `done`. The executor answers `done` when the API says every step is confirmed; it does not read the chain for it.
      - the version a registry call makes or takes back, and when it takes effect: the registry numbers versions itself and waits its own publish delay, and `cancel_pending` names no version, so the version a screen shows is shown and not checked. The author limits (3 to 12 assets, 2% to the ceiling, the turnover and the 48 hours) are the program's to refuse, not the guard's.
    - The deployment it is given, and the plan's number and targets beside the order, are the caller's to get right: they must not come from the server that builds the transactions. A deployment is taken only as the package loaded it from the file committed for its network (`deploymentsOf(network)`, `packages/sdk/deployments/`); an object written by hand, spread from a loaded one or read from `GET /v1/config` is refused by the type and again when the guard runs (`deployment`).
    - The switch that takes a check out for the package's own tests is not in the guard: the tests put `test/rules.ts` in place of `src/guard/rules.ts`, and nothing built imports it.
    - The minimum is held to equality, and the server builds to it (API-BUY-MIN, Oct 6). An order states each trade's minimum when it is made; every build of a step hands the builders those figures (`minimums` in `CreateVaultArgs`, `DepositArgs` and `OwnerSwapArgs`; `statedMinimum` in `packages/schemas`), and a builder given them writes them and works out none of its own. Until then a build took a fresh quote and a slippage and no floor, so its minimum was another number whenever the price had moved, the guard refused the step, and a buy on a chain that trades after its deposit stopped with its cash in the vault (a yield token's price ticks all day). A builder still quotes: when a trade would now give less than its stated minimum it refuses with `PriceMoved` (`PRICE_MOVED`, 409, not retryable) and nothing is built. The terms are never changed for the price; the order is made again, or finished as the next line says.
    - A buy that stopped after its deposit is finished with the cash in the vault: `POST /v1/orders/{id}/continue` (owner only, no body) answers a new order of the swaps the first one left, with the same amounts, quoted now, for the person to review and approve. It has no `depositRaw`, names the first order in `continues`, and is refused when the deposit has not landed, when nothing is left, while a transaction built for a step left can still land, and when the vault holds less cash than the steps would spend. An order is finished by one order: asked again while that one is open the answer is the same order, and from then on the first order builds nothing more and what the second left is the second's to finish, so no swap is planned twice (`orders/continue.ts`). A build of a step and this route each end in one short transaction that takes the order's lock (`lockOrder`: an advisory lock of the transaction, by the order's id), checks again and writes: recording a build checks that no order finishes this one, and storing a continuation checks that none exists and that no step it remakes was built since it looked. So two requests at the same moment cannot both pass. The chain's node is asked everything before that transaction, never inside it; a request holds one connection for it, for a few statements; and the wait for the lock is two seconds at most, after which the answer is 409, retryable. It is for a chain that trades after its deposit: on an EVM chain, where the trades ride in the step that deposits, it is refused. `continues` is the order's word, kept beside the stored request and never read from one. Each refusal carries a code of `OrderErrorCode` beside its sentence (`ORDER_CONTINUED` with the other order's id in `details.continuedBy`, `STEP_IN_FLIGHT`, `STEP_LANDED`, `ORDER_BUSY`, `NOTHING_LEFT`, `VAULT_CASH_SHORT`, `DEPOSIT_NOT_LANDED`, `CONTINUE_NOT_SUPPORTED`): a client acts on the code and shows the sentence.
- **Sending and reporting.** On Solana the wallet signs and the web posts the bytes; the server broadcasts only if they hash to the `messageHash` of an attempt it built. If a wallet changed the bytes, the server does not relay; the wallet sends and the web reports the id. As built (WAL-1), the web's `WalletPort` checks what a wallet hands back before returning it as signed: on Solana the same message byte for byte with this account's valid signature, on EVM the same call, signed by this account, of a plain type, with the fee under a ceiling. So a wallet that changed the bytes fails in the browser with `changed`, and the path where it sends by itself is open: it needs a `send()` on Solana, which `WalletPort` does not have. For every reported id the server fetches the transaction and matches signer, target and call data (on Solana: our instruction's accounts and data) to the built leg before marking it confirmed.
- **Fees.** `GET /v1/funding` returns what is missing on the plan's chain: cash plus native gas. The buy button is disabled until the wallet is funded there. As built (API-2): asked with the plan and the amount, it answers `missingRaw` for the dollar token and for the native token, each figure with its source, time, method and provenance, and `ok` when nothing is missing.
- **Recovery.** "Export key" per family in settings. After the first deposit the app prompts for a second passkey or an email. `scripts/ops/withdraw-without-app.ts` withdraws in kind with only the owner key.

## 10. Backend, keeper and hosting

**API (`apps/api`).** His Fastify app, plus:

- `plugins/auth.ts`: a person by Privy tokens. The MCP server has no key of its own (gate `MCP-TOOLS`): it passes on a person's sign-in when its client sends one. Every `/v1` route declares `config.auth` and `config.limit`. Default deny (API-2): the API does not start with a `/v1` route that declares neither, wherever it is registered, nor with one registered outside the `/v1` scope, where no sign-in is checked and no request is counted; a route with no rule is closed whatever its method. Shelf, risk sheets, quotes, personalize and order creation stay keyless (as built, API-3: the shelf, a shared portfolio's page and its versions answer anybody; an order still needs sign-in). An order is reachable only by its UUIDv4.
  - As built (API-1): the access token goes in `Authorization: Bearer`, the identity token in the `privy-id-token` header, and both must name the same person. `PRIVY_APP_ID` names the app; with none set, every route that needs a person answers 503. Each token is held to its own job: the access token must carry a session id (`sid`) and no `linked_accounts`, the identity token must carry `linked_accounts`, so neither can stand in for the other. Five seconds of clock difference are allowed. For now every order route needs sign-in, creation and the read included: an order belongs to the wallets that made it, the `owner` in a request must be wallets of the identity token, and to anybody else the order answers 404, as an id that does not exist does. An order is still created only with the owner's sign-in: the agent surface (AGT-2) makes plans keyless instead, and the person places the order from the link (gates `AGENT-LINK`, `MCP-TOOLS`). There is no service key; the consent route is not built yet.
- `plugins/limits.ts`, in memory, keyed by Privy user first, then by client address: 60 a minute anonymous, 120 signed in, 10 on the parser, 30 on the builders. `@fastify/helmet`. A CORS allowlist. One error handler that returns a request id and no SQL. No node URL in a log line (`src/redact.ts`, API-REDACT-LOGS): the logger's one hook takes every configured `*_RPC_URL` out by its setting's name and cuts any "URL: …" a library's error names, in messages, error stacks and their causes, as the keeper's `hide` does.
  - As built (API-2): no library, and the numbers are in `LIMITS` and nowhere else. Every `/v1` request counts against its caller: a signed-in person by who they are, anybody else by client address, a sign-in that failed included. A route names a class: the builders (`POST /v1/orders` and the leg build) share 30 a minute per caller on top of that, and the parser's class has 10 and no route yet. A route may name the class of one request from its query (`config.limitOf`): `GET /v1/funding` with `family` plans the buy's order, and counts as a builder then (WEB-4). Over a budget the answer is 429 with `RATE_LIMITED`, `retry-after` and the `ratelimit-*` headers. The limits apply to `/v1` only.
  - CORS is decided by path in `plugins/cors.ts`, on the path as the router reads it (`plugins/paths.ts`): escapes decoded, dot segments resolved, case and doubled slashes dropped, so `/%761/config` is held to the allowlist as `/v1/config` is. `/v1` answers a browser only from `CORS_ORIGINS` (unset: `http://localhost:3000`; `none`: no origin; a star is refused at start), on a read and on a preflight. Every other route, the risk layer's `/risk/*` included, answers any origin as it did, and `tests/risk-routes-untouched.test.ts` holds the API's `/risk` routes to what `apps/risk-api` answers: no sign-in, no limit, the same CORS headers, the same documents.
  - Not built: `@fastify/helmet` and the forwarded client address of the next line. In its place (SEC-PASS-1, Oct 6): `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` says how many proxies the host puts in front of the API, and each caller is then counted by the address those proxies wrote, never by one the caller wrote before them (`plugins/proxy.ts`). Unset, a forwarded address is not believed, so callers behind one proxy share its address and its 60 a minute. To set it, read the host's log after a deploy: the API says "forwarded hops seen: N" the first time it sees each count of `X-Forwarded-For` entries (the count, never an address; at most eight lines). The number to set is the lowest one logged: a caller can write entries of their own in front of the proxies', so a count can be too high and never too low. Once hops are set, Fastify also believes `X-Forwarded-Host` and `X-Forwarded-Proto`, so nothing in the API may decide anything by `req.host` or `req.protocol`. The structurer's six writes outside `/v1` (`OPEN_WRITES` in `plugins/limits.ts`: `POST /goals`, `/plans`, `/plans/:id/transactions`, `/executions/:id/report`, `/policies/:id/revoke` and `/rebalance`) share the anonymous 60 a minute by address, counted apart from `/v1`, and are otherwise as they were. A route outside `/v1` and `/risk` that throws answers the request id, not the error's message, under the status it had, for every 5xx (`plugins/errors.ts`); a 4xx is answered as before. A request's id is random, not a count. A signed-in caller is counted by person only: one address with many identities gets 120 a minute for each, until there is a limit per address beside it.
- The browser calls `/api/*`. `apps/web/proxy.ts` rewrites it to the API host and adds the client address with a shared-secret header; the API trusts a forwarded address only with that header.
- Consent is stored against the order id, the address and a hash of the legs it covers.
- With `LEGACY_STRUCTURER=off`, no route that reaches a signer is registered. As built (API-2): off is the default, and the file that holds the one such route (`routes/monitor-rebalance.ts`) is then not loaded at all (section 2, rule 5).

**RPC, free.** EVM: viem `fallback` over Alchemy free, dRPC and the public endpoint. Solana: Helius free (1M credits a month).

**Jupiter.** The free plan is 1 request a second and 60 a minute per organisation, not per key **[C 2]**. Rodrigo's collectors keep his organisation; the app and the keeper use a second one that Thom opens on Oct 2. Inside it the API is capped at 30 calls a minute and the keeper at 30. All read quotes pass through a cache keyed by asset and size bucket, held for 30 seconds; only leg builds call Jupiter live. Tests use recorded fixtures and the mock router; one named job may hit Jupiter.

**Keeper (`apps/keeper`).** A plain TypeScript worker with `--once` and `--loop` and no HTTP listener. Each pass, per chain:

1. Open a run row (one open run per chain).
2. List auto-follow vaults. Take each one that is behind a version whose `effectiveAt` has passed, or whose `synced_version` is behind its accepted version.
3. Adopt where needed. Read the vault from the chain and plan with `planRebalance`.
4. Skip a leg whose live quote costs more than the tolerance against the reference price; log it as `skipped`.
5. Write the leg row, build, simulate, compare the simulated balance changes with the plan, sign and send. Vaults go serially in random order.
6. When a vault has every position inside the band, set `synced_version`. The keeper then leaves it alone until the next version. Drift rebalancing later is this pass without step 6.

- Solana send: blockhash just before signing; compute limit = units used × 1.2; priority fee from recent fees, clamped to its cap, and at least 1 micro-lamport a unit where the cap allows it (with no price instruction, a wallet such as Phantom adds its own and the guard refuses the changed transaction; devnet's recent fees are 0), none where the cap is 0 or too small for the limit; the same signed bytes go to Helius Sender and the RPC, rebroadcast every 2 s until confirmed or past `lastValidBlockHeight`.
- EVM send: `pending` nonce stored on the attempt; `simulateContract` first; `maxFeePerGas` at twice the base fee (memory).
- Retries: a reverted leg is never sent again. A leg that expired without landing is re-planned from chain state in the next pass, at most 3 times per vault and version, then an alert.
- Alerts go to a Discord webhook: a rejected leg, three expired attempts, low gas, a stale feed in session, a vault past half its loss budget, a failed authority check. The keeper holds no guardian key; it only alerts. Each pass pings healthchecks.io.
- As built (KEEP-1), on Solana only: `apps/keeper` (`README.md` there). Each round reads every auto-follow vault from the chain through the adapter, in random order, and gives each at most an adoption (a version due by the cluster's clock that adds no asset, not while paused), a sync, and one leg planned by `rebalancePlan` on chain state and the prices of that vault's own holdings. Before building a leg it skips a vault that is blocked, has no price account or has used its loss budget, and passes over a trade on an asset switched off for the keeper (gate UNIVERSE: no oracle), with no usable reference, in cooldown, out of session, in a multiplier window or with a transfer hook. A vault that holds a switched-off asset gets no leg at all, since the program values every held position by its reference. Records that differ from the accounts are synced only when every changed position has a fresh reference in range; otherwise the vault is skipped with an alert, because a synced unit with no usable reference stops every leg in the vault. A leg is remembered with its signature and last valid block height from before it is sent until the chain settles it (landed, reverted, or expired past that height); an error or no answer after sending never counts as not taken, and nothing more is planned for its vault meanwhile. A reverted leg is never sent again for the same vault and version (team lead, under Thom's authority, Oct 5): every round that passes it over raises an alert, and a vault that takes another version is planned afresh. Both are kept in a state file per network and genesis outside the repository (`KEEPER_STATE_DIR`), so `--once` runs remember as `--loop` does; one keeper holds the file at a time (a lock beside it, hard-linked into place whole, taken over only from a process that is gone, under a takeover lock that moves the stale one aside and checks it before removing it), and a lost file forgets them. One JSON line per vault per round; with `--loop` a round that fails (the node did not answer) is a `round-failed` line with an alert and is retried after a back-off of 15 s doubling to 5 minutes, never the end of the loop; an alert on every line of a vault past half its loss budget; `--once`, `--loop`, `--dry-run`; the record's genesis checked, mainnet refused; it signs only as the record's default keeper. A shared portfolio holding gold gets the one-tap rebalance prompt, not auto-follow (gate GOLD-ONE-TAP). Not built yet: run and leg rows (`keeper_runs`, `keeper_legs`); `synced_version`; the expired-attempt count; Helius Sender rebroadcast; alerts beyond the log line.

**Snapshot worker (`apps/snapshot`, PORT-1, gate `SNAPSHOT-WORKER`).** A plain TypeScript worker beside the keeper, with `--once`, `--loop [--interval 600]` and `--dry-run`, no HTTP listener and no key. It keeps what the product needs to show a plan over time: every ten minutes, one row of `vault_snapshots` per vault (section 4). Its README (`apps/snapshot/README.md`) has the commands and the lines it prints.

- **It reads and never signs.** It is given a reader of each real chain (`createSolanaVaultReader`, `createEvmVaultReader`), never an adapter, loads no signing entry and is not in the boundary test's list of who may sign. `tests/snapshot/reads-only.test.ts` fails on a builder, a relay, a signer or a send in its folder, by name or by JSON-RPC method (`eth_send...`, `eth_sign...`, and every method under `personal_` or `wallet_`, which a reader has no call for: `personal_unlockAccount` and `wallet_addEthereumChain` as much as `personal_sign`), and on a viem wallet client. The one file that builds anything is `mock-world.ts`, and only on `packages/chain-mock`.
- **Test networks only.** `mainnet` in any `CHAIN_NETWORK_<CHAIN>`, whatever the chain's mode, stops it before a node or the database is touched. Each node is then held to its deploy record (`assertNode`), and a network whose rows would be labelled `live` is refused. Rows are `sandbox` from a test network and `mock` from the mock. The `vaults` rows, order steps and earlier snapshots a pass starts from are filtered by that label, so a mock address is never asked of a real chain.
- **Which chains.** `SNAPSHOT_CHAINS` (a comma list), or every chain whose `CHAIN_MODE_<CHAIN>` is `live` or `readonly`, which mean the same here. A chain on the mock is read only when it is named, so nothing fills the tables with sample rows by omission. Base has no reader for a real network yet and is refused there.
- **Which vaults.** Only our people's. By address at every pass: the `vaults` rows of the chain, which the goal join writes when a vault is opened, and every address the worker has snapshotted or found. By owner, the heavier read: at the first pass, once an hour, and at once for an owner new to the worker. Owners are the `user_wallets` of the chain's family (nothing writes that table yet), the owner of any order with a confirmed step on the chain, and the owners the `vaults` rows name. A vault one of them opened outside the app is found within the hour. An address with no vault is skipped and not asked again until the next hourly read.
- **A pass, per chain.** Open the run: one open run per chain, whatever the label. A start closes every run left open on its chains. A pass that finds a run open closes it as left open and goes on when the run started longer ago than any pass can take (two intervals, and 30 minutes at least; 30 minutes with `--once`); a younger one may be another worker's pass, and the pass is skipped with one line. Read the chain's height (a slot on Solana, a block on EVM; null when the node cannot say it in ten seconds), its settings (the keeper's pause, the loss budget and the band: `Config` on Solana, the factory on EVM) and the asset list. Read the vaults, four at a time. Read the prices of the listed assets they hold in one call, and asset by asset if that call is refused. Value each vault with `view()` of `packages/basket` and write its row with the prices it stood on. An asset the chain has no price for is kept with no value. A vault that holds an asset whose price read failed is not written at that pass, since a row without that price would understate the vault. One JSON line per vault (`read`, `failed`, `skipped`, with the reason) and one per pass.
- **What fails a pass.** The settings or the asset list could not be read; no vault at all could be read and a read was refused as unavailable; no price at all for the same reason; no vault was read and at least one failed, whatever the reason (`no vault was read: 3 failed`); or the database. A pass with nothing to read, because no vault is known or none is at the addresses asked, is a good pass, and a chain that cannot say its height fails nothing: the row carries none. The run row then carries the reason, and that chain backs off alone, 15 s doubling to 5 minutes, while the others keep their interval. The loop is the keeper's, copied into `apps/snapshot/src/loop.ts` because no app imports another, and held equal to it by `tests/snapshot/loop.test.ts`. One vault that cannot be read, valued or written beside one that was is a `failed` line and the others go on. What ends the worker at start is said the same way as in a pass: a database that does not answer reads `the database did not take a query (ECONNREFUSED)`, the code of the refusal and never the query. No line and no stored reason carries a node's address, the database's or the ping's (`hide.ts`).
- **Health.** `SNAPSHOT_PING_URL` (healthchecks.io) is pinged after a pass when no chain's newest pass failed or was skipped, and its `/fail` after a failed pass. A skipped pass sends neither, so a chain that an open run keeps from starting lets the check run out.
- **The mock run.** Each process has its own chain in memory, so the worker cannot see the API's mock. It replays each `vaults` row labelled `mock` at the row's own address (the same owner and plan number), with a deposit of the cached value bought at the row's targets, and moves the mock's prices as a function of the time alone. With no row at all it makes one sample vault. These are sample figures, labelled `mock`, for building the web with no node.
- **Cost, against the free allowances above.** Reads go one vault a call today. Solana: 2 to 3 `getMultipleAccounts` a vault a pass and three calls a pass, so about 430 calls a vault a day at ten minutes; fifty vaults would be about 650k a month, most of Helius's free million if nothing else shared the key. Robinhood Chain: about 40 `eth_call`s a vault a pass, sent as JSON-RPC batches. The hourly read by owner adds one `getProgramAccounts` an owner. A read of many vaults by address in one call is not built on either reader (the Solana reader reads many internally; the EVM reader would share one block and one read of the settings). It is the first thing to build before many vaults or a shorter interval.
- **Storage.** A row of a three-asset vault measured about 1.8 KB on devnet, so 144 rows a day is about 0.3 MB a vault a day with its indexes, 9 MB a month. Fifty vaults would pass Supabase's free 500 MB in about five weeks. No thinning of old rows is built: a rule for it is a decision to take before the worker writes to the hosted database.

| Piece | Where (all free) | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Web | Vercel Hobby, one fixed origin | Terms say non-commercial (section 17) |
| API | Render free web service | Sleeps after 15 idle minutes and takes about a minute to wake. UptimeRobot's free plan pings `/health` every 5 minutes **[C 12]** |
| MCP | Render, its own web service (gate `MCP-TOOLS`) | Stateless; calls the API through the SDK, with no key of its own (`apps/mcp/README.md`) |
| Postgres | Supabase free | 500 MB. The free direct connection is IPv6 only; use the shared pooler in session mode, port 5432 **[C 12]**. The client's pool is set from the environment (`packages/db/src/pool.ts`): `DB_POOL_MAX`, and `DB_PGBOUNCER=transaction` for a pooler in transaction mode (port 6543), where the driver prepares no statements. The API's locks are transaction-scoped, so they hold in either mode. Data API off |
| Keeper | `keeper --loop`, run locally while testing; a small VM at deploy | A gas-only key, kept away from coding agents. It only has to work Mon to Fri, 14:30 to 20:00 UTC |
| Snapshot worker | `snapshot --loop`, run locally while testing; beside the keeper on its machine at deploy | No key of any kind: a node per chain and the database. Test networks only (gate `SNAPSHOT-WORKER`) |
| EVM collector | An hourly loop on the same machine | No chain keys; its own database role |
| Solana collectors | Rodrigo's Mac | Unchanged |

GitHub Actions is the later home for the keeper, not the MVP one. It needs Rodrigo's consent, one workflow on `main` that checks out a pinned commit of `basket`, an environment restricted to `main`, `--ignore-scripts` on install, and a test that a run from another branch cannot read the environment's secrets. Scheduled runs can also be delayed or dropped, and they stop after 60 days without repository activity **[C 16]**.

## 11. Web app

### Private vault conversation (Thom, Oct 7)

Opening an owned vault restores its private conversation beside its current measured holdings and chain targets. The model receives this context, the available asset catalog and sourced risk readings. It may explain the vault, ask a relevant question or propose an allocation with short reasons tied to the person's objective. This preview path uses the model's proposed weights, validated by code; it does not run the existing parameter allocator (`MODEL-LED-VAULT-CONVERSATION`). The original creation flow remains available while the new path is reviewed.

The strategy preview shows its objective, allocation changes, reasons, trade-offs and missing evidence. Sources remain inspectable. It is separate from current holdings and has no order, signing or applied-strategy action. Account, vault and chain changes invalidate late responses. Historical assistant text is display history, never execution authority; reopening requires current context and fresh validation for another proposal. Local history is labelled while server history's reviewed migration is pending. Legacy and shared-product vaults begin a private conversation without importing another creator's history.

Invest centres the conversation and strategy preview from the first turn, using the same compact scale as Analytics. The full owned-vault grid belongs on Portfolio; Invest offers a collapsed switcher with actual holdings and direct vault links. The new-goal conversation has no vault or holdings yet: its model context and response identify the selected chain, and unknown planning size stays unknown. The guided funding flow that sat beside it was removed on Oct 8 (Thom). A model preview becomes buyable only through a separately reviewed confirmation and model-plan contract; it must never be reshaped into an old engine plan with invented forecasts.

The compact Invest heading uses the shared sans `WORKSPACE_TITLE` register in the Invest conversation component, implementing Thom's requested Analytics-like workspace density. Other product headings retain `PAGE_TITLE`; the heading guard checks this exception by exact component and register. Plain words handed from the landing or a `#goal` fragment prefill Explore for an explicit send after sign-in, with owner isolation and no automatic model or financial action. A plan-way continuation the removed guided flow left in a tab (`tf-invest-way`) is read by nothing.

Products are public shared vault strategies (Thom, Oct 8). A person creates and refines their private vault in the conversation, then shares its confirmed target strategy from the vault. Publishing reads the actual owner/address/basket-bound targets, including cash, shows a frozen preview with the product name and description, and uses the existing explicit publish review and signature. It never initializes an unrelated token mix or silently changes weights to fit registry limits. Real-network target derivation requires a fresh chain read; a server-only fallback cannot prove it. A changed source strategy is shown for a new review before publishing. Existing recipes/signing contracts carry the reviewed strategy, not the private conversation; durable source-vault lineage is a separate API capability and is not claimed by a frontend source label.

**The contract.** Rodrigo's design system is on `main` and it is binding: `.design/branding/working-brand/patterns/STYLE.md`, the component specs beside it, and `working-brand.theme.json`. A screen that breaks one of its seven rules is wrong, however good it looks. In short: a provenance pin after every yield, price and FX figure; MOCK always as a hatch plus the word; the disclaimer from the one `DISCLAIMER` constant; no Japanese words or clichés; nothing like Teiten; no blue or violet; the brand recedes in a partner embed. Everything is square (2px radius, no shadows, no pills) except the typing box. Light and dark both ship. His voice rules apply to every string: answer, then reason, then risk, then action; "I" for the agent; no exclamation marks; MOCK is the only uppercase word.

Stack kept: Next 16.3.8, React 19.3.0, Tailwind 4.3.3. There is no UI library: tokens go into `globals.css` through Tailwind's `@theme`, with shadcn-compatible variable names, as his `token-mapping.md` lays out. Added: Privy, viem, TanStack Query, next-intl, `lucide-react` behind his one icon wrapper (the wrapper is built and draws its seven icons itself; the package is not installed yet), Playwright with axe. `cacheComponents` stays off. No Server Functions for writes; the Fastify API is the one backend. As built through WEB-1, TanStack Query, next-intl and Playwright are not installed (Playwright came with WEB-3, with `@axe-core/playwright`): the first screens keep their state in two providers (the wallet port, and the person with their chain), the dictionary is two plain modules, and the tests run in happy-dom. Each comes in with the first screen that needs it.

**His screens are extended, not replaced.** His specs already describe the product flow: the composer, the constraint sheet, the goal card, the plan legs, the exit-plan line, the execution list. They name the screens they belong to: home, the plan view and the monitor. So the web app is those three screens, rebuilt on his primitives, with the vault under them, plus the screens his system has no spec for. This replaces the earlier idea of parallel screens with his switched off.

Order of work:

1. **The design system is applied first.** Tokens, fonts and the primitives in `components/ui/` (button, field, card, data table, status mark, provenance pin, MOCK plate, explorer link, icon) come from his specs. Done by hand in BRAND-1 (Oct 3), for Rodrigo to review; his brand pipeline has not been run. What exists is under "The design system in the app" below.
2. **Route groups, as his embed spec requires:** `app/(app)/` for the product, `app/(marketing)/` for his landing page, and a bare `app/embed/[id]/`. The embed is his, stays, and is read-only. As built (WEB-1) there are two groups, each with its own root layout, and no layout above them: `app/(app)/` is the product on the design system, and `app/(structurer)/` holds every page written before it (home, the plan view, the monitor, `/risk/*` and the embed), moved as they were, with the layout they had. WEB-2 rebuilt home and the monitor in `app/(app)/` and deleted his two; WEB-BEARING rebuilt `/risk/*` as `/analytics/*` in `app/(app)/` (the old addresses redirect); `(structurer)` keeps the plan view, in English and with no wallet button; an address no route has is answered in the product's shell (`app/(app)/[...missing]`, `app/(app)/not-found.tsx`). The embed is rebuilt in a third group, `app/(embed)/`, with a bare root of its own (WEB-EMBED, below), and his `/embed/[id]` is gone. `app/(marketing)/` is his landing page at `/` (WEB-2b, below), with its own document: the three faces, the language, dark unless the visitor chose light, and no wallet, sign-in or API. The development pages are in `app/(app)/dev/`, under the product's layout: a layout of their own would exist only under `next dev`, and the route types Next writes for the dev server and for a build would then disagree, which fails the typecheck in a checkout that has run both.
3. **Then one agent per screen,** against the mock adapter.

- **Same for everyone** (the shelf, a shared portfolio's page, risk sheets): statically generated with `revalidate: 60` and a 3-second fetch timeout, so a slow or sleeping API serves the last good copy.
- **Per wallet** (plans, portfolio, vaults, orders, quotes): client components with TanStack Query. Every key includes owner and chain, which fixes the audit's stale-wallet bug.

| Screen | Route | Built from his specs | What we add, from his primitives |
|---|---|---|---|
| Goal (home) | `/`, `/fit/[slug]`. As built (WEB-2b): `/goal`; `/` is his landing page for everyone; its bar ends with the same account control as the product's (gate `ONE-ACCOUNT-CONTROL`, Thom, Oct 9) | Composer; constraint sheet with "Build my plan" blocked until valid; lattice loader | Sheet fields for the new inputs (amount in dollars, themes, holdings). The chain is not a field: it is the wallet's, and the sheet says which; per-field flags where the model and the rules parser disagree. `/fit/[slug]` is the same screen started from a shared portfolio |
| Plan | `/plans/[id]`. As built (WEB-3): `/plan/[id]`, the plan a goal built, before it is bought; `/plans/[id]` is his page until WEB-2 rebuilds it, and the two then become one | Goal card as header; plan legs; schedule chart; exit-plan panel; risk sheet table; disclaimer block; execution list | The vault panel: its limits in numbers, the auto-follow switch, pause state, withdraw. "Portfolio changed: rebalance" and "new asset: accept" prompts |
| Monitor | `/monitor` | Goal cards; drift table with "Out of band"; execution list | One row per plan, with its chain; a plan whose chain does not answer is shown as "unavailable"; the rebalance order. As built (WEB-2): a panel per vault from `GET /v1/portfolio`, read only (below) |
| Shelf | `/shelf` | Cards, status marks, pins | A card per shared portfolio: name, author address, platform badge, value following, the chains it is published on. A signed-in person is offered only those with a recipe on their chain |
| Shared portfolio | `/indexes/[slug]` | Plan legs, exit-plan line, Bearing heatmap tile, data table | The recipe of the person's own chain; versions and a pending version with its effective time; follow; "Start from this" leading to the goal screen |
| Order | `/orders/[id]` (built, WEB-3; the buy before it at `/plan/[id]/buy`) | Execution list; primary button that names the action and amount | Review (summary, minimum received, warnings, consents); funding missing on the order's chain; a status per leg; resume on reload; wrong wallet |
| Vault | `/vaults/[chain]/[address]` | Plan bar, data table, explorer links; for its owner the workbench with the conversation and the invest card | A read-only view of any vault, so a visitor with no funds sees real state. For its owner (gate `VAULT-PAGE-ACTIONS`, Oct 9) the page leads with Deposit and Withdraw, signed in place in its right pane, and changes go through the conversation |
| Publish | `/publish` | Fields, error summary | One simple form; limit errors from `previewPublish`; a leg per chain |
| Sign-in and notices | `/sign-in` (built, WEB-1); the notices on the order | Buttons, cards | Passkey or wallet; no chain is asked: a person starts on the chain they were looking at, or on their outside wallet's, and chooses the chain of a new plan on `/goal` (`CHAIN-SWITCH`, `CHAIN-AT-THE-PLAN`); before the first deposit, the terms and the "unaudited, team holds the keys" notice |
| Bearing analytics | `/analytics/[page]` (stocks, commodities, stablecoins, lending, simulation) and `/analytics/methodology`; `/risk`, `/risk/[asset]` and `/risk/methodology` redirect there (built, WEB-BEARING) | Rodrigo's Analytics 2.0 (`prototypes/hero-3d.html#analytics2`, `assets/analytics2.{js,css}`, `analytics-charts.js`); pins, data table, disclaimer | The section in the product's shell, with "Analytics" in the bar |

**The first product screens (WEB-1).** What exists, and what the next screens stand on. The files are under `apps/web/`.

- **The shell** (`components/shell/`). `AppDocument` is the whole document of a product route: `tf-app` on `<body>`, the three faces, then the dictionary, the wallet port and the person, then `AppShell`: a skip link, the bar (`AppNav`: the mark, the wordmark, "Goal", and "Sign in" or the wallet of the plan's chain with "Sign out"), the page at 1280px, and a foot with the disclaimer from the `DISCLAIMER` constant at body size, the language and the appearance. A product screen is a page under `app/(app)/` and gets all of it. The wallet adapter is not mounted on a product route: its button renders one thing on the server and another in the browser. The bar shows nothing about the person until the port has loaded, so both draw the same.
- **Light and dark, with no flash.** The choice is a cookie (`tf-theme`) read on the server, which writes `light` or `dark` on `<html>`; with no choice the class is `tf-auto` and one media query in `globals.css` gives the dark tokens when the system is dark. No script runs before paint (the lint bans `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`). `components/shell/theme.test.ts` holds the `tf-auto` block equal to `.dark`, token by token.
- **Languages.** `i18n/en.ts` and `i18n/pt.ts` hold every sentence of the product screens; the type of the first is the shape of the second. The language is a cookie (`tf-lang`), or the browser's `Accept-Language`, or English, read on the server (`i18n/server.ts`); screens read it with `useT()`. Chain names and MOCK are not translated.
- **Sign-in** (`/sign-in`; `features/wallet/SignIn.tsx`, `features/account/SignInScreen.tsx`). Two cards: a passkey, created or already had, and a wallet from the list of those found in the browser. Every failure is a sentence of the dictionary that says what to do (`features/wallet/sign-in-view.ts`); what Privy or a wallet threw is shown to nobody. With the API not answering, or the app set up wrongly, sign-in is off and says which, and the technical reason shows under `next dev` only. `?next=` is taken only as a plain path to one of the product's pages (`features/account/next-path.ts`): one slash, plain segments, no dot, backslash, escape, query or fragment. When the screen changes under the person (after a sign-in, the pick, "Ask again", a sign-out) focus goes to the heading while they wait and to the card that took the pressed button's place, and a status line says where they are; the bar puts focus on "Sign in" after a sign-out and says when a sign-out failed. Nothing moves when the page only loads.
- **The current chain and the switcher** (`features/account/`, gate `CHAIN-SWITCH`; since Oct 9, gate `CHAIN-AT-THE-PLAN`, the current chain is chosen on `/goal` beside the box, `ChainChoice.tsx` and `features/goal-conversation/GoalChain.tsx`, and the bar no longer shows or switches it: gate `ACCOUNT-IN-THE-BAR`). `AccountProvider` asks `GET /v1/me` once per person and gives every screen `useAccount()`: the account (`loading`, `signed-out`, `unknown` with why: the API did not answer, no longer knows the sign-in, asked for fewer requests, or every chain of theirs is switched off; nothing is assumed; `no-wallet`; or `ready` with the current chain and the chains a wallet of theirs signs on), `chain` (the current chain signed in, the chain someone signed out is looking at) and `choose`. Signed out, the chain is the address's (`?chain=robinhood`), else this browser's (`tf-chain` in local storage), else Solana. A person with wallets and no chain yet is not asked: they start on the chain they were looking at, if a wallet of theirs signs there and the server runs it, and it is stored with `PUT /v1/me/chain`; never while a wallet a passkey owes is still to be made (`port.walletsOwed`). Signed in, the bar's right side is one account control (`AccountMenu`; Thom, Oct 6 and, for what it shows, Oct 9, gate `ACCOUNT-IN-THE-BAR`): a button with a wallet glyph and "Account", of one width, named "Your account" for a screen reader, opening a disclosure with the person's wallets, one row for each chain they have a wallet on: the chain's name with how it is run where it is not live (the words "Test network" or "Sample", no glyph), the short address with the copy icon beside it (`CopyButton`, named "Copy address": it copies the whole address, turns into a tick and says "Copied"), "View on" the chain's explorer where the network has one; then "Sign out" as a plain last item. The phone's sheet opens with the same block. Nothing in the bar shows or switches a chain, signed in or out. A choice of where new plans start is made on `/goal`: signed in it is stored by the API, signed out it is kept in the browser; a chain no wallet of the person's signs on and one the server has switched off are not offered there, and a choice that was not stored says why. The shelf shows the current or browsing chain and names it in the address. A plan's screens and its buy use the plan's own chain (`use-plan.ts`); a plan on a chain no wallet of theirs signs on is not offered for buying. The monitor reads every chain the person holds a wallet for, and the activity every chain's orders. A shared portfolio's page signs on the current chain only: when a vault of the person's on another chain follows it, the page says so and offers the switch to that chain. A start chain the API refuses (409 or 422) is said as such, with sign-out as the way on, not as a server that did not answer. The throwaway wallet of development has no account on the API: its chain is worked out in the page, under the MOCK plate.
- **The goal screen** (`/goal`, the product's first screen; `features/goal/`). The composer, example chips that fill it and send nothing, then the goal card as a draft, the limits (`ConstraintSheet`), and what came of "Build my plan".
  - Reading: `POST /goals` with `{ text, language }`, the one parser the API on `staging` has. It was made for goals in reais, so `read-goal.ts` maps what it found into a `BasketSheetDraft` and leaves the rest empty: it never turns reais into dollars, and the screen says the reader can miss a dollar amount or a date. Before the text is sent, `pre-read.ts` reads its own words in English and Portuguese (a dollar amount, "18 months", "2 years", "by 2031", protect, grow or income, and low, medium or high risk); after the answer they fill only what the reader left empty or answered with a default of its own (its "accumulation" and "medium"), and the sheet's source line adds "filled from your words". An amount in reais is left for the person (USD-ONLY). The country, which the schema needs, starts as the one the browser's language names ("pt-BR" is Brazil) when nothing else says it, and its hint says so until the person changes it. The example chips are not sent at all (`examples.ts`). When the API gets a parser for the vault sheet, `read-goal.ts` and `pre-read.ts` change.
  - Limits: the fields of `BasketSheet` (3.6) that a person states, plus the amount. The chain is not a field: the sheet shows it as a fact, with the sample glyph and "test network" when it is one, and "Not set" with the way to sign in when there is none. `checkSheet` parses with the shared schema; "Build my plan" is handed only what it parsed, and is blocked while a field does not fit, the person is signed out, or the chain is not known. An amount reads the same in both languages: `40,000` and `40.000` are forty thousand; a space inside it or a stray sign is not a number. A field with nothing in it is "still missing", a wrong value "doesn't fit". The text of a goal is held to the reader's own bounds (3 to 2,000 characters), each with its sentence.
  - Building: one function, `buildPlan(apiFetch, sheet)` in `build-plan.ts`: `POST /v1/baskets/personalize` with `{ sheet: BasketSheet }`, expecting `{ id, proposal: BasketProposal }`. The route is built (ENG-2, section 7); a built plan is kept in the tab and "See your plan" leads to `/plan/[id]` (WEB-3). Where an API does not serve it, it answers 404 and the screen says "Your limits are set. The plan can't be built yet." and shows no plan in its place. 429 is "slow down", a 4xx with `GOAL_NOT_ACHIEVABLE` is the sheet's "no plan fits", another 4xx sends the person back to the fields. A 401 or 403 says to sign in again and a 409 that the chain is chosen first. An answer is shown only for the limits it was asked for: no other goal is read while a plan is built, and an answer is dropped when the limits or the person changed meanwhile, or when the plan is for another goal, amount or chain. A built plan is labelled by its own figures (`planProvenance`): live only when every one is, the plate with "test network" for a test network, the plate alone otherwise, and never live when it names none. What was typed and read is kept in the tab (`sessionStorage`), so signing in and coming back loses nothing; it is forgotten when the person signs out or another signs in.
- **No signing** (until WEB-3, below, whose order runner is the one way in). There is no buy button, and no screen can reach a key. The port a screen gets from `useWalletPort()` has no `sign`, `send`, `signMessage` or `exportKey`: the object does not carry them. The whole port is `useSigningPort()` in `features/wallet/signing.ts`, which nothing the app ships imports yet. `components/shell/product-routes.test.ts` reads what every product route is built from and fails when a file outside the wallet's seam (nine files it lists) imports the whole port, takes anything from a seam file but the three hooks and types, imports a package that is not on its list (no wallet or chain library is, and `@privy-io` is named in one file), or so much as names a signing member, a wallet's own signing method or `window.ethereum`. It also fails on an import from a `dev`, `test` or `fixtures` folder, and on any path to the wallet adapter. The first screen that signs adds itself to that test's list on purpose, after the guard exists.
- **His pages.** Moved, not changed, with small exceptions, so that a route the API does not serve is a sentence and not a raw error. Home and the monitor were rebuilt by WEB-2 and his two files deleted with `components/GoalFlow.tsx`, `components/StatsCard.tsx` and `lib/missing-route.ts`, which only they used.
- **What the primitives gained.** `ConstraintSheet` takes `facts` (rows it states and does not edit, such as the chain), an `id` and the `level` of its title, points the blocked button at the sentence that explains it, and says a field with nothing in it is missing where a wrong value does not fit. `Card` takes `mockLabels`: what a screen reader hears after MOCK, in the language of the view, and a note under the plate ("test network"). `Composer` takes `maxLength`, and its label key `send` is `submit`, so that no file a screen is built from names a signing member. `labels.ts` has `edited`, `mockAnnounce` and the missing and fill sentences. `components/shell/ChoiceChip.tsx` is a chip with `aria-pressed` and a check mark when chosen, for the chain, the language and the appearance: colour alone does not say which is on. Each product page has its own title and description (`components/shell/metadata.ts`). `scripts/check-build.mjs` calls development-only every `dev`, `test` and `fixtures` folder of the app at any depth, and no package's.

**The plan, buy and order screens (WEB-3).** Under `apps/web/features/order/`, on the primitives, in both languages.

- **The plan** (`/plan/[id]`, `PlanScreen.tsx`). A plan is kept on the server with the person who made it (API-PLANS, Oct 6): `GET /v1/baskets/{id}` answers it to that person, and never to another (404, as for an id that names nothing); a plan made from a link is still anybody's to read while the agent surface is on, and the answer says which it is (`fromLink`). "Build my plan" keeps what it answered in the tab, under the plan's id and for the person who built it (`plan-store.ts`), as a cache: a tab that did not build the plan (a new one, another device, a sign-in again) reads it from the API (`use-plan.ts`). The risk roll-up is not stored, so a plan read back shows none; the goal screen's "Your plan is built." links to it. The goal as the heading; what the plan holds, with the bar when it has four parts or fewer (each with its reason) and a table of every line; the projected range with the pin of the plan's first yield observation; the exit plan with the cost pinned to its liquidity observation, or "not measured"; the income verdict; the flags; the risk roll-up as the API sent it (`rollUp` beside the plan in the personalize answer, optional, never worked out here; without it the screen says none was sent); the MOCK plate with "test network" on a test network. The disclaimer is the shell's foot, once per page. A plan for another chain than the person's is not offered for buying (`ONE-CHAIN`). Built against the shape `build-plan.ts` reads and a typed fixture: the personalize route (ENG-2) was not on `staging` when this was written.
- **Buy** (`/plan/[id]/buy`, `BuyScreen.tsx`). Since Oct 7 one card with one press (`InvestCard.tsx`, gate `INVEST-ONE-PRESS`): the amount, funds only when the wallet is short, the trust notice the first time, the order with each step's minimum, and "Deposit $X" ("Invest $X" until `WORDS-VAULT-DEPOSIT`, Oct 9), which runs the steps in the card; the same card deposits into a shared portfolio's vault, adds money to a vault, and is what the pane of `/goal` mounts once a deposit's review is confirmed (`DEPOSIT-IN-PLACE`), so `/goal` no longer leads here. Before that, from Oct 6, four steps on one card, one open at a time (`BuySteps.tsx`, gate `BUY-STEPS`, removed): Amount, Funds, Trust, Sign, with a progress strip that marks each done and a "Continue" that opens the next and moves the focus to it. Funds, for a short wallet, says "Insufficient balance." and which token to add, with no figure ("Add USDC to your wallet to deposit.", then "Add a little SOL for network fees." where the fee token is short; Thom, Oct 12), beside the wallet's address on that chain with the account menu's copy control (on the sample chain, the heading and the sample-cash button alone, since nothing can be sent there); a wallet that holds enough reads "Your wallet has what this deposit needs.", without amounts; the table and its source lines stay behind "Show the details"; on a test network with a faucet key, "Get test funds" asks `POST /v1/testnet/fund` and reads the wallet again. Trust shows the four points in short and the whole notice behind "Read the full list". What each step holds: the amount (the plan's by default, $10 to $1,000,000, read as the goal screen reads one); `GET /v1/funding` for that amount, plan and wallet, read again a moment after the amount stops changing, with each balance pinned and the address to fund when something is missing (on the mock, a button that funds the mock wallet); the trust notice from `TRUST_STATUS` (`packages/schemas/src/trust.ts`, section 13: unaudited, the team's upgrade keys and the admin address, the keeper's limits in numbers from the deploy's record, issuers, the United States, a lost passkey), ticked once per person and version of the text and kept in this browser with its time until the consent route exists; then one primary button, "Review the steps to buy $X", off with its reasons until all is there. It makes the order (`POST /v1/orders`) and keeps the plan's id and lines beside it in local storage (`order-record.ts`). Nothing is signed on this screen.
- **The order** (`/orders/[id]`, `OrderScreen.tsx`). The review: the deposit, each step with what it spends and, per trade, the least it receives (in whole units with each token's symbol, read from the deploy's committed record (`features/order/units.ts`), never from the API; a token with no committed units, as on the mock, in its smallest units) and how far that is under the quote; the server's warnings; a box for each consent the order needs. "Sign and deposit $X" keeps that order with the consents, then runs it through the order runner (`run-order.ts`), the one file the app ships that holds the whole wallet port, which hands it to `execute()` with what `features/wallet/README.md` item 3 lists. Each step shows its status from the API, the phase the executor reports while it runs (building, checking it against the review, signing, sending, waiting for the chain) and its explorer link once it has one; the status line is an `aria-live` region; each answer of the executor gets its sentence and at most one next action (`order-view.ts`): run again after `cancelled`, `waiting` or `error`, approve one step again after `needs_review`, a new order after `refused`, `failed` or `expired`, the other order after `blocked`. A refused step names the check that failed. A reload reads the kept order and offers to continue; the API's later answers are shown, never signed from. An order made in another browser is shown and not run: its plan is not here to check against.
- **The look, from his references** (Thom, Oct 5: the screens match Rodrigo's mockups). The plan is the plan pane of the showcase case (`goal-showcase-case.md`, `prototypes/hero-3d.html`): a head with the title and a muted sub-line and one MOCK plate for the whole plan, the limits as mono chips, the figures as stat cells joined by hairlines (`card.md`), the legs as `plan-leg.md`'s bar and label list with each reason under its leg, the exit line, and a foot with "sample data, not live" or "test network, not live" and `DISCLAIMER_SHORT`; the goal sentence above it in the serif, as the goal card's header. The buy screen was the guide's "Controls" panel beside the trust notice; since Oct 6 it is one card of four steps (gate `BUY-STEPS`), the funding table and its source lines behind a disclosure in the funds step, and one quiet line at the top of the card the only word that its figures are samples or the test network's: no MOCK plate on this screen. The order is a card of stat cells and an execution list (`data-table.md`): step, verb, amount, status word, `Tx ↗` with the shortened signature and a copy button. Where they differ, and why: no photograph or quote column (the person's own goal is the heading); no chart (the plan carries no schedule to draw); the legs are the plan's assets, not its sleeves, because the plan does not say which sleeve an asset is in (more than four assets fall back to the table); a leg has no yield, since the plan carries none per asset (a dash, as `plan-leg.md` draws cash); the balances in the funding table carry no pin (they are not prices), their source is the footer line. One primitive changed: a MOCK exit tier that wraps grew past its fixed height and overlapped the next line (`ExitPlanLine.tsx`, `h-5` to `min-h-5`). `apps/web/e2e/buy.spec.ts` photographs each screen at 375 and 1280 px in light and dark, and the guide's components and provenance sections and the prototype's plan pane at the same widths, into `SCREENSHOTS_DIR`.
- **A trade's route is read (L1 of the Oct 9 review).** On EVM the guard opens the call data each swap hands its router and holds it to the one route the deployment states for that router (`routes` in the deployment file): Universal Router's `execute` with one `V4_SWAP` of `SWAP_EXACT_IN_SINGLE`, `SETTLE_ALL`, `TAKE_ALL`, in the pool of the trade's two tokens at the fee and tick spacing the deployment records for the traded asset, no hooks, the trade's amount and deadline; refusal `route`. One pool per asset, against the cash (since Oct 12): the deploy record states it for the builder (`assets[].pool` in `deployments/<chain>-<network>.json`), the guard's file states it for the guard (`assets[id].pool`), `tests/evm-vault/record.test.ts` holds the two to each other, and a trade of two assets or of an asset with no pool recorded is refused by both. `packages/sdk/deployments/mainnet.json` is a placeholder the loader refuses.
- **Two chains.** A chain is bought on only with a deployment committed for its network in `packages/sdk/deployments/`: on the test network Solana and, since WEB-RH-BUY, Robinhood Chain (46630). A chain with none (Base, mainnet) is not ready, and the plan, buy and order screens say so plainly. On Robinhood Chain a buy is an approval of the deposit to the plan's vault, then a create that deposits and trades; its explorer links are built from the web's own chain table. A shared portfolio is published on Robinhood Chain as on Solana, since AGT-4: the shelf offers publish, and the form is shown, only where the chain's deployment names its registry (`publishableOn` in `features/order/readiness.ts`); where it names none, the shelf says publishing there is coming and the form shows that notice alone.
- **Checks.** `features/order/order.test.ts` (amounts, targets, what the browser keeps, which chains can be signed on, the API's answers, a sentence for every answer of the executor), `order-screen.events.test.ts` (what the executor is handed, a refused step with its reason, `needs_review` asked again and handed back once, the wallet's no, a revert, a second tab that runs nothing, a browser with no locks, a reload that runs the approved order whatever the API says now, a Robinhood Chain buy reviewed and handed over, a chain with no deployment not ready; `execute` itself is a double there), `plan-buy.events.test.ts`, and rule 3 of `components/shell/product-routes.test.ts`, which admits the runner and nothing else. In a browser, `apps/web/e2e/buy.spec.ts` (Playwright, `pnpm --filter @colosseum/web e2e`, its own CI job): sign in with the throwaway wallet, read a goal, build the plan, buy it, review and sign, against a stub of the API on the mock chain (`tests/e2e/stub-api.ts`, the SDK's double of the order routes), with the real guard on the mock's bytes until every step is confirmed; and a second buy in which the stub lowers one swap's minimum and the guard refuses it. `buy-robinhood.spec.ts` runs the stub's mock as Robinhood Chain (`E2E_CHAIN=robinhood`, its own CI step): an approval, then a create that buys, both confirmed. Each screen is checked with axe at 375 px in light and dark, and for no sideways scroll.

**Home and the monitor (WEB-2).** Under `apps/web/features/portfolio/`, on the primitives, in both languages. Nothing on either signs.

- **The goal page** (`/goal`, `app/(app)/goal/page.tsx`; it was `/` from WEB-2 until his landing page took `/` in WEB-2b). The goal screen first, followed by a compact product-style card collection for a person who already holds vaults (Thom, Oct 7 screenshot follow-up). Each card names its vault (stored name, joined goal where available, else shortened address), its chain, its individual measured value with its pin, and one Open vault link to the existing vault detail. The compact heading links to the full portfolio. Values are never added across chains in this entry; unavailable figures are not invented. Each card keeps its own sample/test provenance. This entry uses the existing owned portfolio read while Rodrigo's PORT-3 overview and analytics integration are pending. A visitor, a person with no vault, and an API that cannot say all get the goal alone. Before a goal is read the screen follows his prototype's "Tell us what your money needs to do" (`hero-3d.html`, `#simulate`): on a wide screen the question sits beside the typing box (five columns and seven, 48px apart), and a phone stacks them; the question is `text-h2`, the lead `text-body` and muted, the box's label is read to a screen reader only (`composer.md`), the placeholder is his ("Describe your goal… an amount, a date, and how fast you might need it back."), the keyboard hint is under the chips in the mono face (the box is still described by it, through `Composer`'s `describedBy`), and a visitor gets his last line, pointed at sign-in ("Like how it reads? Sign in for a plan on the chain of your wallet, with every number sourced."). Readings, his to reopen: no type token is his 41.6px at 1440 or 28.8px at 375; `text-h2` (38px and 31px there) is the nearest at both, and holds two lines as his does, so no size of its own was added. His lead measures 16px, which is `text-body`. The box opens at one line and grows to five, as `composer.md` says ("Auto-grows from 1 to 5 lines", `rows={1}`), where the prototype's `composer-lg` opens at three: on a phone his longer placeholder fills three lines anyway. The hint keeps the app's words ("Enter to read it · Shift+Enter for a new line"), since nothing here is a simulation; the uppercase eyebrow and the dark ground stay out (STYLE.md: uppercase is MOCK's alone; the app is light by default, `token-mapping.md`). Sign-in leads to `/goal` by default, and `?next=` takes `/goal` and `/monitor`, not `/`. The bar's links are "Goal" (`/goal`) and "Portfolio" (`/monitor`); the mark leads to `/`. The monitor's panel takes his case's look too (WEB-2b): the vault's facts as chips (address, version, auto-follow), the value line, then a bar of its parts by weight with each part's weight and target (four at most, as `plan-leg.md` allows; more is the table alone), the table, and a foot with the read's time and `DISCLAIMER_SHORT`.
- **The monitor** (`/monitor`, `MonitorScreen.tsx`). `GET /v1/portfolio` once the person is known, again on "Read again"; an answer is shown only for the person it was asked for, and a change of the chain new plans start on reads nothing again (`use-portfolio.ts`, gate `CHAIN-AT-THE-PLAN`). One serif line ("What your vault holds."), the chain with its plate, then a panel per vault (`VaultPanel.tsx`): its address cut, the value with its pin, cash, auto-follow on or off, the keeper's losses of the last seven days as a share, a version of the followed portfolio still to come with its time and new assets, and a table of holdings (asset, amount, price and value each pinned, weight, target, drift with a true minus), then the time it was read. The disclaimer sits under the panels (`disclaimer-block.md`: "Monitor, under the plan summary card"), and only there: the shell's foot does not draw its own when the page has one (a `:has()` rule in `AppShell.tsx`; the e2e spec counts one visible block on the monitor and on home). A vault that is not live is a mocked card, with "test network" under the plate on a test network; the chain line takes the API's label for the chain it read. Every vault's goal card and panel carry its chain's badge; vaults on more than one chain are grouped under a heading per chain, each with its vaults' total and its pin, and one total labelled "across both chains" (`CHAIN-EVERYWHERE`).
  - What each pin is handed (`portfolio.ts`): a price, its own source, time, method and age (`pinSourceOfPrice`); a holding's value, its price's source and time with the method saying it was multiplied by the amount; the vault's value, the sources of every price it stands on, the oldest of their times and the read's, and the age of the stalest price when one is stale. A label is live only when the vault and every price under it are.
  - Where the API cannot say: signed out (the way to sign in, nothing asked), no chain yet (the way to choose), no vault on any chain ("You have no vault yet", the way back to the goal; nothing is said of one empty chain while another holds vaults), the route missing (404: "This server can't read vaults yet", no holding drawn), the chain switched off or not answering (503, or a 409 with a chain code: "Unavailable" with the watch mark, since nothing is known to be wrong, and "Read again"), 401 or 403 (sign in again; for the throwaway wallet, which a real API does not know, its own sentence), 429, unreachable, and an answer that is not the frozen shape, names a chain twice or files a vault under a chain it is not on.
  - Readings taken, his to reopen: the monitor's header is the serif line, not a goal card, because a vault read carries no goal sentence; drift has no "Out of band" status, because the band is the chain's setting and is not in the read; the vault's switches (auto-follow, withdraw) and the rebalance are not offered, because no screen signs here; assets are named by their id after the chain (`solana:usdy` is USDY), because the read names no symbols, except Robinhood Chain's dollar, which is tUSDG also where the mock's id says `usdc`; his stats card ("Live on mainnet", the first structurer's counts) and his list of everyone's recent plans are not on the new home, which shows the person's own vault in their place.

**Bearing's analytics (WEB-BEARING).** Rodrigo's Analytics 2.0 rebuilt in the app (Thom, Oct 5: the app looks like his mockups), under `apps/web/features/bearing/`, in the person's language: every sentence is in `i18n/bearing/en.ts` and `i18n/bearing/pt.ts`, a dictionary of its own so the other routes do not ship it (his words, as he wrote them, are the English), and the chart primitives take their words as `labels`. Figures and dates follow the language through the app's `LOCALE` and its `utc` date (`fmtFor` in `format.ts`, `useFmt` in `parts.tsx`): his $2.6M, 6.82% and 2026-10-03 15:07 UTC in English, US$ 2,6 mi, 6,82% and 3 de out. de 2026, 15:07 UTC in Portuguese. The sources and methods in a pin's popover are the API's, in English, with ISO times, in both. `locale.test.ts` fails on any file of the section or its routes that sets a language or a locale of its own.

- **Pages.** `/analytics/[page]`: stocks, commodities, stablecoins, lending and simulation behind his retractable side menu (220px, a 56px rail, a drawer under 900px; the choice is kept in `localStorage` as `tf-an2-side`), and `/analytics/methodology` (the old `/risk/methodology` text). Each page has his counters, pie, chart with its metric selector and 24 h / 7 d / 30 d ranges, table and copy; filters, metric, range, the lending tolerance and the simulated sale are kept while a person moves between pages (`BearingProvider` in the section's layout). `/risk` leads to `/analytics/stocks`, `/risk/<asset>` to the stocks or commodities page filtered to that asset, `/risk/methodology` to the methodology (`next.config.ts`). The section takes the whole width of the shell (`AppShell`: `has-[[data-ui=bearing]]:max-w-none`).
- **Data.** Read in the browser from the risk routes the prototype reads, on `NEXT_PUBLIC_RISK_API_URL` or else `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` (apps/api mounts `/risk/*`): `/risk/assets`, `/risk/pools` (also per asset), `/risk/pools/recorded`, `/risk/pools/:address/liquidity` and `/liquidity/history`, `/risk/facts/assets/:id`, `/risk/assets/:id/history` and `/split`, `/risk/facts/lending` with `/:account` and `/history`, `/risk/recoverable`, `/risk/facts/methodology`. Every route exists; a failed read is shown as its reason, never as a figure. Derived figures (sums, the covered share, the best path) are worked out in the browser from served facts, each with a method that names its parts, and a sum short of a part is a lower bound ("≥").
- **Per chain (WEB-BEARING-CHAINS).** A Solana / Robinhood Chain toggle at the top of every page reads every figure for one chain. The chain is named in the address (`?chain=robinhood`, which the side menu's links and the page's own links keep); with none named it is the one chosen on these pages last (`tf-bearing-chain` in local storage), else Solana. It is the pages' own filter (gate `CHAIN-AT-THE-PLAN`): it neither follows nor sets the chain a person's new plans start on, signed in or out (`chain.ts`, `BearingProvider.tsx`). Nothing is read before the chain is known. The toggle is the one place the chain is named (gate `CHAIN-EVERYWHERE`, amended): no counter, card or table row carries a `ChainBadge`; every pin's popover still names the chain. Solana's reads are the prototype's addresses, unchanged; Robinhood Chain's name it: `GET /risk/assets?chain=robinhood` (the seeded `robinhood:<sym>` rows with the EVM collector's `evmq-0.1` curves, pool TVL null with its reason, since its pools are not in the registry, PLAN-UNIVERSE RU.14 and DU6) and `GET /risk/pools?chain=robinhood` (empty, with the reason); a Robinhood stock's own routes are read by its address, which `resolveAsset` now finds in any case, and its curves under their own version (`curveVersionOf`), the recoverable value with no xStocks issuer route. Lending and stablecoins say "Not collected yet on Robinhood Chain" and read nothing; any figure Robinhood Chain lacks says "not collected yet", with no chain's name and once per card. The stocks page ends with **the chains side by side** (`ChainsSide.tsx`, `GET /risk/chains`): assets tracked, pool TVL, exit capacity at ≤ 1% cost in the time of week it is now, and 24 h volume from `risk_pool_flow`, per chain, each row headed by its chain's `ChainBadge` (the one place the page mixes chains), each figure with its pin or the reason it is missing. The plan inputs match an EVM curve to a shelf address in any case (`curveKey` in `curve-version.ts`; the shelf holds lower case, the collector mixed). The tests and the e2e stub read Robinhood Chain from a fixture (`fixtures/risk/bearing-robinhood.json`) whose every figure is labelled `fixture`, so it shows with the MOCK plate.
- **Volume 24 h.** Bearing's own, from the swap history (`flow.byWindow` of an asset's sheet), where it is collected; where it is not, DexScreener's 24 h volume of the selected pools (`discoveryVolume24hUsd` of `/risk/pools`, read when each pool was registered), under its own source, "DexScreener · 24 h", with a method that says it is DexScreener's figure and not Bearing's, and the counter's note saying so (`dexVolume` and `assetVol` in `dex.ts`, gate `VOLUME-DEXSCREENER`). A pool DexScreener gave no figure makes the sum a lower bound.
- **Responsive figures (BEARING-MISSING-NOT-ZERO, Oct 7).** A counter has the same responsive basis and reserved label, figure and note regions before and after a read, so the waiting row keeps its box. The figure number stays together; its partial qualifier, stale metadata and note can wrap at readable boundaries. Both languages retain the complete readings and missing-data explanation, with no document overflow at 375 px. The existing loading-box and EN/PT lending browser checks pass unchanged, including light/dark axe.
- **Live or stale.** The risk API states no staleness of its own, so the section states it, as his snapshot shows it: when the collectors' newest reading (the latest end of any capacity curve) is more than two hours old, the banner says every figure is stale, and every pin is hollow with "stale" and that figure's own age (`fact.ts`, `STALE_AFTER_MS`); otherwise the pins are solid and the banner says "Newest reading from the collectors: …" (it said "Live from the collectors, as of …" until `WORDS-ONE-NAME`: "live" over figures marked stale read as a contradiction). A figure whose own reading is older than two hours is stale on a live page too (SPYx's volume 24 h, read 52 hours before his recording, is). An open page looks at its clock every minute, so it turns stale while it is open. A figure the API marks other than `live` keeps its mark (MOCK or test network); every measured figure is live. When the API does not answer after three tries a second apart, the banner says so and every figure waits for it; the page asks again every minute and reads everything afresh when it answers. A failed read is never kept. His snapshot is never read by the app: it is the fixture of the tests and of the e2e stub (`tests/e2e/stub-risk.ts`).
- **Charts.** `components/ui/TimeChart.tsx` (panes on one time axis, value axis on the right, crosshair and readout by mouse, finger and arrow keys, range tabs), `DistChart.tsx` (liquidity by price band, zoom), `Sparkline.tsx`, with `chart.ts` and `chart-scale.ts`; the colours are the `--tf-bearing-*` tokens in `globals.css`, his values from `analytics2.css`. The pie and the simulation's flow are Bearing's own (`parts.tsx`, `Flow.tsx`).
- **The heatmap tile** (`components/ui/HeatmapTile.tsx`, `heatmap.ts`; `bearing-heatmap-tile.md`). A primitive: 7 days by 24 hours as a table drawn on a CSS grid with `role="grid"`, one tab stop, the arrow keys, Home and End through the hours, the hour read out under the grid with its pinned figure; the five `--tf-heat` steps at the quintiles of the hours shown (more depth lighter on warm black, darker on paper); an hour with no sample is the ground with an en dash, never hatched; a stale tile has the hatch band and "stale · N h", a MOCK one the band and MOCK; "View as table" gives the days as rows, which can be put deepest first. Cells are at least 24px (WCAG 2.5.8), so on a phone the grid scrolls inside the tile. Bearing shows it when one asset is selected on the stocks or commodities page (commodities always, as it holds gold alone; `/risk/<asset>` leads there): that asset's median sell cost by Eastern hour at $50,000 (`GET /risk/assets/:id/heatmap`; the hourly medians exist only at the curve's grid sizes, and $50,000 is the nearest to the pages' $100,000), timed by the asset's newest capacity reading since the route gives no time. It is in the showcase (`/dev/ui`).
- **Not built.** His first analytics view (`#analytics`, the per-asset sheet with candles, depth chart and heatmap) and its `?preview=1` MOCK distribution; an asset in the stocks table opens the simulation for it instead. The depth and grouped-column charts of `analytics-charts.js` are not ported, as no page here uses them.
- **Checks.** `features/bearing/bearing.test.ts` (his hand checks on his recording: covered 6.82%, $2.57M, 52.57% for one pool, TSLAx $100k as two hourly sales losing $552; the regime rule and the calendar against the API's fixture; staleness), `bearing.events.test.ts` (filters, side menu, tolerance, the chart's keys, every figure pinned and stale, nothing MOCK), `components/ui/heatmap.test.ts` and `HeatmapTile.events.test.ts` (the ramp, the keys, the no-sample cell, stale and MOCK, the table), and `e2e/bearing.spec.ts` (every page with axe at 375 px in light and dark, no sideways scroll at 375 and 1440, the redirects).

**His landing page (WEB-2b, then `LANDING-HERO`).** `/` (`app/(marketing)/`, `features/landing/`) is the hero alone, as in the first demonstration of `IDENTITY-2` (Rodrigo, Oct 8), in both languages and both grounds. There is no foot. The 3D joint, the showcase, the typing box and the closing of `hero-3d.html` are gone from it (gates `JOINT-3D`, `PLAN-JOINT` and `CLOSING-COINS` superseded).

- **Who gets it.** Everyone: the logo leads here from the app too (Thom, Oct 6, gate `LANDING-SIGNED-IN`; until then a person signed in was sent on to `/goal`). The product sets a cookie (`tf-in`, `SIGNED_IN_COOKIE`) while someone is signed in and clears it when they sign out, and the landing page reads it on the server: for that person the bar's account control starts as the chip's placeholder and loads their wallet at once, where a visitor's is "Sign in" with no wallet loaded until it is pressed (gate `ONE-ACCOUNT-CONTROL`). The cookie holds no id and proves nothing; the API checks every sign-in. His flow's "once connected the way in is the app" (`compact-nav.md`, "Wallet connected") is kept by "Plans" and the hero's "Start a plan".
- **The bar** (`LandingBar.tsx`): static, full width, on the ground with a hairline; the face and the wordmark home, Plans (`/goal`), Bearing (`/analytics/stocks`), Docs (the API's `/docs`), and the one action, the account control (`LandingAccount.tsx`; gate `ONE-ACCOUNT-CONTROL`): "Sign in" (`/sign-in`, opened as the dialog over the landing, which is not left), outlined beside the hero's filled action, or the account chip with its menu for a person signed in.
- **The hero** (`Landing.tsx`): the line "No product fits everyone. So we make the products fit your goals." (Rodrigo, Oct 8; it replaced "Tell it the goal. Get the portfolio cut for it.") in Inter Tight 600, its lead, "Start a plan" (`/goal`, honey) and "See a plan" (`/shelf`). Behind it the honey fog (`components/shell/HoneyFog.tsx`, also behind Bearing's page head): one WebGL2 context with an ambient fog (domain-warped noise under a halo that drifts round the upper right) and a small stable-fluids simulation the pointer stirs, as a chroma halo but slower and softer; with no pointer an invisible cursor wanders and stirs it. Smoke in honey on night, deeper honey ink on paper (stronger there, `LOOK`). Half resolution, paused when hidden or off screen, the ambient fog alone without float targets, one still frame under reduced motion, nothing without WebGL2.
- **The numbers** (`stats.ts`): read on the server from the API's `/stats` within 1.5 s (plans made, wallets with a confirmed transaction, the value last observed in their vaults), each pinned to that route and its `asOf`; `live` on mainnet, `sandbox` with "Test network" and the hatch elsewhere. When the API does not answer, the sample strip of the identity sheet, pinned `mock`, with the hatch and "Sample figures". Live numbers carry no change figure: the API keeps no history.
- **The plan pane** (WEB-3's `/plan/[id]`) gains his case's chart (`features/order/PlanChart.tsx`): the balance the plan's dollar yield projects over its term, from the engine's own range (`card.expectedReturn`, low to high a year), drawn dashed as a band, with the two end figures under it on the pin of the plan's yield observation and a line saying stocks and gold are not projected. With no yield observation, or `yield_not_read`, or a range of zero, there is no chart. An income plan pays its yield out each month, so its balance does not grow: it gets no chart, and the line says what the yield pays over the term in all, on the same pin.
- **Readings, his to reopen:** marketing is dark unless the visitor chose light (`token-mapping.md` section 6); the appearance is one icon in the top bar (`ThemeToggle`, gate `THEME-TOGGLE`): the sun on a dark page and the moon on a light one, drawn by the `drawn-dark` variant from the server's class; a press flips what is drawn and stores `light` or `dark`.
