There is no foot of switches. The eyebrows are sentence case, the compact bar is solid (no blur), the prototype tag is gone, the pane foot says `DISCLAIMER_SHORT` where his says "Simulation. Not investment advice.", and the cases show the plan drawn as a joint where his show photographs (`PLAN-JOINT`): each is a delta his specs already list. The CTA is "Sign in", the app's word, where his says "Connect wallet". Every pinned figure in a case carries the pin's MOCK plate, so a figure and its unit can take two lines in a cell where his hatched dot takes one. The typing box opens at one line (`composer.md`), his at three. The heading of the closing is the primitive's `text-h2`. The stage's joint is a joiner's drawing of one pinned through-tenon (gate `JOINT-3D`), not his three textured members; `joint-stage.md` says so in its opening line and its fallback table. With reduced motion the seated drawing stands alone, where his table shows the drawing in two states (exploded for the hero and steps 01–02, seated from step 03); the stills cross from apart to seated only in the pinned layer, without WebGL or with data saved.

**His bar, goal card and activity in the product (WEB-2b).**

- **The bar** of every product page is his compact bar (`CompactNav`, compact from the start, fixed, solid, with the skip link first), with his items on the product's routes: Products (`/shelf`, the shelf of shared portfolios, current on a portfolio's page and on publish too; WEB-4), Invest (`/goal`, current on the plan and order pages too), Portfolio (`/monitor`, for someone signed in), Analytics (`/analytics/stocks`, Bearing's analytics, current on every page under `/analytics`, the methodology too: there is no Resources item, Thom, Oct 6, and the methodology is linked from Bearing's side menu) and the account control, one control in three states on every page with a bar (gate `ONE-ACCOUNT-CONTROL`, Thom, Oct 9; `features/account/AccountControl.tsx`): a still placeholder until it is known who is here, "Sign in", then the account chip (a wallet glyph and "Account"; gate `ACCOUNT-IN-THE-BAR`) whose menu lists the person's wallets and holds "Sign out". A wait that lasts is never its label: after half a minute the help is under it ("Try again", "Sign out"). "Sign in" is the bar's one call to action, the primary button as on the landing's bar (compact-nav.md: CTA = primary), 40px like "Sign out"; on `/sign-in` itself it is the page the person is on, a link marked current (`aria-current="page"`, the 2px primary underline the bar gives a current link, now for a current page as well as a section), so the screen's "Continue with a passkey" is the view's one primary (Oct 6, Thom). The sign-in screen is one centred column as wide as the bar can grow (860px): the headline, the lead, the two cards and the foot (the disclaimer and the language and appearance row, by the shell's rule for a `data-column="centred"` page) share its left edge; the cards are as tall as each other with their one button each at the foot, on one line and of one width. In the product and on the landing, sign-in is a dialog over the page (`SignInDialog.tsx`, `LandingSignIn.tsx`, Oct 6): every link to `/sign-in` opens it (the landing loads the wallet and the panel on the first press, the frame and a loading line first), it is the design system's modal (popover ground, hairline, 2px, no shadow, a scrim of the ground at 85% with no blur, a 12px rise, a crossfade with reduced motion) and a full-height sheet below 640px, and once the person is signed in it closes on the page they were on. The ways in are one button each (`SIGN-IN-FLOW`): "Continue with a passkey" uses the device's passkey and never makes one on a failure: it says why, that a new passkey opens a new account, and offers a secondary "Create a new passkey"; "Connect a wallet" opens our list of the wallets found, one entry each with its name and icon, and a wallet that signs on both families asks which chain before it signs. On a phone the links are in his sheet under the menu button, with the address at its top. The links are the router's (`linkAs`), so a page change keeps what the app holds, and say `aria-current="page"`. Over the landing's stage the bar decides its state from where step 03 and step 02 are on every scroll (the 60% line of `compact-nav.md`), so a jump past step 03 (End, a link to a section) finds it compact. Before step 03 the full bar is see-through over the joint until copy reaches it: the hero's heading and each step's copy are marked `data-under-bar`, and while any is in the bar's band the band takes the page's ground, so no line runs through the wordmark (`compact-nav.md`: the bar stays legible). On a phone the copy stands at the foot on its opaque plate, as `joint-stage.md` has it, and a line is shown only while its middle is below 42% of the screen: it fades before its plate can rise into the joint, which takes the top third. Reading taken, his to reopen: `compact-nav.md` says the app keeps the plain bar of `STYLE.md`; Thom asked for his compact bar across the product, and it is used there.
- **The portfolio** opens each vault with his "Goal card and plan" (guidelines.html, Components), side by side: the goal card (`VaultGoalCard.tsx`) and "Your plan · N parts" with the bar of its parts by weight and each part's target. The goal is joined to the vault by the plan's number on chain, from the server: `GET /v1/me/plans` lists the person's plans (their own, and those from a link they bought), each with its goal sheet, its card, its buys and the vault's number (`server-plans.ts`), so the card, what was put in and "What was done" are the same on a new device. What this browser kept still comes first (`OrderRecord.goal`: the sheet, the plan's card, the income verdict, the time; `basketOfPlan` gives the number by the API's rule), and the portfolio stands on it alone when the server has no list. The server also keeps the join in the database (section 4, "The goal join", PORT-1): `vaults.basket_id` names the person's plan, for the snapshot rows and the portfolio section's own routes (PORT-2, PORT-3). The card's sentence is the goal's, its date the order's day plus the horizon, its figure the vault's value with its pin, then what was put in and the plan's exit line, and "See your plan" ("See the order" for an order that names no plan, a shared portfolio's). What was put in is the sum of the orders of that plan whose deposit is confirmed on chain, as the API last said (`putInto`): an order kept before it was signed, or never signed, counts nothing, and with none confirmed the card says nothing of it. The engine gives no status for a vault, so the card says "No status yet: the engine gives none for a vault". The one word it gave is an income plan's verdict for the plan's own amount: it is said, as "On track when the plan was built" or "Short of its income when the plan was built" with the gap as its reason, only when what went in is that amount. A vault nothing joins (a shared portfolio bought in another browser: it has no goal, and the server's list is of plans) gets the card with its value and says so; no target is made up. With goal cards on the page the heading is the sans face (goal-card.md: the serif is the cards').
- **Activity** (`ActivityPanel.tsx`, `activity.ts`) is his "Disclaimer and activity": the disclaimer from the one constant under "Not licensed advice" on the left, and on the right one line per step that reached the chain, from the order as the API last said it: what it was, where it stands, when, MOCK and "test network" where they apply, and its explorer link. It closes the order screen and the portfolio (the orders this browser placed for the person, each read again with `GET /v1/orders/{id}`). The API has no route that lists a person's orders or the keeper's trades, so the keeper's lines ("Rebalanced …", "I moved …") are not shown yet: that needs the route, and the reason each keeper leg stores.
- **The portfolio section** (`/portfolio`, `features/portfolio-section/`, PORT-3). A person's plans over time, read from the four routes of section 3.3 and from nothing else; nothing on it signs. The bar's "Portfolio" opens it (Rodrigo, Oct 8; the monitor at `/monitor` and a vault's page count as Portfolio too), and it is also reached from the monitor's vault panel ("See over time") and from the goal page's vault card. Five pages under a row of tabs (`#portfolio-nav`, `data-ui="portfolio"`; the side menu was taken out on Oct 8, gate `PORTFOLIO-BOARD`), in both languages, with a dictionary of its own (`i18n/portfolio/`).
  - **Overview** (`/portfolio`), a board (Rodrigo, Oct 8, after a reference he gave). On the left: the total value of the vaults, their count, what went in (confirmed deposits through the app less confirmed withdrawals at their value when ordered, from `GET /v1/me/withdrawals`), the all-time PnL (value less what went in), and over the chosen period the PnL with its share, how many vaults gained, and the best one. On the right: the chart of the total value over the period, a solid line that is green where the value is at or above what had gone in by then and red where it is below, cut where the two cross; or the value as stacked bars by vault or by asset, a bar a day (an hour over 1D). Periods 1D (by the ten minutes), 7D and 30D (by the hour), 1Y, This year and All (by the day; All from the day of the first deposit); 30D first. Under it, a row a vault: its name (the same sentence everywhere, `vault-title.ts`), chain, value, what went in, all-time PnL, the period's PnL, and the status as the server gave it (`ON-TRACK-V1` is never worked out in the browser). A row opens the vault's own page (`/vaults/[chain]/[address]`), where its chat and its plan are; the plan's own page of this section is reached by its address. "New plan" leads to `/goal`. Its look is the gate's: rounded boxes and round controls (the second exception to STYLE.md's square rule), the chart modes as icon pills, the period as a drop-down, a plate in words in place of the hatch band, a tooltip only when the chart is pointed at. The figures are added in `overview-series.ts` and held by `overview-series.test.ts`: a deposit is never a gain, since a vault counts only from its first reading and as its value less what went into it by then.
  - **A plan** (`/portfolio/plan/[chain]/[address]`): the goal as the page's heading; the value over time, a solid line drawn in SVG with each deposit marked and listed; each part against its target in the design system's table, with the band as a shaded range and "Outside the band" in words and shape; what leaving would cost per asset; where the risk sits (the roll-up); the latest trades; and the activity panel of the monitor for this vault's orders.
  - **Rebalancing** (`/portfolio/rebalancing`): the steps that traded or adopted a version, grouped by vault, each a stacked block: when and what kind of time it is, whose step, why, the trade in words, the quoted cost said as a quote, drift before and after, the outcome, and the explorer link. A keeper's entry says it was worked out from two readings.
  - **Exposure** (`/portfolio/exposure`): per chain, the holdings by what each asset tracks and by who issues it, as bars with dollars and percentages, the selling cost per asset, the roll-up's flags as sentences, and the holdings with no price.
  - **Methodology** (`/portfolio/methodology`): what a snapshot is, the rule's name and its lines in order, where each figure comes from, what the pages cannot say yet, and that none of it is advice.
  - **On every page**: a pin after every figure, built from the stamp the answer carries, and no number where the answer carries no source or no time; the hatch and one quiet line a card for the mock and for a test network (MOCK-QUIET); a snapshot older than an hour said as stale from the answer's own flag; a chain that did not answer as a sentence; sums never add figures of two kinds: the overview adds the vaults of the most live kind the person holds (live, else a test network, else the mock), across the chains of that kind, and says which it leaves out (Rodrigo, Oct 8: a total portfolio value; before, sums were per chain); the exposure answer's `total` is not shown; one disclaimer.
  - Readings taken, his to reopen: the section keeps the 1280 page and the product's type sizes, because it is a consumer screen and Bearing's full width is for a dense instrument; each page writes its own title and the shell draws none, because the serif is spent once a screen; the plan card is built from primitives in the goal card's order, because `GoalCard` holds one pinned figure and has no place for the age of the read; a vault with no sheet is named by what the chain shows it following, then by what it was opened to follow, then by its own name, then by its chain; card reasons avoid "band" and "position" and the methodology defines them, because the voice guide keeps jargon one layer down; chain names are the wallet's, so a test network is said by the mark alone; no data library was added, the provider follows the monitor's read (TanStack Query still waits for a screen that needs it); the value figure's axis does not start at zero and its labels are scale, not pinned figures, while a deposit's dollars are only in the pinned list under it; shares, targets and differences carry no pin beside a pinned value, as on the monitor; a held part with no price switches the band off for the whole table, because the rule says such a plan cannot be weighed; the roll-up's own exit numbers are not shown, because they carry no stamp; drift before and after a step is words with the time of its reading, not a pin, because the answer gives it no source; a step's times on the rebalancing page are in the reader's own zone with the zone named, the other pages in UTC; whose-step marks (an upright bar, two flat bars) are new and are not in the icon registry; an explorer link is drawn only where the entry's label matches the network this app signs for, and never on the mock; on a mock chain the issuer split is replaced by a sentence, because every issuer there is a stand-in; costs are written in basis points and defined once a page.
  - Known and not built: the answers carry no time of the answer itself, so "read N ago" ages on screen until "Read again"; a keeper trade's amounts come raw and are read with the app's own units table, so an asset outside it shows a sentence in place of an amount; the exposure answer says nothing of staleness; the stub answers every vault and every window with one sample; the pages have not met the real API. The Portuguese wording ("No caminho / Atenção / Fora do caminho", "retrato", "margem", "nosso operador" and the rest) is proposed and is Rodrigo's to settle.

**Icons and the link preview (WEB-FAVICON).** From the mark (`Mark.tsx`, `logo-directions.md` "Cuts by size" and "Icon"), never the wordmark. `scripts/make-icons.mjs` writes them and they are committed; Chromium (Playwright) draws the rasters, so no image library was added, and the script writes the `.ico` and re-encodes each PNG as RGBA itself.

- **`app/icon.svg`** is the small cut (16 grid, for 12–21 px), `ink` on a light tab and `hinoki` on a dark one through `prefers-color-scheme`, the pin a hole. At 16 px every edge is on a whole pixel: a 4 px post, a 1 px joint gap, a 9 × 8 tenon end with 2 px walls round the pin; only the pin's four corner pixels are soft, so it reads as a round pin.
- **`app/favicon.ico`** (16, 32, 48) is for what reads no SVG: `hinoki` on a `black` tile, the small cut at 16 and the master at 32 and 48, each cut on its own grid, never scaled across one. Its link says `48x48`, and the SVG's `any`, so Chromium takes the SVG.
- **`app/apple-icon.png`** (180) and `public/icon-192.png`, `public/icon-512.png` (the manifest's): the master at 62% of the tile on `black`. The 512 is in `public/`, not `app/icon.png`, so a tab is never given a 512 px tile in place of the SVG.
- **`app/manifest.ts`**: `tenonfi`, lowercase as the wordmark and every title writes it; `black` for both colours, as the landing opens. **`themeColor`**: `paper` / `black` by the browser's scheme on the product, `black` on the landing (`lib/brand-grounds.ts`).
- **`app/opengraph-image.tsx`** (1200 × 630, `next/og`): the primary lockup (the face, then `tenonfi` in Inter Tight 600 at −2%), the landing's headline in Inter Tight 600 and "Made to measure. Every joint shown." in Inter 400, text and muted on night; no figure. The two static instances (Inter Tight 600, Inter 400) are committed under `assets/og/` with their OFL files. `metadataBase` is left to Next, which takes the deployment's URL on Vercel; a local build warns and uses localhost.
- **In the embed** the root's icons and preview stay: a frame shows no tab icon, so nothing of ours reaches the partner's screen; they show only on `/embed` opened in its own tab, which is our page. It sets no `themeColor`.
- **Checks:** `scripts/make-icons.test.ts` holds the committed files to the script (the cut, the two colours and no other, 16/32/48 RGBA in the `.ico`, the tile sizes) and tests the two encoders; `check-frames.mjs` asks the production build for `/icon.svg`, `/favicon.ico`, `/apple-icon.png`, `/opengraph-image`, `/manifest.webmanifest` and its icons, each in its type and size, and reads the links in the heads of `/` and `/goal` (`check-icons.mjs`).

**Invest as one screen (WEB-INVEST-TWO-PANE, gate `INVEST-TWO-PANE`, Oct 7).** Removed from the web on Oct 8 (Thom): `/goal` is the strategy conversation alone (`features/goal-conversation`), the files named below are deleted, and the API routes they called remain. What follows is the record of what was built. `/goal` is one shared conversation-and-plan workspace (`features/invest/InvestScreen.tsx`). Thom's later screenshot follow-up replaces the empty split view: before candidates or a plan exist, the conversation is naturally sized, the composer stays close to its latest message, and only learned facts appear as editable context. An unknown-facts table and empty plan outline are not drawn. Once plans exist, the desktop workspace shows them beside the conversation without a heavy dividing wall; on a phone, the existing plan disclosure opens with its focus, Escape and return-focus behavior. The goal form (`GoalScreen`, the constraint sheet on this route) and the "plan is built" box are gone.
- **The conversation** is behind `Conversation` (`features/invest/conversation.ts`): `turn(what was sent, the sheet so far)` answers the sheet so far, the facts still open, what to say as keys, the one question to ask, and the valid sheet once it is whole. `readerConversation` is today's: POST /goals for a first sentence, the app's own reading of the words over it (`pre-read.ts`), the page's examples known without a reader, and answers taken as they are. What the reader only assumed (a goal, a risk, a time frame nobody said) is left open and asked. The screen says every sentence from the dictionary (`invest.say`, `invest.ask`), with the figures from the sheet.
- **The pane**: `data-state` is `empty`, `facts`, `choice` (below), `plan` or `invest`. The facts are amount, income (an income goal only, and it may be left out), time and risk, plus what the money is for; a tap on one posts a turn that asks about it again. No country. `PlanPane` (`features/order/PlanPane.tsx`) is the plan "showing how", drawn here and on `/plan/{id}`: the answer in one line (income reached, the gap in dollars, or the projected range with its pin), the engine's ways to close a gap as buttons (pressed, the figure the engine wrote changes the amount or the income, `ways.ts`, and the plan is built again; on the plan's page the press leads here with the plan's goal, `handoff.ts`), the holdings with the reason that decided each, the exit plan, the figures and the chart, "Details", and "Invest $X".
- **Building**: nothing is built until the person asks ("Build my plan", gate `GUIDED-INTAKE`); after that a changed fact builds again at once. Signed in, `POST /v1/baskets/personalize`; signed out, `POST /v1/baskets/propose`, and the plan is built again as the person's own once they sign in (in the dialog) and their chain is known, or when they move to another chain. The pane's last state is the plan with the one-press invest card under it (`<Invest of={{ plan }} amount … />`, gate `INVEST-ONE-PRESS`), mounted once the plan is the person's own on a chain that can be invested in; until then the plan has its own button, which leads a visitor to the sign-in dialog. What the card tells is said in the conversation: one line as each step runs, in the card's own words; "Your vault is open." with the way to the portfolio; or that the buy stopped, with the way to finish it on the order's page. `/goal` is a signing route for that reason (`SIGNING_ROUTES`, `product-routes.test.ts`).
- **The plan as a picture (Oct 7, Thom: "the plan on the side panel could be more visual").** `PlanView` draws, and works nothing out. The headline follows the goal: to grow or protect, what most of the plan is in (the largest kind of the roll-up, else the largest holding) and the engine's bad-fall figure ("60% in stocks · in a bad fall about −$4,000"), with the yield range as a small pinned line only where there is a reading; for income, the verdict, and under it a meter of the pinned monthly estimate against what was asked. The allocation is one thick bar in the wood ramp (`bg-leg-1..4`, a fifth holding takes the first again), a part per holding with its verified token logo, served locally (Thom, Oct 7: add the actual logos). Unknown tokens or failed image loads use ticker letters; a part under 12% remains plain. The same mark appears in the labels, holding rows and owned-vault card labels. Original token artwork retains its colours for identification; the UI and allocation ramp retain the brand palette (sources in apps/web/public/assets/tokens/README.md). The bar is a picture (`role="img"`, the shares in its text alternative) and nothing in it is pressed: under it each holding has a label (mark, name, share), a button of at least 32px, and focusing, pointing at or pressing a label lights its part and its row. A row is one line (mark, name, share bar, share, dollars; the yield with its pin only where the holding has one) with the deciding reason closed under "Why this share". One drawing after it: the bad fall as a bar against the amount put in, and, where the plan pays its yield out (`card.cashFlow` is `monthly`) and the range has a source, what is paid out added up over the months as a dashed band on the yield's pin. A plan to grow or protect gets no curve: the engine projects no price. The way out is a meter of the plan's exit cost against 1%, the cost at which what can be sold is read, with the cost on its pin; not measured is an empty meter and one line, never a zero; a tier that passes no `meter` (the product pages, whose tiers are all at the 1% bound) draws none; then one sentence that tokens can be taken out at any time and selling to cash is not offered yet. The engine gives one exit cost for the plan and none per holding, so there is one meter.
- **Product holding figures.** A shared portfolio uses the same yield observation as the engine world (`primaryYieldsOf`), including its lower-yield choice when method and date tie. Bearing’s exit figure is present only with samples and a valid measurement date of its own; a missing date is never replaced with the time of the request. A dated, sampled zero is still a measured zero. A fixture remains sample data.
- **What the conversation cannot take is said.** A message that names a single stock or coin (`PICKS` in `conversation.ts`) is answered with our own name for it ("I can't pick single stocks like Nvidia yet…"), never the typed words; "more risk" and "safer" are a step from the risk held, and at the ends say so. Words that change nothing never ask "shall I build?" a second time. The build reply is not offered once a plan is built for the sheet, also after a sign-in that left the page; a plan built again as the person's own is not announced twice; an app turn identical to the one before it is not added. Once built, the line points at the pane ("The plan is on the right. Review it and invest, or change anything."; on a phone, "ready below").
- **After the review of Oct 7.** The income meter's whole bar is what was asked: solid to the low end of the estimate, the end the engine's verdict is measured on, lighter on to the high end, and solid to the end only where the verdict says the income is met. A clause with a negation in it ("I don't want more risk", "not $5,000", "não quero dez anos") is left out before the words are read (`affirmed`), on a first sentence too, so what is refused is never taken; "no" counts only in English. "Meta" names the company only in English. A go-ahead is yes, ok, go, build, sim and their forms, never a filler ("so", "and", "please"). A first sentence's monthly figure is kept while the goal is still open. A way to close a gap is posted as the person's own turn ("Make it $163,100"), not the engine's sentence. While a fact is asked about again or the plan is being built again, the plan on the pane has no invest card and no button, and says it is from before the change. Someone signed in whose plan is still a visitor's is never told to sign in: "Make this plan yours", or nothing to press while their chain is read. A chain our server has switched off builds nothing, and the conversation says so. On a phone the pane opens as a dialog (focus in, Escape, focus back, the conversation inert behind it). Fields read back from the tab are held to the sheet's own values, on the sheet and on every turn, and a value that is not one is never said. `invest-guards.events.test.ts` holds what the goal form's tests held: a late answer, a plan for another chain or not in the frozen shape, nothing asked of the wallet, the hatch sweep, the server's refusals in our words, the chain switched, the box's bounds, a goal in reais.
- **The guided intake reads the conversation (gate `GUIDED-INTAKE`), signed in.** `intakeConversation` (`features/invest/intake-conversation.ts`) is the second `Conversation`: every turn posts the first message, the later ones in the person's own words (`followUps`; up to 199 later messages, retaining every accepted message in chronological order, each trimmed to at most 2,000 characters, and at most 22,000 characters in the full text joined by blank lines), the answers given by a press (`answers`, by field) and those answers as they stood at each later message (`answersThen`) to `POST /v1/baskets/intake` (`intake.ts`, which checks the answer's shape, since the web does not import the engine's types). The prior engine sheet and read-back are never promoted into pressed answers. A turn beyond either conversation bound keeps the prior accepted state, explains the limit and offers Start over; existing pressed facts can still be edited. The engine groups old messages into context and replays only its latest twelve blocks separately: an ancient model-only refusal answered by a plain yes may need confirmation again, with no buildable sheet until it is settled. Longer conversations require the expanded API to be deployed first; an old API refusal preserves prior state. The question shown is the server's first open one, in its words, with replies: the choices it names, the usual amounts and times, "No date set", yes and no where it asks to leave something out, and what it read and asks to be sure of as the first reply. The facts on the pane are the server's draft. Once nothing is open its read-back is said sentence by sentence as given, and the sheet a plan is built from is the server's, sent to `personalize` as it came (limits, mix, sleeves and all). A theme and a stated mix show as their own facts ("Theme: AI · 30%", "Held as you said: 80% dollar yield and 20% stocks and crypto"), and a goal with no date shows "No date set". The server's sentences, question and sheet are never read back from the tab: the kept messages and answers are posted again (`replay`). A visitor, and anyone the route cannot read for (404, 401, 403), gets the rules reader and this app's own questions, and the public `propose`; once they sign in, their next turn is read by the intake from their own messages, with the facts they had as answers. `cantPick` is the rules reader's only: the intake's server says what no stock fits (THEME-NONE-YET).
- **After Thom's live conversation of Oct 7.** One source for the facts: what our server read, with every answer given by a press over it (its draft is of the text alone), so a fact is never lost and a reader that takes over holds all of them. Where the intake does not answer (404, 401, 403, a 5xx, an answer not in its shape) the turn is read by the rules, "The assistant didn't answer, so I'm reading this the simple way for now." is said once, and the next turn asks the intake again with every message and the facts held as answers. A read-back is followed by "Yes, build it" each time the sheet changes, also once plans stand: nothing is made from a change until the person says so, and the plans from before it cannot be invested in meanwhile; with plans standing for that exact sheet, only what the read-back says that it did not say before is shown. "How do you want the money held" has replies (All of it, 70%, Half, 30%, None) sent as the answer to that question (`answers.mix`), and words that bring the same question back are told so. No reply is ever empty, and a turn that throws says it failed. "Start over" (a button by the title, or "start over", "clear it out", "reset", "recomeçar") empties the conversation and the pane. Who read a turn (the model, our server's rules with its reason, or this app's rules) is logged to the console. Inline reader diagnostics require both a development build and an explicit `tf-debug=1` local browser opt-in; the default creation experience shows no implementation tags.
- **The candidates as rows, and words over presses (Oct 7, later).** In the pane a candidate is one row at full width: its name and aim with "Choose <name>" at the right, its bar across with the legend on one line, then its figures in a row (yield observed and cost to sell on their pins, the bad fall, and with withdrawals the months paid), and the rest of the scorecard under a fold that is open where there is a gap to close. Side by side only where the block itself is at least 56rem wide (a container query, so never in the pane). When a plan exists, its pane scrolls with the page; the conversation can stay in view with its thread scrolling inside it. Before any plan exists, the conversation has no viewport-height spacer between the latest message and composer. An answer pressed for a fact gives way to a later message that speaks of that fact ("High" pressed, then "make it low risk").
- **Said back before a question, and a way out beside a refusal.** While something is still asked, what our server read so far is said first, from its own fields and never from the typed words: the facts, the themes by the names it gives them and a stated mix ("I have what you want held: AI."), "I don't have a way to pick stocks by that yet." only where it read a name to nothing on the chain (THEME-NONE-YET), then "One thing I need first:" and its question. Where its codes say something asked for is not held because of the goal (`mix_dropped_for_goal`, `themes_dropped_for_goal`) and the goal is not growth, "Make it a growth goal" is offered beside the confirm; it answers the goal as growth and drops the monthly income. No other way out is offered: the reply has no field for the nearest portfolio it names, and its questions carry no reason. The invest card's "Invest $X instead" (`onAmount`) changes the amount as the person's own turn, and the plans are made again.
- **Three plans in the pane (gate `THREE-PLANS`).** `buildPlan` reads `candidates` and `candidatesNotShown` beside the plan: each candidate once, each its own stored id, every one an answer to the sheet that was sent, sorted into the fixed order Cover, Spread, Carry; a server that sends none answers one plan. The pane's states are `empty`, `facts`, `choice`, `plan` and `invest`. In `choice`, `Candidates` (`features/order/Candidates.tsx`) draws them side by side (stacked where the pane is narrow) with none picked or marked and no primary button: each with its aim, its headline (what most of it is in and its bad-fall figure; with withdrawals, the months paid on the yield's pin and the stress that pays the fewest), what it holds as one bar, its scorecard (yield observed and selling cost on their pins, issuers, credit share), and with withdrawals the engine's ways to close a gap as buttons, per candidate. Those the engine did not offer are named with its reason. A candidate is picked by "Choose <name>" or by saying its name alone or after "choose", "pick", "I'll take" (a sentence that only mentions one, or names two, picks none); then the pane shows that plan in full (`PlanPane`) under "You chose <name>" with "See the plans again", and the invest step is headed "Invest $X in <name>" and buys that candidate's own plan id. Nothing can be invested in before a pick. Plans made again from a change have none picked; the same plans made the person's own after a sign-in keep the pick. One candidate alone is opened as the plan.
- **Conversation ownership (Oct 7 review fixes).** A read or replay is tied to the requesting person, chain and conversation generation. Changing account or chain, resetting or unmounting invalidates its success, error and busy-state updates; an old reply cannot release a newer request. A visitor who signs in retains their already-held conversation, but an unfinished visitor read is not applied to the signed-in state. Start over is usable while a read is pending and remains held while a plan is being built. A pressed allocation (`intake.held`) survives the tab round-trip only after its integer shares and whole-plan sum are validated; explicit null keeps the person's “None” answer. Stored server sheets, read-back sentences and questions remain discarded and are replayed from person-origin words and answers.
- **Kept**: a goal handed from the landing or a partner's embed is the first turn; the conversation is kept in the tab (keys and the person's own words only are read back) and forgotten on sign-out; the slow sign-in notice is said at the head of the conversation.

**The flow, end to end, after the audit of Oct 6 (WEB-FLOW-FIXES-1; the report's finding numbers in brackets).**
- **Goal screen.** A chip is read on the click [1]. The income example is whole ("$80,000 for $300 a month of income, 5 years, low risk") and opens with nothing missing [2]. A goal or risk the reader answered with a default of its own, which the words did not say either, is marked "Not said in your goal: I assumed this" until it is changed [3]. The note about the reader and the "parser: …" line are gone; what was not found is named in one line [4]. Signed out, the build button's place holds a primary link, "Sign in to build my plan", and nothing is said as an error (`ConstraintSheet`, `next`) [5]. Goal, time, risk, country and amount are in view; holdings, the glide and the language are folded under "More limits" (`ConstraintSheet`, `more`), which opens itself when a folded field does not fit; the headline is not made from "0 months" [6]. The page moves to the built plan, with the keyboard [15].
- **Plan.** The chips that repeated the heading and the short disclaimer line are gone; how the range was worked out is in Details; a flag this app has no sentence for is one plain line ("The plan carries one more note we can’t describe yet.") [12]. An income plan says what its range a year comes to a month, as an estimate with the pin of the yield reading it stands on ("About $147 to $163 a month if the projected range holds. An estimate, not a promise."), whether or not an income was asked; a gap is said to the cent, with the ways the API gives to close it, each in its sentence, and one button, "Change my limits", which hands the goal screen the limits the plan was built from [11]. The API sends the ways as sentences, so they are not a button each. A plan is kept in the browser (`localStorage`, the eight newest) as a cache, so the screens open at once; the server is the last word (API-PLANS, `GET /v1/baskets/{id}`): a cached plan it says is gone or another person's (404, 403) is dropped and not shown, but only after it was asked once more with fresh tokens (a stale sign-in is answered as nobody is), and never for development's throwaway wallet, which a real server always answers as nobody; one it does not answer about stands, and on sign-out the cache and that person's order records are forgotten in this browser (so a half-done order cannot be resumed from this browser after a sign-out; the trust acceptance stays: it holds no figure; what was signed for a step stays, since it is what stops a second signature). A plan nobody has says so with "Build it again" [14].
- **One name for a token** (`tokenName` and `displayName`, `features/order/amounts.ts` and `plain.ts`), on the plan, the order, the funds step, the portfolio, the vault page, the shelf and the activity: "syrupUSDC (Maple)", "Cash (USDC)", "SPYx". A test network's token goes by the token it stands in for ("tSPYx" is SPYx, "tUSDC" is USDC) and the card says "Test network"; the engine's sentences are passed through the same rule (`plainNames`). Robinhood Chain's dollar keeps the one name it has, tUSDG [13].
- **Buy.** A lone mark in an amount is read as the page's language reads it, so "10.555" in English is refused, not read as $10,555 [18]. An amount that is not the plan's says the plan was built for another [16]. The funds step names cash as the plan does, offers one way to fill the wallet at a time, and writes its source line with the one time format [19]. The short points of the trust notice leave out the keeper's limits for a plan's own vault, which nothing trades (they stay in the full list); a notice accepted before is not a step again, and is still there to read at the review. Accepted means by this person, for this text, in this browser (`tf-trust:<person>` in `localStorage`; the API's consent route is not built): another person, an earlier text or a new device is asked again, and without it the review's button refuses. The acceptance records whether the keeper's limits were shown, so someone who accepted on a plan's buy is asked again on a buy the keeper may trade [20]. Step 4 is "Review", with the same words for a plan and a shared portfolio [21].
- **Order.** A done order offers "See your portfolio" and "Buy more" [22]. A trade is said in the token's own units with the price that minimum means ("receive at least 0.0099 SPYx (at most $606.06 each)"); the mock's tokens have their decimals in `units.ts`, held to `packages/chain-mock` by `tests/web-units.test.ts`, and a token with no units gets how far under the quote the step may land and no raw count [23]. A refusal's check and the guard's words are behind "Details for support"; when a later step fails after the deposit landed, the page says the deposit is in the vault as cash, and leads to the portfolio first, a new order beside it. Where the server has `POST /v1/orders/{id}/continue` the first thing offered is "Finish buying with the cash in your vault": the route is looked for only once an order has stopped, by its status: asked with something that is not an order's id, a server with the route answers 400 and one without answers 404; the button is not there until it answers. The trades the next order is held to are the approved order's own, by step, with only each step's standing read from the API. The order it makes is kept with the trades the first one left, and is offered for signing only if it deposits nothing, opens nothing, says which order it finishes, and its trades are those trades (`checkContinuation`, `order-check.ts`) [24]. The order page has no disclaimer block of its own: the shell's foot draws it, which the order screen's test holds in both languages. Every time is "Oct 5, 2026, 15:00 UTC" (`features/portfolio/figures.ts`, `utc`) [25]. The step that opens the vault and buys is called that; "as our server reports it" is gone [26]. The order's page no longer lists its steps a second time; the portfolio lists what was done under the order it was a step of [27].
- **Portfolio and vault.** Cash is a holding row, so the shares add up to 100 (`holdingsOf`); the table is "What you hold", with "Share now", "Planned" and "Difference", to one decimal at most, rounded together so a column adds up to 100.0 (`sharesOf`), with each row's difference worked from the two shares as written; the keeper's losses keep two decimals [28, 32]. The chips, the auto-follow and keeper tiles and "No status yet: the engine gives none" are gone from the first view: the vault's address, the version it follows, auto-follow and, with auto-follow on, the keeper's losses are behind "Details", and the goal card says "Goal date: October 2029" or the income verdict [31]. The goal card leads with what was put in when that is not the plan's amount, says what a vault bought from a shared portfolio follows, and its quiet line is in the language of the view [30, 32, 33]. The vault's address leads to its page, which leads back and to the explorer, counts cash as a holding and writes its figures as the portfolio does [34, 35]. When the chain the bar is on holds no vault and another does, the page says so first and names the chain of each vault [36]; "No wallet of this sign-in is on …" is said only where there is none [37]. A link drawn as a button goes through the router inside the shell (`components/shell/RouterLinks.tsx`) [33].
- **The rest.** A passkey that is not taken says so, and "Create a passkey" says under it, before any press, that it opens a new, empty wallet [39]; since Oct 9 it stands beside "Use my passkey" from the start, and a closed prompt is a calm note (gate `SIGN-IN-PAIR`). On a shared portfolio's page a vault bought from a goal goes by that goal, and the creator's address and the routine text check are behind Details; where the version and weights come from stays in view [41]. Findings 34, 38 and 42 (the link to the vault's page, the shelf's sentence, the publish form) came with `WEB-FLOW-FIXES-3`; this branch keeps them as they are.
- **Not here:** the test tokens' issuers and tiers and the credit budget [8, 10], test funds on the hosted API [17], plans, goals and history kept on the server [14, 30], shared portfolios published [40], withdrawing [29], and the country asked at sign-in [7].

**Why a plan holds what it holds (WEB-PLAN-BINDING, from Thom's own buy of Oct 6: $80,000 for $300 a month came out 25% syrupUSDC and 75% cash, beside "starts with 100% in dollar yield").**
- **The reason that decided a line** (`bindingReason` in `features/order/plain.ts`), in the engine's own sentence: the credit limit (`CREDIT_BUDGET*`), the cap on a token or an issuer (`ASSET_CAP`, `ISSUER_CAP*`, `SINGLE_STOCK_CAP`), the exit ceiling (`EXIT_CEILING`, `TIER_CEILING`); on cash, why money stayed (`UNPLACED`, `NO_DOLLAR_YIELD`, `YIELD_TOO_SMALL`, what is set aside). Where a line only started (`SLEEVE*`, `CASH_NEAR_DATE`, `GLIDE`) is said only when nothing else is. The legs show that reason first, and the summary sentence says it for the largest holding and for cash, so it never states a share the lines contradict (a test scans every share and amount in it).
- **What was left out** (`removed[]`) is listed in Details, each in the engine's sentence, and money that could not be placed is said by the cash line's own reason in place of the general note.
- **An income goal says its income:** "Earn $300 a month from $80,000 for 12 months.", on the plan, the goal screen's card and the vault's goal card.
- **On the portfolio** cash is a part of the plan like the others ("Cash (USDC) 75%", counted in "Your plan · 2 parts", with its segment in the bar), and the parts and the holdings table use the plan screen's names ("syrupUSDC (Maple)"). A short income plan's status is in figures: "When this plan was built, it paid $71.77 a month of the $300 you asked for." (`GoalCard`'s status takes no date after a whole sentence).

**The plan in plain words (WEB-PLAN-CLARITY, Thom, Oct 6).** The plan screen showed engine codes and ids. Now (`features/order/plain.ts`):
- **No code on the page.** Each flag of the engine and the roll-up is a sentence in the dictionary (`plan.flagWords`, English and Portuguese), each once; a flag no sentence names is said by one sentence that there is a note, never by its code. Kinds of asset are words (`plan.kinds`). The notes and the spread of the plan ("How the plan is spread, and what selling costs", was "Risk, as our server rolled it up") are in a closed "Details" under the plan.
- **Names, not ids.** "syrupUSDC (Maple)", "Cash (USDC)", "SPYx": the shelf's symbols, the issuer where it says what a dollar-yield token is, a test network's token by the token it stands in for. The web cannot read the registry (`tests/boundaries.test.ts`), so the symbols are a list in `plain.ts`; an unknown token goes by its symbol in capitals, never its id.
- **One sentence at the top**, from the lines: "$200 for 2 months, high risk, on Solana: $150 stays in Cash (USDC) and $50 goes to syrupUSDC (Maple)." and the largest holding's own reason. No rate in it: the plan carries no rate per line, and a rate needs its pin.
- **A short or flat plan** (under 6 months, or a projection under 1% of the amount) is said in a sentence on the yield's pin ("In 2 months: about $201.50 to $201.60", to the cent where the dollars round the same) instead of a flat chart; the chart's ticks are one per dollar label.
- **A bad fall** is a sentence: "In a bad fall: you'd lose about $0, since nothing here is stocks, crypto or gold.", or the engine's estimate.
- **Punctuation:** the basis sentence keeps one full stop ("gold.." came from the engine's own); a leg with no rate shows no "· —".
- The MOCK plates are left to `web/mock-quiet` (build-3d).

**His partner embed (WEB-EMBED).** `embed-shell.md` and guidelines.html section 08 ("The joints stay. The wood becomes theirs."), in `app/(embed)/` and `features/embed/`.

- **A bare root.** `app/(embed)/layout.tsx` is a document of its own: the stylesheet, the language, and nothing else of ours. No bar, no wallet provider, no account, no font: a test of the import graph holds it (`product-routes.test.ts`), and the e2e finds no `wallet-adapter`, Inter Tight or honey hex in the page, nor a nav or main landmark (the host owns those).
- **The partner's skin, from the frame's address.** A frame does not inherit its host's CSS, so the host names its colours in the URL: `/embed?fg=&bg=&muted=&border=&accent=&radius=&button=&font=&mono=&scheme=` (`features/embed/theme.ts`). Each is taken only as an opaque `#rrggbb` colour, a length (or `pill` for the button), or a list of font names; anything else is dropped, and the system's colours stand in (`Canvas`, `CanvasText`, and the text mixed toward the ground for the muted colour, since `GrayText` on white is 3.9:1). Anyone can write that address on our origin, so the colours have a floor (PR #83 review): the ground and the text colour are taken together or not at all, and only at 4.5:1 or more; the muted colour and the accent are taken only at 4.5:1 on the ground, and the muted colour is the text colour when none is taken. So the disclaimer, the pins, the hatch and MOCK cannot be made invisible from the URL, and `embed-shell.md`'s low-contrast case (drop the hatch, keep MOCK) cannot arise: a faint muted colour is refused instead. The shell draws the partner's container as the guide does: their ground, hairline, radius and 1.25em inside.
- **`/embed`: the goal, then its limits, then out.** A typing box and a button in the partner's skin (not our rounded composer, which the spec says does not appear), read by the public reader (`POST /goals`, no sign-in); the limits as rows; then "Build this plan in tenonfi", a link that opens `/goal` in a new tab with the goal in the address's fragment, which no server sees, and `/goal` reads it into its box. Any site can link to `/goal#goal=<text>` the same way; it only fills the box and is read by the public reader, and nothing is built or signed until the person asks. A plan is not built inside the frame: building one needs the person's sign-in (`POST /v1/baskets/personalize`), and nothing is signed there. The disclaimer and the credit ("Powered by", the symbol and the word, in the partner's muted colour, linking to tenonfi in a new tab) close it.
- **`/embed/[chain]/[address]`: a vault, read only.** The public vault route (`GET /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}`, WEB-4): its value and each part on a pin, the hatch and one quiet line ("Sample figures · test network") where not live, and a link to the public vault page; an answer for another vault, or none, is one sentence.
- **Framing.** `lib/frame-policy.ts`, through `next.config.ts`: the embed's two pages, `/embed` and `/embed/<chain>/<address>`, answer `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'self'` plus the https origins in `EMBED_FRAME_ANCESTORS` (a deployment's list of partners, space separated); every other address answers `frame-ancestors 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `/embed/x` and `/embed/a/b/c` included, since they fall to the `(structurer)` page for an unknown address, which has a wallet button. Next matches a header's source without regard to case while its routes keep it, so `/Embed` would take the embed's policy on that same page; `proxy.ts`, which every spelling of `/embed` reaches, holds any address that is not exactly one of the two to nobody. `check-build.mjs` starts the production build and asks 13 addresses who may frame them (`check-frames.mjs`). The list is read when the app is built (it is written into `routes-manifest.json`), so naming a partner takes a rebuild. No partner is named yet, so on a deployment only the app itself can frame the embed until one is.
- **Height.** The embed posts `{ type: 'tenonfi:embed-height', height }` to its parent whenever its height changes (`host-height.ts`), to `*`: it carries a number and nothing else, and who may frame it is the CSP's to say. A host checks `event.source` is its frame and sets the frame's height. A host whose listener comes up after the first message (a race the e2e met) asks with `{ type: 'tenonfi:embed-height-request' }` sent to the frame, and the embed answers its parent alone.
- **`/dev/embed`** is a stand-in host: four frames (the system's light and dark, his sample partner in light and dark from `.partner-app`), each sized by the height it posts; `?width=375` narrows them, `?vault=solana/<address>` frames a vault.
- **Differences from section 08** (screenshots at 375 and 1280 beside it): his card shows a goal already built (progress, a pinned rate, a MOCK leg, "See your plan"); this one shows the goal being read, since a plan needs sign-in, and the vault view carries the pins and MOCK. His disclaimer box has an 8px radius under a 14px card; ours takes the partner's one radius. His sample's base text is 15px; ours is the host's (16px in the system's).
 Under `apps/web/features/shared/`, on the primitives, in both languages, light and dark. Publishing and following are orders, reviewed and signed on the order screen (`/orders/[id]`) through `execute` of `@colosseum/sdk`, as a buy is; nothing else signs.

- **The shelf** (`/shelf`, in the bar). `GET /v1/shelf` with no chain: one list of every chain's portfolios, for everyone (gate `CHAIN-AT-THE-PLAN`), each card labelled with its chains and keeping its own sample mark. A portfolio's page shows the recipe a wallet of the person signs on, asks which with a two-option radio group where there are two, and says why where there is none; the buy names that chain. A card per portfolio: its name as the card's one link, the creator's address, the platform badge, the chains, the version in effect and its weights on one line, whether auto-follow is offered (a word and a shape), and the version that waits. The MOCK plate on a portfolio of the mock or a test network.
- **A portfolio's page** (`/indexes/[slug]`). Its name and description, the creator, and the recipe of the person's chain: the version in effect as the plan pane's legs (a table beyond four assets), the version that waits with its time, where the version and weights came from, and the auto-follow offer. `GET /v1/indexes/{slug}/versions` as a table. Signed in: "Buy and follow this portfolio", and the person's vaults on their chain (`GET /v1/portfolio`): a vault that follows it shows the prompt when a newer version is in effect (accept it) or waits (its time), and the auto-follow switch only where the portfolio offers it, the one-tap sentence otherwise (gate `GOLD-ONE-TAP`); a vault that follows something else, "Follow with this vault". Each is a `follow` order.
- **Buy and follow** (`/indexes/[slug]/buy`). The invest card (`INVEST-ONE-PRESS`), which took the place of the buy screen's four steps: amount, funding (`GET /v1/funding` with `family`) and trust notice, then a `buy` with `family` and the version shown.
- **Publish** (`/publish`). Name, address on the shelf (made from the name until edited), description, and three to twelve assets with weights (the committed deployment's assets; on the mock, the mock's shelf, MOCK). The family id is worked out on the page from the address (`familyIdOf`) and shown; an address the person published before is the next version (`update`), another creator's is refused. The four limits are checked on the page (count, 2% to 50% in 0.5% steps, 100%, each asset once) and again by the server and the registry. The order is a `publish` with the form's id and text.
- **The public vault page** (`/vaults/[chain]/[address]`). `GET /v1/vaults/{chain}/{address}` (new, public): owner, what it follows and at which version, auto-follow, value, and each position with its held amount, price (pinned), weight, target and drift. Nothing is signed by the read, and nothing is written for a visitor. The owner's page is where a deposit, a withdrawal and new targets are signed, in its right pane (gate `VAULT-PAGE-ACTIONS`, Oct 9: the plan bar over one table of planned beside now, the technical rows behind "Details", and the page among the routes that may reach the signing port). The owner's read, signed in, may store their vault's number the first time (`vault_numbers`, and their `users` row where they have none; gate `VAULT-NUMBER`); every answer carries `cache-control: private, no-store` and `vary: Authorization`, since the owner's differs. Anybody may ask, so the API keeps an answer for 10 seconds per chain and address (`VAULT_KEPT_MS`, in memory, 500 addresses at most): a link opened by many reads the chain once in that time.
- **What a signature is held to** (`features/shared/terms.ts`). The screen that makes an order keeps its terms beside it in the order record (`order-record.ts`): for a publish the form's family id, text, weights and version; for a buy of a portfolio and a follow, the portfolio's account, version and weights. The order screen hands them to the runner, which makes the guard's `PlanTerms` (`planTermsOf` in `run-order.ts`); the API's order is never read for them. Before offering to sign, the order is held to them (`order-check.ts`): a buy of a portfolio deposits what was typed and spends it on the weights read, split as the API splits it (`tradesOf`); a publish has one `publish` step; a follow only the accept and the switch its terms call for. A follow names a vault by its plan number only when that number derives the vault's address (`vaultOf` of the SDK).
- **The gap API-3 left, closed where it can be.** A follow's terms are read from the chain by the browser, through this app's own node (`NEXT_PUBLIC_CHAIN_READ_RPC_SOLANA`, the node the executor reads, `features/order/chain-node.ts`): `readSolanaRecipe` of the SDK reads the registry's account at the address it derives from the creator and the family id the slug gives, with the cluster's clock, and maps each mint to an asset by the committed deployment. What it reads is what the page shows and what the buy or follow is held to; where our server answered something else the page says so as an alarm. Where it cannot read, the page says which, and shows our server's answer as not checked: the mock (there is no chain), no node set for this app, no deployment committed for the network, a portfolio whose id is not its slug's (not published through the app: the slug-to-id link is the server's word; not offered for following), or an EVM chain (its registry read waits for ADE-2). Where it has a node and a deployment and the read fails (the node did not answer, the account is not the program's recipe of that creator and family, the server named a creator that is no Solana address), nothing is offered: no buy and no follow, with the sentence that the portfolio could not be verified and may have been tampered with (the review of #62: a server that lies could otherwise switch the read off). A line whose token the app does not list is shown by its mint, in the version in effect and the one that waits. The creator address the account is derived with is still the server's word: the page shows it in full.
- **The guard on the mock's publish.** `packages/sdk/src/guard/mock/check.ts` holds a mock publish to the step as the Solana check does: the creator, the family, the hash of the form's text worked out by the guard, the assets and weights, no fee and no flags. The mock has one operation for a first version and the next, so `publish` and `update` are not told apart there; a cancel is refused as `unsupported`.
- **The creator's words** are text everywhere: React writes them as text, never as markup or a link. Where the chain was read, the page works out `familyTextHash` of the name and description it shows and marks them "can't vouch for them" when no version on the chain carries that hash; where nothing was read it says the text is not checked, and does not repeat the server's `textMatches`. The publish form refuses what the server refuses (a link, a bare web address or an email, a hidden character), and updates only a portfolio whose stored id is `familyIdOf(slug)`. An accept never asks to keep auto-follow on where the portfolio does not offer it. The public vault page pins the vault's value as the monitor does (`vaultValueSource`). The creator's address is shown in full, from the chain's account where read; the platform badge only for a creator in `features/shared/platform.ts` (empty until the launch portfolios are published from a platform key), never from the server's `platform`. A portfolio whose id is not `familyIdOf(slug)` is not offered for following (gate `FAMILY-ID`), and every follow names `basketIdOfPlan(familyIdOf(slug))`.
- **Checks.** `features/shared/shared-order.events.test.ts` (6: the executor is handed the form's publish terms, the followed version, the vault's plan number; an order with a step its terms do not call for, or trades on other weights, is shown and not offered), `shared-screens.events.test.ts` (9: the shelf asks for the person's chain and shows markup as text; the switch only where offered, the one-tap sentence otherwise; the prompt's accept; a vault whose plan number is not its own; another chain's portfolio; a buy's version and kept terms; the publish form's id, limits and text), `chain-read.events.test.ts` (8: a node that holds another version and weights than the server: the page shows the chain's and says so, and the buy asks for and keeps the chain's account, version and weights; the text's hash; an unlisted line that waits, by its mint; a read that fails with a node set blocks the buy on the page and on the buy screen, for an EVM creator, an account the program does not own and an account of another creator; a portfolio the chain does not hold). `apps/web/e2e/shared.spec.ts`: a publish through the form, signed through the real guard on the mock, the shelf, the page, a buy that follows it, signed, the vault and its public page; a portfolio with gold offers no auto-follow. axe at 375 px in light and dark on every screen, no violation. The stub API (`tests/e2e/stub-api.ts`) serves the shared routes and orders on its mock chain, gold there with no oracle.
- **Differences from his references** (screenshots at 375 and 1280 px, both themes, beside `guidelines.html` (components, legbar, provenance) and `prototypes/hero-3d.html` (the case's plan pane, the closing section)). The shelf's card is the guide's goal card turned into a list card: a sans title, no serif line inside it, the weights as one mono line rather than a bar. The portfolio's page is the plan pane without its chips and stat cells, since a portfolio has no goal, amount or horizon; its legs carry a dash for the yield they do not state. The buy and the review are WEB-3's. The publish form is the guide's controls (fields and secondary buttons) in two cards; there is no reference for an editable list of weights. The public vault page is a stat row and a table, as the monitor's. None of his references is a shared-portfolio screen: these are the nearest.
- **Not built.** The follow prompt on the monitor (WEB-2's screen, not edited here: `FollowPrompt` is exported for it), a cancel of a version that waits, a read of an EVM recipe from this app's own node (on Robinhood Chain a follow shows the server's answer as not checked, with its own reason, `no-reader`: this app does not read that registry yet, whether or not a node is set), and value following on the shelf. The pages read on the client, not statically generated with `revalidate: 60` as section 11 sets for the shelf and a portfolio's page: they hang on the person's chain and on reads of the chain made by the browser.

**Waiting for data (WEB-LOADING, Oct 6, Thom).** The hosted API sleeps on Render's free plan and can take up to a minute to wake, so every screen that reads waits in the shape of what comes: `components/ui/Skeleton.tsx` draws still boxes in the layout's own grid (a figure, a line, a stat row, table rows, a chart frame, the plan pane, a list of cards), and `Waiting.tsx` puts them in a region that is `aria-busy`, with one polite status line under them. The spec has no shimmer and nothing ambient (card.md, button.md): the boxes do not pulse, and the one moving thing is STYLE.md's loader, the lattice that assembles, shown with its words only once a wait passes 400ms and still under reduced motion. After 4 seconds the line adds "Waking the data service: this can take up to a minute the first time"; after a minute (`wait.ts`) the wait gives up with a sentence and a retry. Bearing's pages wait in their own boxes: the selectors, the row of figures with their labels and their notes laid out unseen under their bars, the two chart cards and the table, so the row of figures does not move when the data comes (`e2e/bearing.spec.ts` holds its box to the pixel); its banner keeps its height with still bars. Every other screen's card waits in its shape (`components/shell/Wait.tsx`), the goal screen shows the outcome card while a plan is built, the partner embed keeps still boxes and words only (embed-shell.md), and the pages made on the server have a `loading.tsx` of the same shapes.

**Each screen waits in its own outline (WEB-SKELETONS, Oct 9, Thom).** The card-shaped waits above gave way to the page a moment later, and everything moved. Now a screen's wait is the screen in outline, so its regions are where its skeleton was when the answer lands: `Skeleton.tsx` gained a line set in the text's own type (`SkeletonLine`: the bar's line box is the text's, at any size), a mark, a table in the screen's own columns and a list row with its mark, and each feature builds its wait from them beside the screen it stands for (`features/order/waits.tsx` for the plan pane, the plan, buy and order pages; `features/shared/waits.tsx` for the shelf and a shared portfolio; `features/portfolio-section/waits.tsx` and `PlanWait.tsx` for the board, rebalancing, exposure and a vault's page; `features/portfolio/MonitorWait.tsx`). Words that are the page's own (a label, a column's head, a block's heading) are already written; a name, a share or an amount is a still bar, and no figure is ever drawn, real or made up. `Waiting` has a `float` form (`components/waits/ScreenWait.tsx`): the status line takes no room of its own, so the region is exactly its skeleton, and it stays at the foot of the window while a tall skeleton runs below it. The boxes are still, as before. After 4 seconds the line now reads "Still loading. The server may be waking up."; there is no progress bar. What a skeleton cannot know is held to its place and width only: a list's length, a sentence's line count, and a notice that a chain could not be read. `e2e/skeletons.spec.ts` holds each screen's read, checks the wait is one busy region with one polite line and passes axe, and compares the boxes of the main regions waiting and settled at 1440 px and 375 px; it also holds that a failed read gives the failure and a retry, and an empty answer the empty state, never a skeleton that runs on. The stub has a switch for looking at this by hand (`STUB_DELAY_MS`, or `POST /__stub/delay`).

**The design system in the app (BRAND-1).** `/dev/ui` under `pnpm dev` shows every primitive in every state, in light beside dark, under the name of its spec, with made-up content labelled MOCK. It is a development route (`app/(app)/dev/ui/page.dev.tsx`, under the product's layout since WEB-1): a production build has no such route, and `scripts/check-build.mjs` fails one that does.

- **Tokens:** `apps/web/app/globals.css`, in the three layers of his `token-mapping.md`: the semantic variables on `:root` and `.dark` (and `.light`, so a light sample can sit in a dark page), the `--tf-*` extensions, and the Tailwind theme. `components/ui/tokens.test.ts` compares every value with `working-brand.yml`.
- **Fonts:** `apps/web/app/fonts.ts` loads Inter Tight (display and big numbers, 600 at −2%), Inter (UI) and IBM Plex Mono (provenance and code) with `next/font/local` from files committed under `apps/web/assets/fonts` (the Latin files Google Fonts serves, byte for byte, with each face's OFL beside them; `app/fonts.test.ts` pins their hashes), so a build fetches nothing: until Oct 6 `next/font/google` fetched the faces at build, and CI failed whenever it could not reach Google. There is no serif anywhere in the product (gate `IDENTITY-2`, Oct 8: Newsreader and IBM Plex Sans are gone). They are served from our origin, each with a fallback whose metrics match, written in `globals.css` with the figures next/font works out (capsize's widths against Arial's). The product's layout preloads Inter Tight and Inter; the mono face is in `app/fonts-mono.ts`, not preloaded, and it is the only one his pages load. There is no condensed face: `font-condensed` is Inter Tight. The Latin files hold no Greek, so the "τ" and "Σ" Bearing writes are drawn by the system's face.
- **The pages that exist look as they did.** The base styles of the spec (ground, ink, face, tabular figures, focus ring) sit under one class, `tf-app`, not on `html` and `body`. WEB-1 put `tf-app` on the product's `<body>` and deleted the two lines that kept Tailwind's base fonts; his layout carries `tf-system-faces` instead, which names the same system faces, so his pages read as they did. One thing did change in BRAND-1: text with the `font-mono` class is in IBM Plex Mono, because the class reads the token.
- **Primitives** are in `apps/web/components/ui/`, each with a test beside it:

| His spec | File | What it gives |
|---|---|---|
| `button.md` | `Button.tsx`, `button-class.ts` | `Button`: primary, secondary, link, chip, icon, destructive; dense; busy; disabled. Busy or disabled, it cancels the click, so it cannot submit a form or be followed as a link |
| `field.md` | `Field.tsx` | `Field`, `Input`, `Select`, `Textarea` |
| `card.md` | `Card.tsx` | `Card`, `CardHeader`, `CardBody`, `CardFooter`, `Stat`, `StatRow`, `CardLoading`, `CardEmpty`. `Card mock` draws the hatch band and the plate itself |
| `provenance-pin.md` | `ProvenancePin.tsx`, `provenance.ts`, `price-source.ts` | `ProvenancePin` (the figure, the glyph, the popover); `pinState` and the `PinSource` type; `pinSourceOfPrice`, which turns a `Price` into what the pin takes |
| `mock-plate.md` | `MockPlate.tsx`, `internal/mock-parts.tsx` | `MockPlate` (always the band and the plate), `MockFrame`, `StalePlate`. The band alone and the plate alone are in `internal/`, for the primitives only |
| `disclaimer-block.md` | `Disclaimer.tsx` | `Disclaimer`, which takes a language and no text |
| `data-table.md` | `DataTable.tsx`, `ExecutionList.tsx`, `ExplorerLink.tsx`, `CopyButton.tsx` | the table, the execution list, the `Tx` link |
| `goal-card.md` | `GoalCard.tsx` | `GoalCard`, as a card and as the plan view's header |
| `plan-leg.md` | `PlanLegs.tsx` | `PlanLegs`; a fifth leg throws |
| `exit-plan-line.md` | `ExitPlanLine.tsx` | `ExitPlanLine` (tiers), `ExitPlanPanel` ("Access to cash") |
| `constraint-sheet.md` | `ConstraintSheet.tsx` | `ConstraintSheet`, to edit and to read; `facts` for what it states and does not edit (WEB-1) |
| `composer.md` | `Composer.tsx`, `composer-keys.ts` | `Composer`, on several lines or one |
| `compact-nav.md` | `CompactNav.tsx` | `CompactNav` |
| `embed-shell.md` | `EmbedShell.tsx`, `tf-embed` in `globals.css` | `EmbedShell` |
| `subscribe-block.md` | `SubscribeBlock.tsx` | `SubscribeBlock` |
| `STYLE.md`, `iconography.md` | `StatusMark.tsx`, `Icon.tsx`, `Lattice.tsx` | the status mark with its word, the icon wrapper, the still lattice for a wait or an empty state |

- **Not built:** the `code` placement of the MOCK plate (the API serves its own docs), the assembling loader (the primitives use the still lattice their specs ask for), and the restyle of the wallet adapter: the product's routes do not mount it at all (WEB-1), and it leaves with his pages (WEB-2). `goal-showcase-case.md` and `joint-stage.md` are built as the landing page's own parts (WEB-2b, `features/landing/`), not as primitives. The heatmap tile was built with Bearing's analytics (WEB-BEARING): a cell with no sample is drawn as its spec says, the ground and an en dash.
- **What a figure is handed.** A yield, a price or an FX figure is shown through `ProvenancePin`, which takes `source`, `fetchedAt`, `method` and `provenance` (the `Sourced` shape) and `staleAgeSec`. Without a source and a method that are more than spaces, and a time that is a date-time with a zone, it shows a dash and "no source yet", never the figure. Only the word `live` is live: every other provenance, one this build does not know included, is drawn as MOCK. Stale is what the API states; the pin never works it out from the time. For a price a screen calls `pinSourceOfPrice(price)`: `staleAgeSec` is the price's own `ageSeconds` when `isStalePrice` says it is older than `maxAgeSeconds` (3.1), and null otherwise. A yield, a quote or a preview has no age yet, so it is never drawn stale until `Sourced` gets its staleness field. An age that is not a number of seconds (`NaN`, below zero) is shown as "stale · age unknown".
- **Words.** The few words a primitive says itself (the pin's name, "stale", "failed (not retried)") are English defaults, and every primitive takes them back through a `labels` prop, for the dictionary. The defaults of the client components are in `labels.ts`, a plain module: a file marked `'use client'` exports components and types only, so a server component never reads a value from one.
- **What throws.** A fifth plan leg, a stock token in an income plan, and a status with no word (`Status`, the goal card, a table row): each is an error upstream, and the screen fails instead of showing it.

**Where his files disagreed or left a choice, and the reading taken.** Any of these is his to reopen.

1. The pin's glyph is drawn from `provenance-pin.md` and the `.yml` (gate `LOGO-2`): an 18 by 12 box, 0.75em tall, an outline with 2.5 corners in line-l2 / muted-2, a square pin 4 by 4 (corner 1) at x 11, y 4 in honey-l on day and honey on night, a 3px hatch for sample, the same square hollow for stale. `typography.md` (`1cap`) says otherwise on the height.
2. The words beside a hollow pin ("stale · 3 h") are caption text in the sans face, as `provenance-pin.md` says; `typography.md` and `guidelines.html` set stale labels in mono.
3. A figure from a test network (`sandbox`) carries the hatched glyph like any other figure that is not live. Since gate `MOCK-QUIET` (Thom, Oct 6) no figure carries the boxed word MOCK: a sample card keeps its hatched edge and says so once, in a muted line at its foot ("Sample figures · test network"); a sample figure keeps its hatched pin or a small hatched glyph, named "sample figure" for a screen reader; live figures keep their pin. The words "test network" are in that line, in the pin's popover, and after the glyph in a row of the execution list. A chain's name takes the glyph, then "test network" in words (`features/account/ChainName.tsx`).
4. The popover is a tooltip, or a dialog when it holds a link, as the spec says. Its source line is a button in both, because the spec asks that a click on it copies the line.
5. The popover is placed against the window, not inside its parent, so a table that scrolls cannot cut it off.
6. The disclaimer's box is a hairline (`--border`), as `disclaimer-block.md` says; `guidelines.html` draws it in member.
7. In the embed, mono text takes `--embed-mono` and falls back to the system's monospace (`token-mapping.md` section 8, `embed-shell.md`); `typography.md` names `--tf-mono` and falls back to Plex Mono. The shell takes `--embed-radius` or inherits; boxes inside it are square when the partner sets none, and the partner's buttons take `--embed-button-radius` (a pill in his sample) or the boxes' radius. A status keeps its word and shape and takes the partner's text colour.
8. There is no condensed face since `IDENTITY-2`: Bearing's dense labels are Inter Tight, its cells Inter at 13px.
9. The MOCK badge has 6px between band and plate, as in `guidelines.html`. A stale plate is the same box in sentence case.
10. The plan-lock is the recipe of STYLE.md: the legs seat one after another, 60ms apart, then the pins drop. A leg's "why" line is always shown unless the screen drives "Why this plan?", when it shows only while the bar is parted.
11. A busy button keeps its fill and changes its label. A month on the sheet is a plain `YYYY-MM` field, the same in every browser.
12. The plain names of the shadow, ring, blur, italic and light-weight utilities are never generated by Tailwind (a blocklist in `globals.css`), so a word in a comment cannot make one. The same utilities behind a variant or with a value in brackets are still generated when a file asks for them; `forbidden.test.ts` is what fails then. Bare `rounded` is still 4px, because the pages not yet rebuilt use it; when they are, `--radius` goes into the theme.

**Where his specs change what we build.**

- **The goal comes first.** The home screen is the composer, not the shelf. A shared portfolio is reached from the plan or the shelf and always leads back to the goal screen.
- **At most four legs in the bar.** His plan-leg spec treats more as an engine error. The bar shows the plan's sleeves (stocks, dollar yield, gold, cash). The tokens inside each sleeve go in a data table under it.
- **Status on the goal card.** "On track", "Watch" or "Off track" comes from the server, never the UI. The engine computes a verdict for income goals only, so for every goal the status is the rule `ON-TRACK-V1` (`docs/GATES.md`, Oct 6), a first version: worked out from the newest snapshot of the plan's vault and printed with the line that gave it, never a probability. `GET /v1/portfolio/plans` answers it for each vault (PORT-2), and the engine's verdict takes its place through the same function, as its `verdict` argument, when ENG-3 lands. The odds his showcase prints stay open (section 17).
- **The exit plan is tiers in time,** such as "up to $4,000 within a day, the rest within 7 days, cost ≤ 0.50%", shown before the invest button, with costs as "≤" or "about" and never as a promise. The roll-up in `packages/basket` gives a cost at a size; it also has to give tiers. Withdrawing the tokens themselves is stated on its own line and is not called access to cash.
- **Test networks.** A figure whose provenance is `sandbox` (a test network, or a local copy of mainnet) is shown with the mock-plate treatment, the hatch, and the words "test network" where a mock says MOCK. It is never shown as live.
- **Pins need staleness.** The pin has a stale state that the API must state; the UI may not infer it. A price states it already: it carries its own age and the chain's limit (`isStalePrice`, 3.1). `Sourced` gets a staleness field at the first interface freeze, agreed with Rodrigo (his own open item).
- **Signing.** A button that signs names the action and the amount ("Sign: swap 5 USDC → USDY"), and there is one primary per view. A failed mainnet line has no retry button and reads "(not retried)"; a new attempt is a new action the person signs. A busy button changes its label; there are no spinners.
- **The agent's log speaks in the first person with its reason and its source:** "I moved $1,200 from USDC to USDY because rates dipped and your June date needs a little more income. Source · Tx ↗". Every keeper leg stores the reason it was made, so the line can be written.
- **Wallets.** His specs style the Solana wallet-adapter button and modal, and his landing page says "Connect wallet". Passkey sign-in through Privy needs the same care. The button says "Sign in" and opens a choice of passkey or wallet (section 17). As built (WEB-1) it is a link in the bar to `/sign-in`, a screen of ours on the primitives; nothing of Privy's is drawn.
- **Languages.** Strings live in a dictionary keyed by language from day one, with human labels for every sheet field. As built (WEB-1): `apps/web/i18n/`, above; the primitives take their words through `labels`. The app is English only since Oct 8 (gate `ENGLISH-ONLY`, Rodrigo): no language switch, `readPreferences` answers `en`, and the Portuguese dictionary stays dormant until it is removed with its tests.

- **Order executor.** The web uses `execute()` from `packages/sdk` with a `WalletPort`-backed signer; there is one state machine, not two. It writes every transition to the API before the next step. One run of an order at a time: the caller holds a lock named for the order around `execute` (in a browser `navigator.locks`, across its tabs), since two runs could each sign a step before either writes down what it signed. On reload, `sent` legs are tracked; a `built` leg whose signature was never reported is reported again, and any other `built` or `expired` leg is rebuilt and signed again only as the as-built text below allows. A failed leg stops its chain only. Status changes go to an `aria-live` region with the explorer link.
  - As built (AGT-1): `execute(order, { api, signer, deployments, plan, consents, signed, chainRead, approvedAgain })`. `order` is the order as the review screen showed it; what each step may sign is worked out from it once, and the API's later answers are read only for what has happened. For each step it builds, runs the guard, signs, reports and waits for the chain, one step at a time.
